# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed

- The account lifecycle is unified behind one canonical status precedence — `archived` → `disabled` → `not-configured` → the §7.3 working statuses (task FSM-2, spec §7.3/§8.2/§13/§14.6; decision D-191 — the D-070 input-set successor implementing D-180's archived-dominance rule): the audit's cross-store finding (TZ-fsm §4) showed the account state smeared across config JSON, SQLite, and process memory, displayed through three projections that could contradict each other — reproducibly, a hand-re-added archived id (archived row + config entry, reachable because `syncAccountsFromConfig` skips archived rows per D-061) answered `collecting`/`fresh` on `/api/status` while the accounts view and the UI attention said `archived`. `deriveAccountStatus` now takes the storage row's `archived` fact as a **required rank-0 input** (D-180's named design choice), so the lifecycle token outranks the entire precedence list including `Disabled` and `Not configured`; every surface consumes the one classifier — `/api/status` and `/api/quota` resolve the fact from the merged row half, `quotameter status` reads it beside each configured account (agreeing with the web UI instead of deriving a live status from the entry), and the UI attention module consumes the verdict over the wire (the archived/disabled branches read the status row's token first, with the raw storage/enable facts kept as the row-absent fallback and the cross-load-race tie-breaker in both directions). **Wire change (backwards-compatible token addition):** the `/api/status` row and `/api/quota` status fields gain the `archived` token (`ACCOUNT_STATUSES`/`ACCOUNT_STATUS_TOKENS` grow to nine; `docs/openapi.json` regenerated; the UI badge renders "Archived" in the neutral tone through the new `overview.status.archived` catalog key). Archiving stays exactly as terminal as D-061 made it — the config-sync skip is unchanged and now pinned end-to-end (a composition test walks archive-via-API → hand-re-add in the file → restart and proves the row is not resurrected, the label not mirrored, and both §13 surfaces answer `archived`); an archived account's reads stay unrefused (§8.2) and now honestly labeled. Tests: the §4.1 contradiction ships red-then-green (the route-level repro answered `fresh` pre-fix, `archived` post-fix — the red run captured in the commit body), the `enabled × archived × configured` matrix walks all eight cells through every projection (the shared derivation, the accounts view, the attention, the `/api/status` route, and the CLI's `Status: Archived` row), the derivation suites gain the rank-0 evidence/dominance/validation cells and the archived-dominance property, and the badge component matrix grows the archived row. The unarchive-policy residual is recorded in D-191 (out of scope, per TZ-fsm R5). Review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, REQUEST CHANGES with 2 P2 / 5 P3 — all folded in): the archived attention notice and the blocked no-snapshot line were reworded to lifecycle-safe facts (their "polling is stopped"/"collection is not running" claims are false in the hand-re-add shape, where the engine keeps polling the configured entry — the class of copy-level projection contradiction this task exists to close), `ui/README.md`'s attention-precedence and badge-count references were updated for the verdict-first ordering, the doc/comment drift sweep landed, `/api/quota` gained the hand-re-add assertion, and the composition test now pins archive-via-API → hand-re-add in the file → restart (row not resurrected, label not mirrored, both surfaces answer `archived`; the dominance-over-live-data half lives in the route matrix's archived cells seeded with successful cycles — the composition suite's provider double fails `fetchSnapshot` closed by design); one e2e suggestion was declined (TZ-fsm §4.4 does not require e2e; the component badge matrix and the integration route matrix carry the proof).

- The polling dispatcher's per-account scheduling state is now an explicit phase model (task FSM-1, spec §7.1/§7.2; decision D-190 — the D-066 successor): the audit found "may this account be polled now?" encoded in six independent variables (the entry's monotonic due, its `Retry-After` gate, its owed-manual flag, live-flight membership, the sticky hang flag, and the D-064 state in the shared store) behind a critical, type-unprotected check order in the dispatcher — the exact shape that produced U-028 (a probe bypassing the gate) and P2-3 (an owed attempt overwritten by a settle). The new pure module `src/services/polling-phase.ts` introduces two layers: a **held `PollPlan`** — the one value an account entry stores (`scheduled{due, gate}` or `manual-owed` carrying the dormant schedule beside the owed marker) — moved only by `nextPlan`, a pure transition table over an explicit seven-event union (key-released, cycle-settled, contained-failure, manual-test-requested, manual-test-rejected, interval-changed, dispatched; every row a field-for-field mirror of the D-066 mutation it replaces, nothing invented, and the clock readings and jitter rolls drawn only where the old code drew them — the re-anchor arguments are supplied to the table only when a consuming row will read them, restoring D-066's read counts on every path); and an **observed `PollPhase`** — a transient per-pass projection (absent / in-flight / hung / manual-owed / manual-gated / probe-waiting / probe-due / probe-gated / waiting / due) the dispatcher switches over **exhaustively with compile-time totality**, so adding a phase without handling it is a compile error, never a missed gate. The D-064 machine is not duplicated: the suspension state enters the derivation as the shared store's own live value (referenced, never copied into held state — the FSM-3/D-189 single-holder guarantee extended), the probe is admitted only through `AccountSuspensions.admitVerification`, and the U-028 order (cadence predicate → gate → admission) is the derivation's fixed precedence. Kind transitions log at `debug` through a new optional `PollingSchedulerOptions.logger` (the composition passes its shared redacting logger). No public contract, wire, format, or event changes; `PollingScheduler`'s surface is untouched. Tests: the golden parity suite `test/unit/polling-phase.golden.spec.ts` (six scripted-clock scenarios over the REAL engine — first-poll rounds, the 429-gated manual attempt, the probe-cadence/gate interplay at the 7 230 000 boundary, the sticky hang with resumption, the mid-flight key change's stale discard, the owed attempt surviving a settle and an interval change — pinning full decision traces as literals captured from the pre-refactor engine and never edited after: the before/after identity proof), the property suite `polling-phase.property.spec.ts` (no per-account overlap under generated command scripts against the production engine; a permanently hung account never starving the healthy accounts' cadence; owed survival with consumption exactly at dispatch; a shut `Retry-After` gate never deriving a dispatchable phase for any plan/suspension pair, equality admitting; a flying or hung account never dispatchable), and the table spec `polling-phase.spec.ts` (every transition row and derivation boundary, including the no-clock-data optionality of the suspended/owed rows). Every pre-existing polling suite — `polling-scheduler.spec.ts`, `polling-suspension*.spec.ts` including the FSM-3 lockstep suite, `server-polling.spec.ts`, the integration `composition-polling.spec.ts` — passes without a single edit. Review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, APPROVE with 1 P2 / 6 P3 — folded in): the reviewer's independent old-vs-new differential harness confirmed byte-identical decision traces across the golden scenarios plus 200 generated command scripts; the P2 (an extra monotonic clock read per suspended/owed settle in the first draft) is fixed by the lazy re-anchor pair above, and the P3s folded in (the per-pass flight-presence map, docblock formatting, a constant rename, the no-starvation property) or declined with reasons recorded in D-190 (skipping the pure store lookup for flying/owed entries).

- The D-064 auth-rejection suspension machine is now held by exactly ONE store per process (task FSM-3, spec §7.2/§7.3; decision D-189 — the D-064/D-176 successor): the audit found the pure `polling-suspension` machine instantiated twice (the scheduler's private `AccountEntry.suspension` and the composition's `AccountSuspensions` holder), kept in lockstep only by convention — the `settleWithHolderFeed` wrapper, the `wiringEpochs` staleness rule, and duplicated `auth_error` outcome constants — so any new event source wired into one holder but not the other silently diverged `/api/status` from dispatch gating. The composition's holder is now the ONLY store: `PollingSchedulerOptions.suspensions` (required) is the engine's entire suspension state (every gate reads `suspensionOf`; the settle outcome application, the `setAccounts` key-identity reports and leave-the-polled-set drops, the manual-request release, and the admission re-arm all write through it), the status routes and the manual-test observation share the same instance, and the scheduler carries no suspension field of its own. The wrapper `settleWithHolderFeed`/`wiringEpochs` machinery is deleted (the engine's own mid-flight entry-identity staleness check — adopted as the single rule; the deleted epoch rule agreed everywhere except the re-key-away-and-back corner, where the old engine applied the outcome too, so dispatch semantics are preserved — gates the one application), the duplicated constants collapse into one `classifyAuthRejection()` factory in `polling-outcomes.ts`, the manual-test observation wrapper becomes the single application site for both §7.2 manual events (a refusal re-enters under the TESTED key's identity — resolving the audit's mid-rekey divergence in the truthful direction — while the engine bridge drops the owed attempt only), and the reload follow-ups become config-sync → engine feed → key-identity report (engine first so its own observation fires the release re-anchor; the loop after it is D-064's idempotent no-op, kept for accounts outside the polled set). The unified corner is recorded: a manual-test episode planted while an account is unpolled now survives enablement (the pause holds; the probe cadence verifies the refused key) where the two machines previously disagreed. Internal refactor only — no public contract, wire, format, or event changes. Tests: the new R5 lockstep suite `test/unit/polling-suspension-lockstep.spec.ts` (scripted sequences through the REAL engine composition against a D-064 reference oracle — `suspensionOf` equals the reference at every event and every decisive clock reading, probes dispatch exactly at the admitted instants, the held-probe manual-rejection order, re-key/same-key-remove-re-add staleness, the leave/rejoin drop, and the joining-inherits corner), the single-holder structural pin in `server-polling.spec.ts`, the reload follow-up order extracted as the exported `createReloadFollowUps` factory and sequenced-pinned by `test/unit/composition-reload-follow-ups.spec.ts` (config-sync → engine feed → key-identity report loop on one recorded timeline), the admission/boolean-report pins in `account-suspensions.spec.ts`, the composed manual-test shape in `polling-scheduler.spec.ts`, and the unchanged integration suites (the end-to-end 401/manual-test recovery over the real adapter passes as-is — the wire-identity evidence).

- The §17.3 coverage gate now reads on the whole product: the UI tree joins the enforced universe and the §15.2 auth/session/hashing modules join the per-file 100% set (task U-060, spec §17.3/§17.1; decision D-188). The deepseek review finding — `coverage.include` was `src/**/*.ts`, so no `ui/src` file participated in the "at least 90% line and branch overall" gate, and D-085's per-file list omitted the four auth modules its consequences said must carry keys — is closed by measurement, not by new tests: `src/services/password-hash.ts`, `sessions.ts`, `session-cookie.ts`, and `login-rate-limit.ts` already measure 100% on every axis (the landed suites exercise every arm), so their keys are added at the measured maximum per U-016's lift-first-then-key rule with no D-089 exception needed. The UI decision is inclusion over exclusion: `coverage.include` becomes `src/**/*.ts` + `ui/src/**/*.ts` + `ui/src/**/*.vue` (v8 remaps the SFCs through the vue plugin's sourcemaps — the per-file numbers are real), and a new `ui/src/**` threshold key holds the UI tree's own aggregate at §17.3's floor (90% on all four axes; a key without `perFile` is judged as one group over its matched files — semantics verified in the installed runner's `resolveThresholds`), so server coverage cannot mask UI decay. The measured baseline at adoption: UI-only statements 95.15 / branches 90.49 / functions 96.84 / lines 96.35, combined 97.98 / 95.39 / 98.02 / 98.44, with zero measurable runner cost (v8 collection is already always-on in `test:ci`; the widened include is report-time only). The documented-exclusion alternative was rejected because the numbers are meaningful and already above the bar — an exclusion would record a choice not to look. The coverage-threshold guard (`test/unit/coverage-thresholds.spec.ts`, D-093) covers every new key: the include pin, the file-universe walk (now `ui/src` `.ts`/`.vue` too), the map entries for the four modules and the UI group pattern, and a refreshed runner-provenance note (vitest 5.1.0).
- The first public release is `v0.1.0`, not `1.0.0` (decision D-186): `docs/release.md`'s versioning policy now makes the first published release a full-cycle rehearsal of the release process — version commit, tag, release workflow, trusted publishing, npm publication, clean-container install — with no §19 completeness requirement, while `1.0.0` becomes the distinct milestone release closing every §19 MVP acceptance criterion. Pre-1.0 semver is defined: while at `0.x`, breaking changes and new functionality bump MINOR (`0.Y+1.0`) and PATCH covers backwards-compatible fixes; the MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH rules apply unchanged from `1.0.0`. The specification is deliberately not amended — §4.4 stays version-agnostic and §1.1/§1.2's "Version 1.0" is a product-scope label for the 1.0.0 milestone, never a publication number (the census found the first-release claim only in `docs/release.md`); task 200's done-condition now states the 0.1.0 gates, the bug-report template's version placeholder reads `quotameter 0.1.0`, and README's scope sentence can no longer be read as the first publication number.
- The API timestamp representation is reconciled with §10 by amendment, not by rewiring (task U-056, spec §10/§13; decision D-183 — successor to D-139): the verified review finding showed every response contract carrying epoch-millisecond integers while §10's original sentence said "API timestamps use ISO 8601 UTC ending in `Z`". The consumer census recorded in D-183 weighed both paths honestly: switching responses to ISO strings would touch ~30 schema fields, every route emission site, and force a designed ISO→number conversion boundary into the chart stack — ECharts' value axis, the annotation comparisons, and every `Intl.DateTimeFormat`/relative-time call are numeric-native (D-148's pinned design) — while §13's first sentence declares the API "internal to the web interface" (no external consumer exists; the package is unreleased) and five landed decisions (D-128 accounts, D-130 status/quota, D-139 history/events, D-142 peak-hours, D-182 `expiresAt`) have staked epoch-ms on every surface since the first route. Spec 1.2 therefore amends §10 (the D-180 precedent): API responses carry timestamps as epoch-millisecond integers; the ISO 8601 UTC form with an explicit offset or `Z` applies to the §13 range request parameters. The artifacts that claimed ISO responses are corrected in the same commit — `src/util/clock.ts`'s `toUtcIso` docblock (its production consumer is `doctor --raw`'s stdout rendering, not the API), `src/util/README.md`'s two stale claims (including the rolling-ranges "Consumers" bullet that named the §13 endpoints as `toUtcIso` serializers — they only ever parse ISO boundaries through `epochMsFromIso`; the bounds are computed client-side by the UI twin, D-147), `src/util/rolling-ranges.ts`'s docblock, and `src/cli/raw-diagnostics.ts`'s rendering note (a CLI display choice, no longer justified through §10's ISO clause) — beside D-183-citing updates in `src/server/README.md` and `docs/api.md`; the OpenAPI prose and `docs/openapi.json` already state epoch-ms truthfully and needed no change. The new `test/unit/spec-timestamp-rule.spec.ts` pins the amended §10 sentence, the 1.2 version/amendment header, the D-183→D-139 decision trail, `docs/api.md`'s response rule, and the absence of the retired sentence from every normative artifact that carried it — the drift class this task found (a spec sentence or docblock quietly contradicting the wire) cannot silently return. Review round (cursor-agent/grok-4.7-high, one round, APPROVE with 1 P2 / 4 P3 — the deepseek primary timed out with no findings body; all five findings verified and folded in): the drift pin widened to the corrected docblocks, the CLI renderer's §10 citation reworded to the display-choice grounds, D-183's supersession list extended to D-021 interpretation (4)'s §10 citation and D-068's reasons clause (both entries immutable, their emission contracts unchanged), `toUtcIso`'s docblock clarified that the request-parameter ISO path never runs through it, and the util README's consumers bullet attributed the retention comparison to the UI twin's `historyRangeDurationMs` (drift-pinned equal to `rollingRangeDurationMs`, which has no production caller today).

### Fixed

- `/api/history` and `/api/events` no longer fail with HTTP 500 on clock-skewed collection rows (task U-057, spec §12.2/§13/§16; decision D-185): the schema deliberately permits them (D-034 rejected an `ended_at >= started_at` CHECK — both stamps are wall-clock reads around one HTTP cycle, and a rejected INSERT would lose the run record), but the §12.2 read stack assumed the ordering — `buildTimelineIndicators` threw on an inverted run and `detectCollectionGaps` threw on a valid point preceding the interval timeline's coverage, so one NTP step-back between a cycle's two clock reads could 500 the affected range. The new pure gate `src/services/history-skew.ts` (applied at the API composition, the pure services' fail-fast contracts untouched) carries three explicit rules: an inverted run's contradictory span is skipped while its record is kept — projected to a zero-duration cycle at its trusted `startedAt`, so it still bounds §12.2's record-based stopped silence (dropping it could merge two legal neighbor silences into a fabricated outage) and a failed cycle still marks at its start stamp; a point preceding the timeline's coverage is left unjudgeable by the gap detector (seeding the timeline instead would fabricate cadence coverage and can mint false gaps — the fail-safe direction mints none; the point itself still renders as series data); and a skewed interval change is anchored at `min(startedAt, endedAt)` in `intervalTimelineFromRuns`, so a late-biased start stamp cannot tighten a straddling pair's judgment into a false gap after a cadence grow. The tolerances surface as one aggregated sanitized §16 `warn` per rule per account (kind, account, count, epoch-ms bounds — never a row payload); the response contracts are unchanged. Tests: the deterministic and property suites over the gate, the change-anchor cells in the view suite, plus integration blocks in both API suites seeding the mixed clean/skewed world through the real facade write path (`ended_at < started_at` runs and a snapshot stamped before its own run's start) and asserting 200 responses with the real gap, marker and stopped-region arithmetic, the two-legal-legs and skewed-change regressions, and the diagnostics on the log sink. Review round (cursor-agent/grok-4.7-high, one round, REQUEST_CHANGES 1×P1/2×P2/1×P3 — the deepseek primary timed out with no findings body; all four findings verified and folded in): the P1 fabricated-stopped-region fix replaced the first draft's skip with the zero-duration projection, the P2 false-gap fix added the change anchor, the P2 test-gap finding added the two-legal-legs regression, and the P3 log-spam finding added the per-request diagnostic aggregation.
- The settings password change now revokes sessions BEFORE the configuration write — the fail-secure order `quotameter passwd` already had (task U-058, spec §15.2/§6.4; decision D-184): the 2026-11-06 full-project review found the two password-change paths disagreeing on security semantics. `createConfigFileSettingsStore.apply` wrote the new hash through `owner.mutate` (persist + reload) and only then called `repository.deleteAllSessions()` — so a throwing revocation returned INTERNAL with the NEW hash already effective and every old session still valid, the exact state §15.2's "changing the password revokes all sessions" forbids, invisible behind a generic error. The store now revokes first and writes second (passwd's recorded D-118 reasoning): a failed revocation refuses the whole change with nothing written — the incumbent hash stays effective with its sessions coherent — and a mutation failing after a successful revocation is rethrown with the revocation stated arm-truly (sessions gone; the running service keeps the current password; the file's state named as uncertain, because a rejected reload leaves it holding the new hash ahead of memory; a zero-count revocation appends nothing). The revocation deliberately runs outside the file owner's mutation queue: it is a synchronous storage call needing no atomicity with the write (every interleaving is fail-secure) and the mutation callback stays a pure file edit; the login-in-the-gap residual every path shares — including its compromised-password reading — is recorded in D-184. The `SettingsStore.apply` seam contract extends to both rejection meanings (the revocation failed, or sessions were revoked and the change then failed); the endpoint still answers the INTERNAL envelope, detailed text to logs only. The shared ordering rationale of every password-writing path (`passwd`, the settings PUT, init's fresh-password leg) now lives once in `docs/security/password-change-revocation.md`, with the code docblocks, `src/server/README.md` (whose "after a password write … the fail-safe direction" claim was the inverted rationale), `src/cli/README.md`, and the stale task-144-only mentions in `src/services/sessions.ts`/`src/services/README.md` pointing at it. Tests: `test/integration/config-stores.spec.ts` gains the order proof (the harness's repository double snapshots the raw file bytes at each revocation — the happy path's snapshot carries no hash yet), the mandated injected-rejection cell (a failing `deleteAllSessions` leaves the file byte-identical with the old `scrypt$` hash and the reload never running), the placement pin (the revocation precedes even the mutation's file read — a malformed file plus the failing revocation answers the revocation error), the arm-true report cell (a rejected reload after a 2-session revocation carries both facts, asserts the file-ahead state, and preserves the cause chain), and the no-annotation cell (a zero-count revocation rethrows bare). Review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, APPROVE with 1 P2 / 6 P3): the P2 folded in (the first draft's "the next sign-in still needs the current password" annotation is false in the reload-rejected arm, where the file already holds the new hash — the message now states the shared truth and the uncertainty), and all six P3s folded in (the compromised-password half of the login-in-the-gap rationale, the zero-count rule restated as the settings path's own, the shared doc's path set widened to init and its §6.4 step scoped to the process-owning paths, the `passwd.ts` docblock link, the seam contract's second rejection meaning, and the test pins — placement, file-ahead state, cause chain, and a genuinely one-shot failure hook).

- The production Z.ai adapter is now constructed from the live configuration — the configured request timeout finally reaches the transport, and `collectedAt` follows the shared clock (task U-054, spec §6.2/§6.4/§7.2/§8.3/§10; decision D-179): the composition's registry default had been `createInitialAccountProvider()` — a bare `new ZaiCodingPlanProvider()` — so the schema-validated and documented `providers.zai-coding-plan.requestTimeoutSeconds` (5–60 s, default 15) never reached the transport (the adapter kept its 15 s default regardless of the setting) and the adapter constructed its own system clock, violating the D-021 single-clock discipline at `collectedAt`'s last unserviced seam. The new `createConfiguredZaiProvider({ config, clock, logger })` in `src/server/providers.ts` — the registry default `composeApplication` now registers, still wrapped by `observingManualTests` — derives the §7.2 deadline from the LIVE effective configuration at every request (`providers['zai-coding-plan']?.requestTimeoutSeconds ?? CONFIG_DEFAULTS.provider.requestTimeoutSeconds`, ×1000), delegating to a `ZaiCodingPlanProvider` constructed with `{ timeoutMs, clock, logger }` and memoized by the effective value: an unchanged configuration reuses one adapter instance, a reload that changes the seconds constructs the replacement on the next request — the lazy form of D-026's §6.4-live classification ("governs the next request"), keeping the change restart-free exactly as the settings surface's closed restart trio already promises (no restart-required annotation changes). The reader shape keeps the registry entry's identity stable, so the manual-test observation wrapper stays intact and the polling recorders — which capture `registry.get(...)` once per wiring build — hold the live wrapper and can never pin a stale deadline; no polling-engine, registry, or re-registration change was needed. The shared redacting logger (§16) is injected too, so the adapter's skip reports and endpoints-2/3 soft-failure warnings become visible in production logs instead of silently dropped. The CLI's pre-configuration paths deliberately keep `createInitialAccountProvider()`'s D-052 defaults (dossier base URLs, 15 s): `init`'s connection test (§5.3 step 6) runs before the configuration write — D-102's ordering (the write is step 5's configuration half, deferred to the run's end, so a fresh run has no file yet and a re-run keeps the same answer-driven factory) — and `doctor`'s per-account probes are a diagnostic whose probe budget deliberately stays the spec's own default, independent of the configuration under test; the running service's manual connection tests (`POST /api/accounts/:id/test`) DO use the configured deadline through the registry's observed live wrapper. Tests: the new `test/integration/zai-coding-plan-configured-provider.spec.ts` (the factory over the loopback mock through its base-URL test seams: the configured deadline expiring both the collection cycle — a re-thrown D-042 `TransportError` kind `timeout` — and the credential check — `ProviderUnavailableError` with `transportKind: 'timeout'`; a schema-legal 5 s deadline delivering, the D-087 house value; `collectedAt` from an injected fixed clock; a reloaded deadline applying on the very next request through the SAME wrapper, which an adapter pinned at construction would deliver; both default-fallback arms — absent providers section and a foreign-provider record; the manual-test observation intact over the live wrapper in the composition's exact nesting; the shared logger capturing a soft-failure warning with the §15.4 marker key absent), the new `test/unit/composition-provider-wiring.spec.ts` (the composition default's textual pin — the wiring is behaviorally unobservable in the test environment because every composition suite injects `providers` and the default adapter's production base URLs are unreachable under the no-network guard, so reverting to the D-052 defaults factory, the original finding, would otherwise leave `make check` green), plus the `server-providers.spec.ts` identity/construction pins. `src/server/README.md`, `src/cli/README.md`, the provider dossier, and the adapter island's own docblocks document the two-factory split; `docs/agents/review.md` also receives a one-blank-line formatting repair (the just-landed chain reorder 2f2ce16 left it unformatted, which fails the `make check` format gate for every task cut after it). Review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, APPROVE with 0 P0 / 0 P1 / 2 P2 / 5 P3): both P2s folded in (the §5.3 step-citation correction — step 5 creates the configuration file, step 7 installs the service, the first draft wrongly wrote "the step-7 configuration write" — and the composition-wiring textual pin); four P3s folded in (the dead `metricCatalog` conditional replaced by the direct assignment, the adapter island's two stale docblock spots — "later tasks wire it" and "created … with default options" — the per-test log sink and request-delta assertions in the integration suite, and the fallback cells' documentation of the value pin and the load pipeline's always-materialized `providers`); one P3 declined with the reason already recorded in D-179 (doctor mirroring the configured deadline — the reviewer himself classified it a design idea; the deliberate diagnostic independence stands, the connectivity row being an observation, not a simulation of the service's deadline).

### Added

- The §5.3 step-5/7/8 completion of `init` (task U-059, spec §5.3/§5.4/§15.1/§15.5/§11.4; decision D-187) — the three halves a full-project review found deferred or advertised-but-absent: **step 5's database** — a fresh run now creates the data directory (`0700`, chmod'd explicitly after the recursive mkdir so the shell's umask cannot mask it away) and brings the database to the current schema right after the configuration write, refusing first under a **live** §5.6 single-instance lock holder (init never runs backup or DDL under a running service), through the storage composition's own open/migrate pair consumed via dynamic imports (`node:sqlite` stays out of every dispatch graph; the module's ExperimentalWarning on stderr is the documented property of invocations that actually open a database); the sequence is idempotent (a surviving database migrates exactly as the service would at start), and a failure **aborts the run** (exit 1, nothing installed, the written configuration explicitly kept — §5.3 step 5 is a must, not best-effort: every reachable failure leaves an environment no service can start in, and the §5.3 step-6 connection-test tolerance deliberately does not extend). **Step 7's `/api/health` verification** — `runServiceSetupStep` now reports whether the run actually started a service, and a started service gets the new bounded loopback poll `verifyServiceHealth` (30 s total / 2 s per request / 500 ms between attempts; healthy is HTTP 200 with §13's `{"status":"ok"}` body; the probe host is loopback by construction, `redirect: 'error'` at the fetch site): in `init` only when a service was started and the configuration is not password-pending (the retry sleep is deliberately ref'd — mid-poll it is often the CLI process's only pending work) (§15.1 refuses that start by design — the probe is skipped, the D-101 journey intact), with a failed probe reported honestly (stderr + `status`/`logs` guidance) but never failing the run; in `service install`'s system route the probe completes §5.3's privileged verification after `is-active` — the endpoint read best-effort from the owner's configuration (no readable configuration skips honestly, exit 0, D-108's independence intact; a probe that ran and failed exits 1), making the rendered plan's "verifies it (systemctl is-active and /api/health)" advertisement true; install's user route stays unprobed (§5.3 prescribes verification for the privileged leg — recorded asymmetry). **Step 8's summary** — the fresh run closes with `renderInitNextSteps`: the effective URL (`http://<host>:<port>`, IPv6 literals bracketed, a wildcard bind rendered as the machine-address placeholder — `0.0.0.0` is not a client URL), the SSH-tunnel access **exactly for a loopback bind** (only a loopback URL is unreachable from another machine, and the tunnel is §15.5's recommended remote-access method; a non-loopback bind renders its direct URL only), the `quotameter status`/`quotameter logs` command names, and the configuration and data paths. Tests: the lifecycle order, the database abort, the pending-skip, the probe-host matrix, and the poll machine (healthy/wrong-status/wrong-body/redirect/refused against a real loopback server the suite owns) in the unit suites; a real launcher leaving a genuinely migrated `quotameter.sqlite` (journal read back), the db-failure abort with a squatter file, the reproducibility pin (pid-normalized warning), and the piped + PTY access-block pins in the integration suites, and the module's own suite over the real filesystem and storage seam (the umask-exempt `0700`, the idempotent journal read-back, the live-holder/stale-lock/invalid-lock refusals). No public API, wire, format, or event changes.

- `GET /api/auth/session` — §13's third auth row, "current session and auth-mode information" (task U-055, spec §13/§15.1/§15.2/§14.6; decision D-182): the endpoint `src/schemas/api/auth.ts` had recorded as deliberately absent (deferral to task 180's consumer) now exists — task 180 landed with the D-159 tri-state workaround and never wired it, so any non-UI client following the published contract got the `/api` catch-all 404 and `docs/openapi.json` could not document the row. The route joins the auth-routes plugin as a **public** reader (`PUBLIC_AUTH_ROUTE_CONFIG`, the D-121 opt-out health and login use — its client is precisely the client that may hold no session yet, D-159's chicken-and-egg) and answers the closed body `{ mode, authenticated, session? }`: `mode` is the per-request §15.1 verdict in D-121's own vocabulary (computed by `classifyAuthMode` over the same mode/bind/live-password/proxy facts the guard reads — the scope injects them from its own `auth` options, one source of §15.1 truth, so guard and reader cannot drift), `authenticated` is the §15.2 verdict over the presented cookie (`authenticateSession` verbatim: missing/empty cookie never touches storage, every invalid outcome — garbage, expired, revoked — collapses into `false` per D-124's indistinguishable-absence rule), and `session.expiresAt` rides exactly the valid arm as the sighting-REFRESHED effective expiry (the reader composes the same primitive every authenticated request does — no minting, no cookie write; D-146's no-re-issue holds). Nothing leaks before authentication that §13 withholds: every mode cell is already wire-visible through the guard's own answers on protected routes (200/401/403-instructions), and `not-required` never reveals whether a password is configured — the response states what a probe would force. The D-159 UI session cache stays probe-free by recorded reconciliation (D-182): no boot round trip in the common `auto`-loopback world, the wire's own answers remain the flip authority, and consuming the endpoint for exact sign-out visibility or deep-link pre-seeding stays a future UI task. Tests: `test/integration/auth-routes.spec.ts` grows the reader's suite (the exact closed bodies per §15.1 mode incl. the fail-closed passwordless cells, the refreshed-expiry arm with the row's slide pinned in storage, the collapsed invalid-cookie arms, and the public-yet-Host-guarded construction proof through `buildHttpServer`), and `test/integration/api-sweep-contract.spec.ts`'s session probe flips from the 404 pin to the composition's honest `{ mode: 'not-required', authenticated: false }`. The D-144 artifacts updated mechanically: the third operation in `scripts/openapi-document.mjs` (public, health's error-code set — `UNAUTHENTICATED` deliberately absent), `docs/openapi.json` regenerated, `docs/api.md`'s public set and reading guide, and the `src/server`/`ui` READMEs reconciled.

- The retention cleanup schedule, composed into the running service, and §11.3's reduce-retention warning surfaced through the settings contract (task U-053, spec §11.3/§5.6/§6.4/§14.5/§16; decision D-178): `runRetentionCleanup` (task 091, D-063) was implemented and tested but had no production caller — the verified review finding: §11.3's "cleanup runs at startup and daily" never happened while U-051's composed polling kept accumulating runs, snapshots, metric values, events, sessions, and raw payloads unbounded, and the `retention:applied-days` marker the cleanup writes (the documented hook for the reduce-retention warning) had no reader. The new `src/server/retention.ts` (the D-176 polling-composition shape) exposes the `retention` lifecycle component, registered directly after storage in the documented §5.6 order — now `[single-instance-lock, storage, retention, graceful-drain, polling, http-listener]`: the startup cleanup runs strictly after the storage component's migrations (registration order), and teardown cancels the daily wait before the database closes, the cleanup being one synchronous call that can never interleave with the close (no in-flight-tracker entry by design). The daily cadence is one fixed-duration 24 h abortable wait re-armed after each completed run — a duration cadence, not zone-aligned midnight (§11.3 names no alignment, and the Node timer is monotonic by construction); wall time enters only as each run's cleanup `now`, read from the shared clock (D-021). §6.4 reload-awareness is read-at-run-time: each run takes the live `app.historyRetentionDays` — per §11.3's own sentence, a reduced window applies "at the next cleanup", so no reload feed exists. Faults are contained per §16 (the polling-settlement precedent): a failed run — startup or daily — is one `warn` and never rejects `start` nor kills the schedule (D-063's repeat-safety makes the next run the retry); a success is one `info` record with the removal counts; storage-not-open skips with a `warn`; a broken wait seam is `error` and ends the schedule for the process. **The warning contract:** `GET /api/settings` gains a top-level `appliedRetentionDays` (integer or null — null before any completed cleanup), read by the production settings store from the marker through the repository with a fail-soft parse (a corrupted marker reads null, never a 500; the next cleanup's write is the repair); the settings UI renders a live, non-blocking warning under the retention field whenever a draft §11.3's range would accept is below the applied window — including an unchanged draft that is already below it (a pending deletion is a pending deletion), self-clearing once the next cleanup rewrites the marker, absent for null and for out-of-range drafts (the range error stands alone), and never blocking the save. The composition gains `retentionSleep` (the `pollingSleep` time-compression precedent). Tests: `test/unit/server-retention.spec.ts` (the schedule matrix: startup input, lazy storage resolution, default-window fallback, daily re-arm under fake timers, read-at-run-time reload, stop cancellation incl. double-stop, both contained-fault arms, the skip, the broken-seam death), `test/integration/composition-retention.spec.ts` (the production composition over a pre-seeded temporary database with a scaled clock anchored at a fixed §11.1-scale base — a direct `Date.now() × scale` clock overflows SQLite's safe-integer epochs — plus the compressed sleep: startup deletions + markers + the GET's applied window, the first scaled day's cleanup, a real settings PUT to a reduced window landing at the NEXT cleanup with the marker flipping 180→30 through the API, and §5.6 stop with an absence wait strictly inside the half-day gate), the `server-main.spec.ts` order pin, the settings double/GET envelope/schema rejection arms, the config-store marker read (valid, absent, corrupt, out-of-range), and the component-test warning lifecycle. `docs/openapi.json` regenerated from the widened response schema; the storage README's "scheduling is not here" pointer now names the schedule module.

- The SIGHUP reload handler in the server runtime (task U-052, spec §6.4/§5.2/§5.6/§16; decision D-177): the documented reload channel is complete end-to-end — `quotameter reload` (task 141) discovers the live §5.6 lock holder and delivers SIGHUP via `process.kill`, and the server runtime now CATCHES it instead of dying to Linux's default disposition (the verified defect: the CLI reported "reload accepted" while the signal killed the service, masked by systemd's `Restart=on-failure`). `runServer` installs the handler beside SIGTERM/SIGINT — after a successful `app.start()`, before the `onSignalsReady` handshake fires, disposed with them in the same `finally` — through the new `installReloadSignal`, and routes it through the composition's reload: `composeApplication` now publishes `applyReload` (the D-176 wrapper every store mutation already flows through — the bare reloader plus the D-128 config-sync, the D-064 key-identity reports, and the polling-engine feeds) and `reloadOnSighup`, the signal face that fires it fire-and-forget and contains every fault at `error` through the shared §16 logger with the two fault worlds told apart (a signal handler must never throw and never leave an unhandled rejection): a fault before the reloader adopts keeps the last valid configuration, while a fault in the follow-up application — after the adoption, the canonical shape being a config-sync racing a shutdown — is recorded as what it is (the new configuration effective, storage/polling possibly lagging), pinned by test with the honest record sequence asserted. The §16 outcome records are the reloader's own `info`/`warn` lines, so a reload outcome is observable exactly where §16 says configuration reload belongs. **Installation semantics (D-177):** the handler exists exactly when a reload operation exists — the production path (the default composition) always; an embedder injecting bare `components` into `runServer` keeps the OS default unless it passes the new `RunServerOptions.reload` seam. The deliberate foreground consequence, replacing task 060's "not handled yet" deferral: a `quotameter run` whose terminal disconnects SURVIVES the TTY's SIGHUP (it triggers a usually no-op, §16-logged reload), because §5.2/§6.4 designate SIGHUP as the reload channel and `quotameter reload` must not kill the service; SIGINT/SIGTERM remain the stop channels, and a signal during startup keeps the OS default (D-024's recorded window). Reloads never touch the shutdown promise or the exit code. The assembly function `buildComponents` is renamed `buildApplication` and returns the whole composed result (the wiring needs the reload beside the components). Tests: `test/integration/cli-run.spec.ts` gains the real-launcher server-side pin — health-ready child, a real SIGHUP after a valid config change: process stays alive and `/api/status` reports the new interval with the `configuration reloaded` info line on stdout; then a real SIGHUP after an invalid file: still alive on the last valid configuration with the `warn` rejection line; then SIGTERM → exit 0, lock released (verified defect-pinning by mutation: with the handler not installed the same test dies at the first SIGHUP with `ECONNREFUSED`; the launcher children also strip ambient `QUOTAMETER_*` overrides so environment overrides cannot silently flip the file-based assertions); `test/integration/composition-sighup.spec.ts` pins the composition surface (`applyReload`'s effective change, the signal face's arrival note and §6.2 rejection behavior, the two-world fault containment with the honest record sequence); `server-main.spec.ts` pins `installReloadSignal`, the in-process SIGHUP routing/disposal/no-reload arm, and the default assembly's registration. Residual windows, recorded in D-177: a SIGHUP delivered during startup (after the lock exists, before the handler installs) or in the post-dispose shutdown tail still terminates — the OS default, with systemd's `Restart=on-failure` recovering. Review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, REQUEST_CHANGES 0 P0 / 0 P1 / 2 P2 / 4 P3): both P2s folded in (the honest two-world fault messages — the first draft's single message misreported a partial application as "kept the old configuration" — and the startup/shutdown-window documentation with the CLI README's "always" corrected to "once the application is running"); all four P3s folded in (five stale rename/reference spots, the rejection-visibility wording at `logging.level: error`, the debug arrival note moved inside the contained phase, the ambient-override strip), with the startup-window code fixes declined for the recorded reasons (they would change D-024's install-after-start semantics or D-112's no-observation-channel design).
- The polling engine composed into the running service (task U-051, spec §7.1/§7.2/§5.6/§6.4; decision D-176 — the successor to D-140's recorded deferral): `composeApplication` now assembles the §5.6 component order `shutdown.ts` documented from the start — `[single-instance-lock, storage, graceful-drain, polling, http-listener]` — over one shared in-flight tracker (the scheduler registers its flights, the drain waits for them, and an aborted cycle's settlement cannot race the database close it sits in front of). The new `src/server/polling.ts` builds one `createPollingScheduler` over the shared clock and the live interval reader plus one `createCycleRecorder` per pollable account (enabled, secret-resolving — trim-blank values resolve nothing — registered provider; skips are logged, never thrown, per §7.2's isolation; the resolved secret reaches only the branded `AccountContext`), so a real deployment finally collects: §7.1's immediate first poll and scheduled subsequent polls persist to `collection_runs`/`snapshots`, `/api/status` can show `Fresh`, the §7.2 retry/`Retry-After`/suspension machinery is production-live, and §6.4 reloads feed the engine through the same composition wrapper every configuration mutation already flows through (an `accounts` change rebuilds the wiring — added accounts poll immediately, removed/disabled ones stop and drop their suspension episode — an interval change re-anchors the schedule). The D-064 suspension is now held by two pure machines in lockstep: the engine's internal one gates dispatch, the composition's holder stays the `/api/status` seam, and the new `polledCycleOutcome` feed hands the holder every settled classification through a per-account continuity epoch that mirrors the engine's mid-flight entry-identity rule exactly (kept rebuilds keep feeding; removal, re-key, and same-key remove/re-add go stale), while the observation wrapper's new `onManualTestRequested`/`onManualTestRejected` bridges route §7.2's manual-test release — and its refusal — into the engine through the scheduler's new additive `manualTestRejected` seam (one owed attempt on the request; re-entry with a fresh 60-minute window and a dropped owed attempt on the refusal). Contained settlement failures surface as one `warn` each (D-066's deferred diagnostics); the drain's events reach the logger (`drain-timeout` as `warn`). The composition gains `pollingSleep` — the `port`/`staticRoot`-precedent test seam for time-compressed integration tests (§7.1's 60 s floor vs. the test gate; the suite injects a ×20-scaled clock plus a matching compressed sleep); the e2e acceptance harness doubles the real adapter's `fetchSnapshot` off (a plain-`Error` rejection — the recorder's fail-fast shape, contained, persisting nothing) so its exact seeded worlds stay still, with the live engine's proof owned by the new integration suite. Tests: `test/integration/composition-polling.spec.ts` — the done-condition through the production construction with the real Z.ai adapter on the loopback mock (first poll, scheduled polls, reload feeds, a live 429 deferral and 401 suspension with manual-test recovery, a mid-cycle §5.6 shutdown whose aborted attempt persists nothing, and the §15.4 marker scans) — plus `test/unit/server-polling.spec.ts` (the wiring filters incl. trim-blank values, delegation, feeds, the holder feed's epoch staleness and drop semantics), the `server-providers.spec.ts` bridge pins, the `account-suspensions.spec.ts` outcome-feed pins, and the `polling-scheduler.spec.ts` cells for the new seam. Review round (kimi/k3.1-pro, REQUEST_CHANGES 3×P1/1×P2; the deepseek primary timed out with no findings): all four findings folded in — the trim-blank startup abort, the same-key disable/re-enable and remove/re-add lockstep breaks, the unbounded manual-rejection divergence, and the acceptance wording.
- Real `quotameter run` command (task U-050, spec §5.2/§5.6; decision D-175): the CLI dispatch's last `notImplemented` stub is gone — `quotameter run` now starts the actual server (`runServer` over the D-140 composition: §6.1 paths, §6.3 permission enforcement, §6.2 refuse-to-start, the §5.6 component order, the wired `/api` surface) and forwards its exit code, exactly what the generated systemd unit's `ExecStart` (`<node> <cli> run`, §5.4) has been pointing at — a fresh `init` on a systemd machine now installs a unit whose service starts instead of exiting 1. The new `src/cli/run.ts` handler imports the server entry point **lazily, inside the handler**, so the dispatch graph every invocation loads first stays light — no Fastify/Pino/`node:sqlite` at table-construction time, which means none of it for `--help` and every invocation whose handler never needs it (pinned executably by a real-launcher `--help` child whose stderr carries no `node:sqlite` warning); commands that DO need server or storage modules (`doctor`, `status`, `passwd`) keep importing them lazily at handler time, exactly as before. The handler writes nothing itself: stdout belongs to the §16 JSON log stream journald collects, startup refusals stay `runServer`'s `quotameter:` stderr explanations, and U-022/D-095's best-effort stop on startup failure is untouched. The retired stub helpers (`notImplemented`, `stub`) are removed from `commands.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/cli-run.spec.ts` (exit-code forwarding, import-failure propagation, the production import seam, and the full default path hermetically in-process — the §6.2 missing-configuration refusal through `runCli(['run'])`), the updated `test/unit/cli-dispatch.spec.ts` (the `run` row's shape and `--help`; a no-stubs-remain pin over the table), and `test/integration/cli-run.spec.ts` — the done-condition: the real launcher over a temporary config with a reserved port, readiness via an answered `GET /api/health` (the D-024 handshake invariant through the public surface — an answered request proves `app.start()` settled and the SIGTERM handlers are installed), then SIGTERM → exit 0, no signal, the §5.6 single-instance lock released, plus the invalid-configuration exit-1 route and the lazy-graph inverse pin; `cli-bin.spec.ts`'s stub test now pins `run --help` at the process boundary.

### Fixed

- Development-container EACCES on host-owned tracked files (one-off dev-environment fix, no plan task; decision D-181): the dev image's `node` user (uid 1000) could not rewrite repository files created by this host's user (uid 1001, mode 664), so container-side `prettier --write` failed — observed live formatting `docs/agents/review.md` (commit `67b75c1`, worked around with a temporary `chmod 666`) — and `make format` / `make lint-fix` were structurally broken on files that actually needed changing, since those tools write exactly the files needing rewrites. The Makefile now exports `DEV_UID`/`DEV_GID` (the host uid/gid; the make assignment wins over the ambient environment — override with `make DEV_UID=… DEV_GID=… image`), `compose.yaml` passes them as build args, and the Dockerfile remaps the image's `node` user to them (`usermod`/`groupmod` — a passwd entry survives, so `HOME` and cache paths are unchanged) before the existing ownership step; a target id another image account or group already holds fails the build with a named error instead of a raw `usermod` crash; defaults `1000:1000` make the remap a guarded no-op, so `1000:1000` hosts and CI (which never builds this image) see no change. Generated output keeps flowing to the data volume per the environment contract — the grounds are artifact cleanliness on every runner, no longer the local uid mismatch — and the stale "container runs as uid 1000" claims in the schema/OpenAPI/DIST_DIR docs and helper docblocks are reworded to the routing contract. The wiring is pinned executably by `test/unit/dev-container-uid.spec.ts` (the release-workflow guard's line-scanner pattern): a dropped `ARG`, a missing guard or `passwd` declaration, or a compose/Makefile edit that stops passing the ids fails `make check`. Migration for pre-D-181 environments (their volumes are uid-1000-owned): disposable worktrees `make clean && make image && make up`; the data-preserving volume-chown recipe (stop the container first) is in `docs/agents/environment.md` ("Migrating a pre-D-181 environment"), with the failure mode — the first container write inside the volumes, not necessarily `make deps`, which can pass untouched with a fresh stamp — and the direct-`docktree build` caveat in `docs/agents/gotchas.md`. Verified live in both directions: the same host-owned probe file fails `prettier --write` with EACCES before the rebuild and rewrites cleanly (ownership unchanged) after; the collision guard verified live (`DEV_GID=100` fails with the named error); `make check` green in the rebuilt environment.

- Stryker initial-test-run failure: the `cli-init-yes` reproducibility cell diverged only in the mutation runner's job environment (task U-045, spec §17.1/§17.3; decision D-173). Every daily mutation run 2026-11-02→04 died with `ConfigError` on the one cell comparing two real-launcher `init --yes` stdouts byte-for-byte, while push CI and `make check` stayed green on the same tips — the hosted runner's live user systemd (D-162's recorded environment shift) coupled the spawned child's §5.3 step-7 service-setup output to live machine state. The diverging bytes were re-captured in privileged systemd containers reproducing the hosted shape on both Debian 252 and Ubuntu 26.04/257 (the failing generation): the stdouts are identical through the entire collected block — including the guard-blocked `provider-unavailable` connection-test row — and diverge at the first service-setup line (run 1 "installed the user service", `enable --now` exit 0; run 2 the "could not be enabled automatically" fallback, `Job for quotameter.service failed because the control process exited with error code.`), reproducing under concurrent invocations — the dry run's parallel load — while sequential pairs stay green; the same machine-coupling's second face, the probe's 10-second timeout race, is deduced from those very jobs (`cli-init-service.spec.ts` passed beside the failing cell, and on a systemd host its asserted absent-route text is reachable only through the probe timing out). Stryker itself was exonerated by the 2026-11-05 run (42002804236), whose initial test run succeeded over all 3877 tests inside D-162's job container. The fix is at the spawn boundary — the real launcher's only test-reachable knob: the new `test/helpers/hermetic-child-env.ts` strips `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` and the `DBUS_*` session addresses from every child environment in `cli-init-yes.spec.ts`, so the probe never spawns the user query and the step takes the deterministic write-only `user-manager-unavailable` route, D-100's byte-identity assertion unchanged in strength (a new pin cell holds the helper's full contract: strip set, verbatim pass-through, fresh object, `process.env` default). Verified on the live-systemd box that reproduced the failure: 8 concurrent full-suite runs, 160/160 cells green. The reproducibility cell now dumps BOTH normalized stdouts AND both stderrs plus the divergence offset on mismatch and compares stderr for identity too, so a future failure is self-evidencing under any runner; `docs/agents/gotchas.md` documents the pitfall and both residuals (the passwd-home unit-file write on live-systemd machines and the system-probe timeout race beyond the env boundary — the D-162 container is the daily gate's guarantee). Scoped Stryker verification: a run with the initial test run scoped to this suite completes its dry run green; the next scheduled daily run is the final confirmation.

- Single-instance live-capture cell pinned against guard-deadline preemption (task U-048, spec §17.1/§5.6; decision D-174): `test/integration/single-instance.spec.ts`'s "never removes a live guard captured mid-break and degrades to contention" flaked once in a landing `make check-all` (2026-11-06) — the guard path held the breaker's stale payload instead of the live replacement (`expected '{"pid":4100001}\n' to be '{"pid":4100002}\n'`), green in isolation ×3 and in a full re-run. The cell constructs D-086's documented one-level-down race through the `isAlive` probe counter, but `acquireGuard` arms its deadline at entry and checks it right after the first failed `publishGuard` — before any probe — so the old `guardTimeoutMs: 20` could be consumed by one scratch-write + `link(2)` + scratch-rm through the loaded threadpool, degrading acquisition to contention before the mid-break window existed (no capture, no hook, the stale payload untouched; the refusal still landed via the attempt limit). Proven deterministically at `guardTimeoutMs: 0` (byte-identical failure, probe count 0) and live at 1/10 isolated runs under the six-hog rig (ambient load ~22; worst measured start→capture span 58 ms isolated-rig, the sizing basis — one warm in-pool sample measured 5 ms); the deadline is now sized from the same rig census per D-094 — 1000 ms, ≈17× the worst, equal to the product's default guard budget when sized — with the ~1 s post-capture wait against the live guard being the pinned semantics itself. Payloads and assertions are byte-identical; the probe hook keeps its counter shape and now installs the live replacement exactly once, at the capture-decision probe (review round P3-1: the old rewrite-on-every-poll hook, amplified to ~300 rewrites by the sized wait, let the final path assertion observe the hook's last write instead of the product keeping the guard). The product protocol is sound under every deadline interleaving (preemption degrades to the same bounded contention refusal with all §5.6/D-086 invariants intact) and is unchanged.

- Compute-bound cell margins for the password-hash suite (task U-049, spec §17.1/§15.2; decision D-094 over D-118's encoding): U-047's stability matrix (full `make check` under the six-hog starvation rig, 2026-11-05) flagged one out-of-scope failure — `test/unit/password-hash.spec.ts` expiring at Vitest's 5000 ms default, green in isolation and in ambient full runs. The suite is genuinely compute-bound, not a synchronization defect: each of its nine scrypt-bearing cells walks the §15.2 KDF one to three times (a full `N=65536, r=8, p=1` derivation per `hashPassword`/verifying `verifyPassword` call; the string-parameters fixture derives at the `N=16384` its stored string carries), so under deliberate CPU starvation the work legitimately exceeds 5 s. Re-measured per the D-094 method (six busy-hog cores over a full coverage `make check`, 2026-11-06, host loadavg ~24 over the ambient parallel-worktree ~18–20): the eight full-cost cells measured 302–916 ms clean (worst of three isolated runs each) and 2.145–5.085 s under the rig — the 5.085 s worst reproducing U-047's expiry, censored at the default's kill — while the N=16384 fixture cell measured 176–220 ms clean and 0.984 s under the rig, borderline against the census's ≥ ~1 s rig arm and included as a same-family KDF cell; the nine now declare `{ timeout: 20_000 }` through the per-test options object (≈3.9× the censored worst; the ≈3× rule holds for any true worst up to ~6.7 s), with the measurement recorded in the suite's docblock. The 20 no-KDF cells (the 17 malformed-parse rows plus the absent, non-string, and node-refused guards) do no derivation work (≤3 ms under the rig) and stay on the default, keeping the timeout a meaningful statement; no assertion, workload, or parameter changed. Stability: the suite green 4/4 in isolated rig runs at loadavg ~27–28 and in the full ambient `make check`.

- Documentation-drift backlog from the external review round (task U-037, spec §4.1/§18; decision D-170): every flagged claim was re-verified against the code before editing. README's "Project status" and CONTRIBUTING's development-environment section now state the landed truth (Stages 0–13 complete — environment, quality gates, storage, provider, time zones, CLI, API, UI with E2E acceptance; Stage 14 packaging/release pending) instead of the pre-Stage-1 bootstrapping era; the README structure map, `docs/architecture/trust-boundaries.md`, and `docs/architecture/package-layout.md` no longer claim TypeBox validation inside provider adapters — TypeBox schemas executed by Ajv validate the config (D-018) and API (D-128) boundaries, while the Z.ai adapter validates responses locally (D-047/D-053) against the §8.4 model it consumes type-only; `test/README.md`'s property-test bullet points at the actual seed convention (`test/helpers/property.ts`: shared `numRuns`, `FC_SEED` replay) instead of at itself; `stryker.config.mjs`'s header states the real scope and cadence (all of `src/`, the four §17.3 critical modules included, daily D-045 run) instead of describing those modules as future work; and the D-162 executor correction reaches `docs/mutation-testing.md` and `docs/agents/gotchas.md` (the mutation job runs in the `node:22.13.0` job container, not on a pinned `ubuntu-24.04` runner image); the same mutation doc's red-job triage case no longer points at "when the critical modules land" future work or sends runs that die before scoring to phantom survivor triage (the current U-045/U-046 state is stated, with U-046 owning the workflow-side fix). `docs/release.md` needed no change: every claim (pipeline steps, npm pin, green-CI gate, daily-not-gated mutation, the task-184 dependency) matches the post-U-029 workflow as committed.

- Cross-module seam pins from the external review round (task U-038, spec §10/§9.3/§13; decision D-171): three unpinned seams, each verified first and closed as fix-or-document. **The clock zone-validation memo is module-bounded** — `isValidTimeZone`'s `zoneChecks` map cached successes and failures unboundedly, its growth resting on the unstated premise that callers pass only validated configuration zones; the verified production domain is indeed config-only, but the exported gate admits any string (failures cached too) and V8's `Intl` accepts case-mangled spellings of valid names, so no honest finite-domain contract exists — the memo now stops caching at the exported `ZONE_CHECK_MEMO_CAPACITY` (1024, sized over the tzdb's ≈600 names including backward links — the engine's own list carries ≈420 — and test-pinned against both counts), answering further names by fresh computation with identical results; a counting-`Intl.DateTimeFormat` test observes the bound executably (cached names construct nothing, beyond-capacity names pay the construction per call). **Peak-hours interval objects are frozen at construction** — D-073 froze the timeline and its intervals array but left the intervals themselves mutable, the outlier against the services layer's value-object convention (the downsample buckets, retention reports, and rolling bounds); both construction sites now `Object.freeze`, with no wire or schema change (the `/api/peak-hours` route copies intervals via spread). **The closed-vs-half-open range seam is documented as deliberate and pinned cross-module** — peak-hours timelines partition `[from, to)` (load-bearing for D-073's contract: adjacent ranges tile with no double coverage) while the rolling ranges (D-068) and retention bounds (D-082) are closed `[from, to]` for point membership (a row stamped exactly at `to` is inside the read); the two conventions meet at §13's boundary where one numeric `from`/`to` pair serves both surfaces, all three module headers now state the seam, the new `test/unit/range-inclusivity-seam.spec.ts` pins both conventions over identical bounds plus each side's forcing property, and the repository suite pins the closed side's SQL (an exact-at-`to` row asserted for every history read, the events row added here) — so silently normalizing either side fails `make check`.

- Crashed mutation runs are reported honestly — no phantom "below threshold", no blanked rolling-issue body (task U-046, spec §4.4/§17.3; decision D-172): since 2026-11-01 a daily run dying in Stryker's initial test run (U-045's `ConfigError`) masqueraded as a quality regression — `steps.stryker.outcome` is `failure` for any Stryker-step failure, so the issue title, the run comment, and the final `::error` claimed a below-break score that was never measured, while the render step's ENOENT left its shell redirect holding an empty body that the upsert fed to `gh issue edit --body-file`, destroying the previous day's report (issue #7's body sat empty for four days while its title said "below threshold"). **One discriminator now separates the two worlds: the `reports/mutation/mutation.json` file's existence** — a scored report plus a failed step is a genuine below-break (the outcome picks below-threshold/pass only inside that branch); an absent report is a crash, the job still red but with the honest annotation "no score was measured". **The crash body is a renderer output** (D-054's committed seam, unit-tested): `scripts/render-mutation-report.mjs` gains `renderCrashReport`/`extractFailedInitialTests` — a missing JSON exits 0 with a body naming the crash, Stryker's last ERROR line as the cause, the failing initial-run tests extracted from the captured log (format verified against the installed Stryker's `logFailedTestsInInitialRun` source; capped at 20, ids/messages clipped), the run link, and an explicit "this is NOT a below-threshold score"; a present-but-unparseable report still aborts loudly, and the scored path is byte-stable (verified against the last real artifact). **The last scored body is irreplaceable by a non-score**: the upsert's only `--body-file` edit requires a scored report AND a non-empty body; a crashed run retitles to "crashed (no report produced)" and posts the crash report as the run's comment — and if the renderer itself aborts, no empty body is ever written anywhere. The Stryker step tees its output (`shell: bash` pipefail) and a preservation step copies `stryker-output.log` into the artifact exactly when no report was produced, so the crash evidence survives past the run log's retention; a new workflow guard spec (`test/unit/mutation-workflow.spec.ts`, the U-033 line-scanner pattern) pins the gating, the log preservation, the single body-edit line, and the two distinct failure messages, while `docs/mutation-testing.md` gains the crashed-run "How to react" case beside the below-threshold one.

- `collection_runs` data-completeness gaps (task U-035, spec §11.2/§7.2/§7.4/§8.4; decision D-169): three review findings (deepseek-v4.1-flash P3-1/P3-2/P3-3, qwen3.8-flash converging on the third) close as populate-or-fix, each decided from §11.2's own column contracts. **Successful runs record `http_status`** — the column D-034 pinned "absent when no response arrived" was constant NULL on the majority path because §8.4's `ProviderSnapshot` carried no status (D-069's recorded gap, which named this §8.4-surface successor decision): `ProviderSnapshot` gains an optional `httpStatus` (validated integer 100–599 at the D-010 boundary), the Z.ai adapter stamps endpoint 1's delivered 2xx (the metrics-originating exchange, D-047 — a soft endpoint's failure never becomes cycle evidence), and the settle seam retains it through `classifySuccess`'s already-existing optional `httpStatus` parameter — the rail D-048 built, which no caller had fed until now; a non-stamping adapter still stores NULL (absent-when-no-response, now a reachable state, not a constant), and an out-of-range stamp narrows to the seam's existing invalid-snapshot `parse_error` run. **Stored raw responses carry `content_type`** — D-051 created the column for §11.2's "content type" row and recorded it as "the header value verbatim or absent", but no wire ever carried a value: `SnapshotParseError` now hands the observed `Content-Type` through D-041's sanitizer (a credential echoed in a media-type parameter stores `[REDACTED]` — §15.4 refining "verbatim" the way D-042 scrubs every retained server string), the persist seam's structural narrowing admits absent/blank-as-absent/non-empty and fails fast on anything else, and `quotameter doctor --raw` reads the real header instead of the permanent "unknown content type" (the renderer already displayed the column). **A thrown retry wrapper no longer bleeds its cycle-start stamp into the next run row** — D-059's wrapper throws only on programming mistakes (a broken sleep seam, an invalid random roll), and that path never reached `settleCycle`, so the recorder's first-attempt `??=` stamp survived into the next cycle: the next row's `started_at` (D-069's "real reading at the cycle's first attempt") could name a cycle up to an hour earlier, misstating §12.2 timeline spans. `PollingAccount` gains an optional `abandonCycle()` hook — invoked by the engine's wrapper-throw containment (a throwing hook is one more programming mistake on that already-contained path: the outer catch makes the same plain-interval fallback) and implemented by the recorder as dropping the stamp; a signal-identity re-stamp was rejected (the fresh-per-flight controller is undocumented discipline the persist suites' own harnesses already violate), and a wiring that never calls the hook keeps the old semantics, pinned by test. No migration: the columns and their CHECKs already admit every populated value; no API/UI wire changes (§13 serves storage records, and the UI imports nothing from `src/schemas/metrics`).

- Playwright JUnit report follows the env-configured output directory (task U-034, spec §17; docs/agents/environment.md): `playwright.config.ts` hardcoded the CI JUnit reporter's `outputFile: 'reports/e2e-junit.xml'` relative to the repository root — the last writing tool that ignored the container uid invariant (container uid 1000 must never write into the host-owned bind mount; a container run with `CI` set failed with `EACCES` only after the whole suite had passed, at report-write time). The XML now lands in **`REPORTS_DIR`** — the same directory the Vitest JUnit report (`test:ci`'s `${REPORTS_DIR:-reports}/junit.xml`), license reports, and Stryker HTML use, per the documented pattern (a CI/report artifact, not a run artifact — `E2E_OUTPUT_DIR` keeps owning `outputDir`, the traces/videos) — with the same host-friendly default `reports/e2e-junit.xml`, so the CI workflow is byte-for-byte unchanged (`CI` sets no `REPORTS_DIR`; the `test` job's `reports/` artifact upload keeps finding the file); the directory is created at config-resolution time (the `scripts/check-licenses.mjs` sibling pattern), so a target directory that cannot be created fails the run at startup instead of after it. The guard `test/unit/playwright-junit-path.spec.ts` pins the fix both ways — behaviorally (CI mode resolves the outputFile inside `REPORTS_DIR` and creates the directory; unset env keeps the local `'list'`-only reporter shape, mirrored from the imported config) and textually (the path derives from `process.env.REPORTS_DIR ?? 'reports'`, no hardcoded in-tree outputFile literal returns, the exact reporter line keeps the junit reporter CI-gated, both junit outputs stay on one `REPORTS_DIR` root, and the CI test job's — not merely any job's — upload path stays in lockstep).

### Removed

- The unreferenced ~1.3 MB repo-root `favicon.png` (task U-037; decision D-170): an exploratory commit's leftover with no reference anywhere in the tree — no `ui/public/` directory, no icon link in `ui/index.html`, never served (the static root is the built `ui/dist` only), never packaged (outside the `files` allowlist and `scripts/check-package.mjs`'s `ALLOWED`). A real UI icon, if ever wanted, belongs in `ui/public/` plus an explicit link (D-170).

### Added

- CI supply-chain hardening (task U-033, spec §4.4; decisions D-157/D-168): the two verified gaps close. **The Node runtime archive** the `install` job downloads from nodejs.org is now verified against SHA-256 digests **pinned in the workflow, in repository history** — the one trust domain a compromised mirror/CDN cannot rewrite (the same reasoning as the SHA-pinned actions; fetching `SHASUMS256.txt` from the same CDN at run time would verify the mirror against itself and is rejected). The digests are the official `https://nodejs.org/dist/v22.13.0/SHASUMS256.txt` values for both Linux architectures, re-verified by downloading and hashing both archives before pinning; the step selects one by a fail-closed `case` arm (unknown architectures exit instead of downloading an empty-arch URL) and checks the file with `sha256sum -c` between the download and the extraction; the bump procedure (every site, from the workflow's `version=` line and digest arms through the `node -v` expectation, the test matrix's declared-minimum entry and comment, the timezone job's pin, the unsupported-node grep, and `package.json`'s `engines.node` to the guard's own constants) is written out in the step's comment. **The Dependabot `dependencies` label** — assigned by both `dependabot.yml` update blocks but missing from the repository (the U-004 failure class) — is resolved by creating the metadata, not weakening the config: it exists on `yepbro/quotameter` (`gh label create dependencies --repo yepbro/quotameter --description "Dependency updates (Dependabot)" --color 0366d6`), with the repair one-liner documented in the dependabot.yml header and `docs/agents/workflow.md`; an in-workflow bootstrap cannot work for a GitHub-managed service that opens PRs before any workflow of ours runs, and the explicit `labels:` declaration stays because a `labels:`-assigned label missing from the repository is silently ignored (documented Dependabot behavior) while the declaration pins the exact label set — one shared `dependencies`, not the defaults' `dependencies` plus per-ecosystem labels (which Dependabot auto-creates). The new `test/unit/ci-workflow.spec.ts` (the U-029 line-scanner pattern over ci.yml) pins the exact download/verify/extract shapes in download → verify → extract order, bans `set +e`/`continue-on-error` softening, binds each digest to its architecture arm, and enforces the version consistency across every ci.yml site (matrix entry and comment included) and `package.json`'s `engines.node`, beside the dependabot declarations. Evidence: the guard green 5/5 with lethality proven on seven scratch mutations (removed and `|| true`-softened verify line, swapped digests, `set +e`, `continue-on-error`, a lone `version=` bump, a dropped label declaration — each failing the intended pin); the exact download+verify lines dry-run clean against the real archive and fail closed on a corrupted one under both `bash -e` (GitHub's default shell for `run:` — `bash -e {0}`) and `bash -e -o pipefail`; `actionlint` 1.7.12 (docker `rhysd/actionlint`) reports 0 errors; digests re-verified against SHASUMS256.txt; green `make check`. The review round (kimi as the first fallback — the primary deepseek-v4.1-flash timed out with no report; one round, APPROVE with 0 P0 / 0 P1 / 4 P2) was folded in before landing: the bump procedure now lists all sites (the matrix entry and timezone pin were missing), the guard pins the matrix comment's version literal, the Dependabot removal rationale is corrected to the documented behavior (the reviewer verified against the Dependabot options reference: missing `labels:`-assigned labels are silently ignored, default labels are auto-created), and the fail-closed wording states the runner's actual default shell — pipefail needs an explicit `shell: bash`, and the checksum check fails closed under both settings (re-verified on a corrupted archive).

- Full Playwright acceptance scenarios (task 183, spec §14, §17.1; decision D-166): the §17.1 acceptance suite lands as `test/e2e/acceptance/` (nine spec files, 23 scenarios) running against the **production Vite build served by the real server construction** — each spec file's `beforeAll` starts `composeApplication` (the assembly `runServer` builds) as a `QuotameterApp` on an ephemeral loopback port over fresh temp directories (the a11y file deliberately starts a second, password-mode world for its login scan), with the **real Z.ai adapter** registered and pointed at the task-026 loopback mock provider (`test/e2e/helpers/acceptance.ts`, the shared harness; the only outbound traffic is the adapter's loopback credential check, and the browser stays same-origin behind the served CSP). The scenarios: the fresh-install empty states on both pages (the overview's guidance block with its live CTA, the accounts page's empty state beside a ready add form — zero load failures, the honest surface the preview-server suites cannot produce); account creation through the real form with §14.2/§15.4 key masking end to end (the constant `********` mask on the card, the marker secret nowhere in the DOM and nowhere in the server's log lines — re-scanned after both credential-check round trips) and the §8.2 connection test against the mock's real 200/401 answers; **all eight §7.3/§14.6 statuses** (Fresh, Collecting, Unreachable, Auth rejected, Stale, Format changed, Disabled, Not configured — each driven through the real derivation over configuration facts and storage rows seeded through the real write path with the adapter's own per-key unit shapes, anchored to the real clock so the server's `now()` and the browser's rolling ranges stay coherent, with the §7.2 last-error/last-success facts beside the badges and the actionable attention notice for the declared-but-unresolved environment key); §14.4 range disabling (retention 10 disables 14d/30d with the "Exceeds the 10-day history retention" sentence, keeps 24h/3d/7d enabled, the 7-day default checked, switching live); §14.4 chart markers (a genuine §12.1 usage-drop reset, one §12.2 collection gap, two stopped periods, one failed cycle — exact deterministic counts asserted with boundary-anchored retrying attribute assertions on the chart's §14.1 accessible name, the range switch proven on the wire by the re-dispatched ~24-hour request windows, and the peak schedule proven end to end through the wire the page fired: the configured window's peak intervals with its multiplier); themes (the populated overview restyles in both explicit modes with the chart canvas genuinely repainting — a bitmap-change observation, not a baseline); responsive layout (360 px: no horizontal overflow on either page, navigation works;
