1280 px: the metric cards share a row); login/logout over **real §15.2 authentication** (scrypt hash in the config, the 401-driven redirect preserving a non-root deep-link destination, the wrong-password sentence, the real session cookie — HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, path-scoped to `/api`, not Secure over plain HTTP — and the sign-out's server-side revocation proven by replaying the minted token to a 401); and accessibility smoke scans of the populated surfaces in both themes (the token cascade verified applied) plus the real password-mode login page (closing the task-172 recorded seam — the preview a11y suite could only scan degradation surfaces). **The one production-code change** is the `ComposeApplicationOptions.staticRoot` seam — a pass-through to the listener beside the `port` seam's precedent, so the harness serves the container's `DIST_DIR` volume or CI's `ui/dist`; default behavior unchanged, pinned by a new composition test. The suite runs on the existing `npm run test:e2e` path (`make test-e2e` builds first; CI's e2e job already builds the UI), alongside the untouched preview-server suites. Evidence: 60/60 e2e green (37 existing + 23 acceptance) against the built package + mock provider in the worktree container, and a green `make check` (221 files, 4371 tests, coverage thresholds met).

- UI component-test coverage gaps closed (task 182, spec §17.1; decision D-165): the §17.1 component-test leg audited against its six list items over the suites tasks 169–181 built, and the genuine residue closed — every arm hermetic (fake backends on the injectable fetch seam, the recording chart runtime, the real i18n composer; no network, no provider). **Account forms**: the add form's provider-select reconciliation gains its own component suite (`test/component/account-add-form.spec.ts` — the first provider as the default, an explicit pick surviving a providers reload, a stale pick corrected to the first listed provider with the corrected id sent, and the empty-list state a later list fills); D-135's canonical-label sends are pinned at last (the create/rename POST/PATCH carry the trimmed label — only the purge gate's trim was pinned before) beside the manage card's identity-line cells (the exact mask line, "Not set", the Active/Archived pair). **Metric cards**: the §8.4 vocabulary completes — both frozen enums (`LIMIT_TYPES`, `METRIC_UNITS`) drive render tables, so DAILY, the OTHER fallback arm, TOKENS, and percent as the unit of absolute values ("42 percent") render for the first time — plus the non-integral rolling window (the regime stated without an invented window phrase) and the invalid metric with a null reason (the Unknown reason, never a bare prefix). **Theme switching**: the controller's default composition-root seams (reads `window.localStorage`; wires `window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` — the query pinned and the seam proven live; a throwing `window.localStorage` degrades to the in-memory choice). **Range controls**: D-147's auto-fallback announcement — the polite live region renders the catalog sentence when a late retention disables the selection and retires on the next manual pick. **States**: the accounts and settings pages' initial loading lines (D-138 had pinned the overview's only — held loads over the suite's stall/gate seams, never an error, never a fake empty) and the shell sign-out's pending lock (the control disables, re-labels to "Signing out…", and a second click dispatches nothing). **Localized strings**: the catalog-completeness proof gains its interpolation half — every `{placeholder}` is supplied at EVERY call site (a static harvest that fails loudly on shapes it cannot parse, the non-literal shapes resolved from their same-file const initializer, and the closed chart-template table for the keys the chart builder interpolates itself), no supplied name is dead, and the whole catalog renders through the real composer (paramless keys verbatim — an accidental plural bar or linked-message syntax compiles to something else and fails; parameterized keys with no residual braces). Lethality proven on three scratch mutations (a renamed placeholder, a drifted seam-table entry, a plural bar in a paramless value), each reverted. No `ui/**` seam was needed — product behavior untouched. Evidence: 580 component tests green (`make test-component`, ~21 new arms) and a green `make check`.

- Responsive and accessible UI behavior, closed as a verify-and-fix audit (task 181, spec §14.1, §17.1; decision D-164): the whole landed UI audited against §14.1's four demands — WCAG AA contrast, keyboard operation, non-color status signals, and widths from 360 px — with three fixes landing and the proof layer extended to the surfaces no cell covered. **The audit matrix** (screens × themes × widths × workflows, recorded in D-164): every page now has an axe scan of its populated surface in both explicit themes (the system-mode duplication is pinned by the theme spec's raw-token reads), 360 px overflow cells cover all four pages (overview and settings existed; accounts and login join), and the keyboard-workflow half of the done-condition gains its first executable proofs (`test/e2e/keyboard.spec.ts`): shell Tab order with Enter navigation and arrow-operated theme select, the login round trip by keyboard alone (redirect → type → Enter submit → destination), the §14.4 range switch through the native radio group proven by the re-dispatched `/api/history` window span, the inline confirmation panels' Enter-open/Escape-close/focus-restoration contract, and the §14.5 time-zone suggestions' ArrowDown/Enter/Escape seams. **Fix 1 — a designed focus indicator**: one global `:focus-visible` rule in `base.css` (a 2 px `--qm-color-foreground` outline with offset, all three modes; CSP-neutral stylesheet), replacing the per-widget UA defaults — pinned by a computed-outline assertion in the keyboard spec. **Fix 2 — panel dismissal**: the accounts card's four inline surfaces (rename form, replace-key form, archive confirm, purge confirm) close on Escape and return focus to their opener button — focus moves IN on open (the archive confirm lands on the safe control, D-135) and BACK on dismissal AND on a successful save alike, with a removed account handing focus to the accounts selector from the parent (the panels previously only moved focus in on open, and every close dropped focus to the body); Escape during a pending mutation is ignored (the dispatched request owns its panel until it settles — resetting mid-flight would suppress the outcome while the request still applies), and IME composition owns the Escape key while active; component arms pin the contract per panel. **Fix 3 — the time-zone suggestion list's keyboard semantics** (179's declined combobox P3, explicitly handed to this pass): the field keeps D-156's native-controls stance (real focusable buttons, no `aria-activedescendant`) and gains the ARIA-1.2-legal linkage — `aria-describedby` to the search hint, `aria-autocomplete="list"`, and `aria-controls` rendered only while the list exists (a dangling id reference is an axe violation; `aria-expanded` on a plain textbox is prohibited by ARIA 1.2 and stays with the declined combobox contract) — plus ArrowDown into the first suggestion, Enter picking with focus returned to the field, and Escape reverting the edit from either the field or a suggestion. **New axe cells**: the charts region rendered with its FULL annotation vocabulary (a points-bearing dual-view series with a reset, a gap, a failed cycle, a stopped region, and a windows-bearing peak schedule — every earlier cell's fixture answered the history load empty, so the chart image, its aria summary, and the toggle had never been scanned, with a live ECharts canvas booted inside the scanned host), the populated accounts page with its purge confirm panel open, and the login failure surface (the `role="alert"` sentence beside the aria-invalid field). The chart zoom-in gesture stays pointer-only by recorded decision: the chart is a `role="img"` text alternative over data fully available as text (metric cards, the aria summary's annotation counts), the zoom reset is keyboard-operable, and ECharts ships no keyboard dataZoom (D-164). The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, REQUEST_CHANGES with 1 P1 / 2 P2 / 5 P3, the reviewer live-probing Chromium for the P1) was folded in before landing: the suggestion pick now returns focus to the field (the picked button's unmount had dropped focus to the body), successful saves and removed accounts restore focus too, Escape-during-pending is ignored (the outcome can no longer be silently swallowed), every Escape handler respects IME composition, the annotated-charts fixture became contract-faithful (time-ascending events, schedule-zone local times), the login write-only pin asserts inside the pending window over a delayed response, and the chart aria summary asserts all four annotation counts.

- Login/logout UI and session handling (task 180, spec §14.6, §15.1, §15.2; decision D-159): the `/login` page becomes the real §14.6 password form and the shell gains the sign-out control, both driven by a client-side session layer that stays **mode-agnostic by design** — the UI never learns the §15.1 auth mode (the only mode-fact surface, `/api/settings`, is itself protected under `required`, and `GET /api/auth/session` stays unwired — D-146 left it to this task's consumer, but the server-side carve-out froze `src/**`; U-055 below later lands the endpoint, and D-182 keeps this cache probe-free over it; more fundamentally the tri-state needs no probe: the cookie rides every request, so the first protected load discovers the truth with no extra round trip). **`ui/src/session.ts`** is the layer: a tri-state `unknown | authenticated | unauthenticated` (factory+plugin+inject, the D-131…D-135 shape, in-memory only — the cookie is the session's only truth and a reload honestly restarts at `unknown`); `unknown` renders protected pages optimistically, and in `auto`-loopback (§15.1's default) no 401 ever arrives — nothing forces `/login`, exactly §14.6/§15.1's semantics. The wire's `UNAUTHENTICATED` envelope answers flip the state through a new `onUnauthenticated` hook on the API client, fired at the taxonomy's own classification boundary so **every session-bearing request** reports the signal whatever dispatched it (D-134's "the notice's UNAUTHENTICATED branch is the seam" prediction superseded by the stronger interception point; the branch stays as the presentation while the redirect takes the page away) — with the login arm alone exempted (a rejected candidate is a statement about the candidate, not the session: in the passwordless `auto`-loopback deployment every login 401s, and one curious submit would otherwise brick SPA navigation until a reload). **Routing**: `installSessionRouting()` installs a `beforeEach` guard (an authenticated visit to `/login` bounces to the preserved destination as a string location, so a destination carrying `?query`/`#hash` keeps them; an unauthenticated visit to a protected target redirects to `/login` with `redirect=<fullPath>`) plus a reactive watcher (a flip while a protected page is already rendered navigates with the destination preserved, never from the login route itself); `loginReturnTarget()` validates the attacker-influenceable `redirect` query — same-origin paths only (leading `/`, no `//` protocol-relative escape, no backslash, nothing resolving onto the login route in any casing or slash form), everything else collapses to `/` (the open-redirect guard). **The login form** is deliberately load-free (zero fetch on mount — the csp-shell e2e cells keep their `/login` deep link; D-156's predicted displacement did not materialize): a blank guard client-side, the write-only password (masked, `autocomplete="current-password"`, cleared at dispatch — the body is built synchronously, so the candidate never lingers through the pending window — sent verbatim: never trimmed, §15.2's 12-character minimum binds the setter, never the verifier), and D-146's wire arms presented as catalog sentences selected by the envelope's `details.reason` (`invalid-credentials` → incorrect, `source-busy` → busy, absent/unknown/hostile → the generic failure — the payload never renders, D-134's discipline); §15.2's budget delays are waited out, not raced — the login request carries its own `LOGIN_TIMEOUT_MS` (250 s, above D-146's 240 s sleep clamp and below the construction's 300 s socket bound, pinned by test; delays are invisible on the wire, so an always-visible hint states the behavior), and pending locks the field and submit with a "Signing in…" label. **Sign-out** is a header button (no login nav entry — §14.6 asks for a page) visible unless the wire said `unauthenticated` or the current route is the login page (an authenticated visitor never sits there); a failed sign-out's shared notice clears on the next completed navigation a successful logout resets the store and **reloads the document** to `/` — a router push would be deduped at `/` (no remount, no fresh loads, no 401 to drive the redirect under `required`), while the reload restarts the SPA at `unknown` and the next wire answers decide the world: under `required` the fresh overview load's 401 redirects to `/login?redirect=/`, under `auto`-loopback the overview simply renders — and every in-memory artifact of the authenticated session dies with the document. A 401 logout answer needs no notice (the hook fires and the watcher navigates); every other failure rides the shared `ApiErrorNotice` in the shell. Evidence: `test/component/session.spec.ts` (the store, the destination-validator table incl. the open-redirect arms, the guard matrix, the reactive redirect), `test/component/login-view.spec.ts` (zero-fetch first paint, the blank guard, the exact §15.3 wire shape, the destination round trip incl. the unsafe collapse, the write-only pins, the D-146 arm matrix incl. hostile and absent reasons, the shared-notice arms, the pending locks), `test/component/api-client.spec.ts` (the auth family's round trips, the per-request deadline override, the `LOGIN_TIMEOUT_MS` relation pin, the `onUnauthenticated` firing matrix), `test/component/app.spec.ts` (the sign-out control, the logout wire pin, the document-reload routing), the updated shell/wiring specs, and `test/e2e/login.spec.ts` (the 401 → login → sign-in → destination round trip, both failure arms, and the sign-out invalidation: the next protected load redirects) beside the login-page axe scans in both themes.

- Settings page (task 179, spec §14.5, §6.4, §6.2, §9.2, §14.1, §7.1, §11.3, §15.2; decision D-156): the `/settings` page becomes the real §14.5 surface over the D-130 annotated settings view and the providers listing — only the §6.4-permitted settings editable, everything else read-only with its served annotation. **The page architecture** (`ui/src/views/SettingsView.vue`): two loads (`settings.get`, `providers.list` — the schedule source), last-good copies with the D-135 failed-load rule (a failed reload keeps the rendered surface beside its notice and a named retry; a failed providers load degrades the schedule section alone). **The read-only facts**: `server.host`, `server.port`, and `auth.mode` render as values with §6.4's "restart required" marker and the remedy line (`quotameter restart` — the exact prescription), never edit controls — the PUT refuses them (D-130), so no control exists to be refused; the §6.1 config/data paths render read-only below. **The editable scalars** (`ui/src/components/SettingsGeneralForm.vue`): display time zone (§14.5's search over IANA zones — a text input with capped, case-insensitive substring suggestions from the browser's own `Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone')` plus a canonical-twin match: the engine lists CLDR-canonical names, so a fully typed tzdb primary like `Asia/Kolkata` surfaces its listed twin `Asia/Calcutta`), polling interval (60–3600, §7.1), history retention (7–3650, §11.3, with the D-147 interplay stated in its hint), and log level (the served contract's fourth live-editable scalar, §14.5-unnamed but contract-real); the PUT body carries **only the changed fields** (D-130/D-140's minimal attempted-write semantics), field-level client guards refuse blanks, non-IANA zones, and out-of-range numbers before any request (aria-invalid + `role="alert"` per the accounts form pattern), a successful save states itself and reloads the served truth (drafts re-adopt), and a non-empty PUT outcome `restartRequired` renders the pending-restart notice naming the fields — the process's hand-edited-file state, never the request's doing. **The theme row**: a labeled select bound to the SAME `useTheme()` controller the header select drives (D-133's hand-off — one source of truth, page-owned `settings.theme.*` keys; the cross-control agreement is pinned by mounting the shell and flipping either select). **The password change** (`ui/src/components/SettingsPasswordForm.vue`): the PUT's `auth.password` channel (the UI's only sanctioned setter beside `passwd`, D-118), rendered in BOTH auth modes with the configured/not-set state line (auto mode uses a password on the §15.1 remote paths — hiding it would strand the operator who set one); the field is write-only by the D-136 discipline (masked, `autocomplete="new-password"`, never prefilled, cleared after every dispatched attempt — success and server refusal alike), the §15.2 minimum (12) is guarded client-side on the raw untrimmed value, and the D-124/D-126 consequence is stated twice: pre-submit in the hint and as the success notice ("All sessions were revoked — sign in again when prompted"). **The peak schedule** (`ui/src/components/SettingsScheduleSection.vue`): each provider's §9.2 configuration rendered as data in its own schedule zone (never the display zone) — schedule time zone, off-peak rate, and the window table (name, days, local start–end, ×multiplier, all served strings verbatim); a null schedule and an empty `windows` array both render §9.2's exact "Peak-hours schedule is not configured." sentence, never an invented schedule, and a registry-empty build states that honestly. **The annotations are the served contract's own**: `source: 'environment'` disables the control and renders the read-only annotation (§6.3), `restartRequired` drives the marker — the page never re-derives precedence knowledge (D-023). The validated ranges, the log-level enum, and the password minimum are UI-local constants in `ui/src/settings-fields.ts`, drift-pinned against their server counterparts by test (the `API_MUTATION_HEADER` precedent — no runtime import of server code); the zone helpers live in `ui/src/timezones.ts` with the validity check mirroring the server's `isValidTimeZoneName` (offset identifiers rejected before the `Intl` probe), drift-pinned over a shared probe set. §14.5's "account management/connection tests" is the landed `/accounts` page (§14.2, D-135) — nothing duplicated here. Evidence: `test/component/settings-view.spec.ts` (the editability matrix — the trio control-free with markers, the marker's served-flag false direction, every env-sourced field disabled with its annotation, file-sourced enabled; the validation arms — interval/retention range tables with the inclusive boundary accepts, blank and non-IANA zones, no dispatch on refusal, aria-invalid, the no-changes disabled save, and the guards-scope pin (a browser-unconfirmable served zone never blocks an unrelated save); the PUT grammar — single-field and combined bodies exact, the suggestion-pick and canonical-twin flows, the picked-suggestion error clearing, the saved/reload/re-adopt cycle, the pending-restart notice incl. its survival of a refused submit, the FORBIDDEN refusal keeping drafts through the shared notice; the password matrix — configured state incl. its flip on success, write-only pins, the 11/12-character boundary, the whitespace-only D-011 refusal, the pending-disabled input, cleared-after-success-and-refusal, the consequence wordings, no password material in the rendered DOM; the schedule matrix — §9.1 verbatim, null/empty-windows sentences, registry-empty; the theme cross-control agreement at the shell level), `test/component/timezones.spec.ts` (the server-agreement probe set, the engine-list facts, the suggestion filter incl. the canonical twin and the partial-alias limit), the shell specs updated for the real page (`app.spec.ts`'s settings cell, `api-error-wiring.spec.ts`'s content marker), and e2e: the per-theme axe scan of the populated page through route interception (restart markers, an env-annotated field, the §9.1 schedule, the password section) beside a 360 px overflow cell in `test/e2e/a11y.spec.ts`, and the csp-shell deep link moved to `/login` — task 179 made `/settings` an API-loading page and that construction wires no data routes (the D-135 `/accounts` precedent, task 180 inherits the displacement). The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, REQUEST_CHANGES with 1 P1 / 2 P2 / 8 P3) was folded in before landing — the correction trail is D-156.
- Synchronized chart cursor and zoom (task 178, spec §14.4, §14.1; decision D-153): the §14.4 sentence "Synchronized cursor and zoom across charts on the same page" lands — hovering one metric chart moves the tooltip/axis pointer on every other chart of the account at the same instant, and a zoom window on one chart applies to all. **The mechanism is source-verified against the 6.1.0 source and is ECharts' own `connect` registry**: every chart of the page is created into one `connect` group (a page-minted id; `chart.group` + `connect(group)`, wired through the runtime seam's `createChart(element, {syncGroup})`), and the registry relays the registered actions' events between the group's instances — the value-carrying `updateAxisPointer` event for the cursor (each receiving chart re-triggers by VALUE through its own richText tooltip channel; the internal `showTip` carries `escapeConnect`, so no loop; CSP-neutral, no DOM styling) and the percent-based batched `dataZoom` action for the zoom, matched on a **stable dataZoom id** (`qm-chart-x-zoom`) every chart of the page shares — percent semantics name the same instants everywhere because every chart's x-extent is the same requested-bounds window (D-148). **Zoom stays inside the loaded range** (percent of the fixed extent; a zoom re-queries nothing — D-147's radios stay the load authority), is one `inside` dataZoom over BOTH x-axes (the strip rides the window) with `filterMode: 'weakFilter'` (bands/markers straddling the edge stay, clipped), `zoomOnMouseWheel: 'shift'` (the page keeps scrolling, §14.1), and a `minValueSpan` of one hour — the floor of D-150's tick ladder, so a zoomed window never renders unlabeled axes (sub-hour rungs stay D-150's recorded follow-up). **The tick ladder re-densifies with the zoom**: the card feeds its own `dataZoom` events back into the pure builder, which walks the zoom span for ticks while the axes' `min`/`max` STAY the requested bounds — the percent base the relay's arithmetic depends on (the alignment invariant). **The shared state is page-owned** (`ui/src/charts/sync.ts`, echarts-free): a `zoomed`/`zoomWindow` pair the coordinator derives from the cards' reports, the empty-group-forgets rule (a range or account switch rebuilds every card — the next window's charts start unzoomed, never re-applying the previous window's percent to different instants), late-joiner catch-up (a card whose data lands last adopts the live window through an attach-time dispatch), and a reset that dispatches to every live handle directly (deterministic, observable at the seam). A refresh re-anchors the window, so each zoomed card resets its own zoom (the old percent would name different instants). **§14.1 surfaces**: a keyboard-operable "Reset zoom" button in the charts header rendered exactly while a zoom is live, and a polite `role="status"` live region announcing the transitions (the applied window's instants in the display zone, or the reset) — one sentence per transition, never per wheel tick; the axis-pointer's on-axis label now formats through the zone (`formatChartDate`) and colors through the tooltip tokens — ECharts' default rendered the raw epoch-ms number in its built-in theme colors. **D-148's deferred merge-based `setOption` follow-through lands**: updates now merge with `replaceMerge: ['series','xAxis','yAxis','grid']` — the four count-varying families fully replace (annotation series and strip axes appear/disappear with the events/peaks props; no stale component survives, the old `notMerge` guarantee) while ECharts' own interaction state (the dataZoom window, a legend series toggle) persists across data, view, theme, and tick updates; stable ids on every grid, axis, and series make the merge matching index-independent (y-axis indices shift when bands appear). The in-chart `axisPointer.link` D-149 suggested was **evaluated and declined with a source-found reason**: a linked strip axis processes with `noSnap`, so its NEAREST marker's tooltip params would join every main-grid hover — stating a failure at instants that had none — and the interval gating that would bound it breaks direct marker hovers; the cross-chart relay (the actual §14.4 sentence) never needed it. Tests: the new `test/component/chart-sync.spec.ts` (the coordinator state machine: both `dataZoom` event shapes, the full-range epsilon, report/reset round-trips, late-joiner catch-up, empty-group forgets), `chart-series.spec.ts`'s builder cells (the dataZoom/axisPointer shape, the zoom-span tick walk with the bounds pinned, the stable ids across shapes), `metric-history-chart.spec.ts`'s mounted cells (the group join, the zoom→ticks feedback, the refresh reset, the identity clear, the detach lifecycle), `overview-view.spec.ts`'s page cells (one group for all cards, the affordance lifecycle, the zone-formatted announcement, the single-chart degenerate), and the new `test/e2e/chart-sync.spec.ts` — the real relay in a real browser over route-intercepted data, asserted through the DOM alone (the affordance appears on shift+wheel over one canvas and retires on the click) — was hardened by the review round: the announcement is snapshotted at transitions (never re-announced per wheel tick), the resize re-fit gate reads the ACTIVE zoom span, and the e2e proves the relay itself through a second-canvas bitmap-change assertion (negative-checked against a build with the group join removed).

- Configured-timezone chart axis tick placement (task 177, spec §14.4, §10; decision D-150): the metric charts' x-axes place their ticks on LOCAL hour/day/month boundaries of the configured display zone — DST transition days included — closing the §14.4 requirement ECharts' own value-axis ladder cannot express (`intervalScaleNiceTicks` places ticks on a uniform epoch-milliseconds grid, which can neither hold a +05:45 zone's :15 phase nor follow an offset shift mid-window). **The mechanism is source-verified**: ECharts 6's `axisLabel.customValues`/`axisTick.customValues` replace the auto tick set with explicit instants (filtered to the extent, still formatted through the axis formatter), and the new pure engine `ui/src/charts/ticks.ts` (the D-148 pure-builder pattern) supplies them — an instant-space hour walk (probe-and-snap, so a ±30-minute zone like Australia/Lord_Howe follows its real 90-minute boundary gaps and its skipped wall 02:00), wall-space day and month walks over epoch-day/calendar-month anchors (a 7-day step keeps one weekday), and a two-probe wall→epoch resolver whose skipped-wall arm lands on the boundary's actual first instant (America/Havana's 2026-03-08 midnight does not exist — that day starts at 01:00 and its tick still says "Mar 8"). **The DST done-condition, pinned on literal epochs**: a 23-hour day renders no tick for its skipped wall hour (New York 2026-03-08: 00,01,03,04… — the first post-transition tick suffixes "EDT"; the local midnights sit 23 h apart), a 25-hour day ticks the duplicated wall hour twice one real hour apart with both offsets named ("1 AM EDT", "1 AM EST"; midnights 25 h apart — and the disambiguation is global over the ladder, so a fall-back longer than an hour, Antarctica/Troll's two-hour shift, names every duplicated wall hour), and the walks stay exact under half/quarter-hour offsets (Kathmandu, Lord Howe). Density follows the MEASURED canvas width (the card reads its host at chart creation and re-fits through its `ResizeObserver` when the change moves the ladder to a different step — a within-step stretch is `resize()` alone; §14.1's 360 px viewport is the unmeasured floor): a wall-clock step ladder — 1/2/3/6/12 h, 1/2/7/14 d, 1/3/6 mo — chosen finest-first against per-kind label gaps, with sub-hour degenerate spans rendering their 0–1 contained boundaries rather than invented sub-hour alignment. Both x-axes of a chart (main plot and the task-176 error strip) share the SAME instants through D-149's fixed insets, the labels render per granularity ("2 PM", "Nov 4", "Nov 2026" — new cached `Intl` formatters in `ui/src/format.ts`), and the tooltip keeps its full `formatChartDate` header. Machine-local `TZ` independence is structural (every primitive takes the explicit zone — the D-021 discipline mirrored client-side) and verified: the suites pass green under `TZ=America/New_York`, `Asia/Kathmandu`, `Pacific/Kiritimati`, and `Australia/Lord_Howe`. The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, REQUEST_CHANGES with 2 P2 / 4 P3 — the reviewer reproduced its findings with its own 16-zone sweep, a headless ECharts 6.1.0 render, and a Vue 3.5.44 self-trigger probe; all folded in). Evidence: the new `test/component/chart-ticks.spec.ts` (the granularity table, the plain/half-hour-zone hour walks, the DST-day proofs — the four transition days above plus Troll's two-hour fall-back and Apia's skipped 2011 calendar day, on pinned literal epochs, the day/month walks incl. Havana's skipped midnight and 7-day/3-month anchoring oracles, the degenerate spans), `test/component/chart-series.spec.ts` extended over the builder (customValues on both axes, the label map and its full-date fallback, the width-driven coarsening, the 360 px floor, the unchanged grid insets), `test/component/metric-history-chart.spec.ts` extended over the mounted card (every tick locally aligned in a +05:45 zone and inside the half-open window, the strip/main axis instants identical, the width seam re-fitting through a controllable ResizeObserver incl. the within-step no-rebuild gate), and the four formatters in `test/component/format.spec.ts`.

- Chart reset markers, peak bands, gaps, and error markers (task 176, spec §14.4, §12.2, §9.3, §14.1; decision D-149): the §14.4 metric charts render the full annotation vocabulary on top of task 175's line pairs — vertical labeled quota-reset markLines, peak/off-peak schedule bands, collection-gap hatch regions, stopped-service hatch regions, and failed collection cycles as distinct-shaped markers on a lower timeline strip. **The encoding is source-verified, not guessed**: ECharts' marker components (markArea) never pass through the decal→pattern visual stage (it runs only over raw series, `eachRawSeries`), so the spec's "hatched regions" can only render through a **custom series** (`CustomView` converts `style.decal` itself) — one silent custom series draws every band kind on the main grid (peak tinted + solid border, off-peak unfilled + dashed border, gaps 45°-hatched + dashed, stopped counter-angle-hatched + solid border + on-band "Service stopped" label when wide enough); resets are markLines on the used series with an on-line "Reset" label; failed cycles are a scatter series on a second, lower grid whose fixed left/right insets (no `containLabel`) align with the main plot by construction, one distinct symbol per §11.2 outcome (diamond/triangle/circle/rect/roundRect/pin — a `never`-guarded switch). **Every tooltip flows through the single axis-trigger richText channel**: per-element item tooltips race the axis flow (the axis pointer replaces them on every move — verified in the 6.1.0 tooltip source), so all marks are `silent` and the one formatter states, for the hovered instant, the values beside the covering schedule band (§14.4: window name, rate multiplier, and times in BOTH the display and the schedule zone — the schedule pair the server's own D-073 local-time strings, verbatim per §9.3's browser-never-calculates rule), the covering gap/stopped region with its duration, and the resets inside the snapped sample interval; the strip's markers carry their prebuilt lines (outcome word, attempts, HTTP status, retry-after, sanitized message) on the datum. **Never color alone (§14.1)**: fill presence, border style, hatch direction, on-line/on-band labels, per-outcome shapes, the legend's "Failed collections" name, and the aria summary's annotation counts all carry meaning beside the four new theme tokens (`--qm-chart-peak`/`-reset`/`-gap`/`-stopped` in all three base.css blocks, beside `--qm-color-danger` for the error markers — verbatim per-mode pins in the e2e chart-contract cell). **The account-vs-metric mapping (D-149)**: resets and gaps are per-metric facts and filter to their metric's chart; failed cycles and stopped regions are account-level and render identically on every chart of the account — the page's D-147 account-level timeline load is the events source, and a new third range-keyed load (`api.peakHours.get`, the typed client's type-only D-134 pattern) supplies the bands; both keep last-good copies cleared on identity/range change (D-135's rule), re-run on refresh, and render distinctly-named retries on failure. Every band clamps to the card's own window (the loads bound their own `Date.now()`); a null/no-schedule/rejected peak response renders no bands — the browser never invents a schedule. The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, REQUEST_CHANGES with 3 P1 / 1 P2 / 4 P3 — the reviewer booted a headless ECharts model to prove three behaviors the first cut got wrong) folded in before landing: the strip's axes bind to the strip's grid with an explicit `gridIndex: 1` (ECharts' default attached them to the main grid, rendering the strip inside the main plot); the axis-tooltip formatter renders value lines for the two line series only (`silent` does not keep the bands series out of the axis tooltip's collection — its epoch-ms span datum joined the params as a quota value); the band tint is `fillOpacity`, never element opacity (zrender's element opacity fades the border and hatch together with the fill — the stopped region's hatch rendered at 8% alpha); plus the y-axis label truncation inside the fixed inset, the served-bounds tooltip times, a grid-geometry double-count fix, plural-neutral count wordings, and the pins for all of the above. Evidence: `test/component/chart-series.spec.ts` extended over the pure mapping and option (per-metric/account-level filtering incl. clamping and degenerate drops, the served-interval band mapping, six-distinct symbols, markLine structure, per-kind band styles incl. the hatch rotations, the strip's grid alignment and label ownership, the single-channel tooltip's §14.4 peak content with both zones, gap/stopped/reset/error lines, and the `renderItem` element against a stubbed api incl. the label-width threshold), `test/component/metric-history-chart.spec.ts` extended over the mounted card (the props flow into `setOption` — this metric's reset only, the peak pair, the strip with its legend name; the aria counts; the live theme restyle re-reading a band token; the null-prop 175 shape; the no-schedule arm), `test/component/overview-view.spec.ts` (the peak-hours load's D-139 grammar, range/account re-dispatch, the failure + named retry, the refresh's +1), the `peakHours.get` round-trip in `test/component/api-client.spec.ts`, `formatChartDuration` in `test/component/format.spec.ts`, and `healthyApiBody`'s new peak-hours arm (the no-schedule default) keeping the app-mount specs and the e2e route interception honest.

- §13 login/logout endpoints (task U-041, spec §13, §15.1, §15.2, §14.6, §15.4; decision D-146): the plan gap D-125/D-126 recorded closes — `src/server/api/auth-routes.ts` registers `POST /api/auth/login` (public per D-121's `PUBLIC_AUTH_ROUTE_CONFIG` opt-out) and `POST /api/auth/logout` (session-bound, bodiless) inside the `/api` scope behind `ApiRoutesOptions.authRoutes` (the D-128 optional-options rule), composing the four landed §15.2 primitives with no rule re-derived. **Login** runs D-125's documented order — `delayBeforeAttempt` → sleep-if-delayed → `verifyPassword` → `recordAttempt` — inside a **per-source mutual-exclusion section** keyed by `request.ip` (socket-derived, header-unforgeable because `trustProxy` is never enabled): at most one attempt per source is in flight — a same-source newcomer is answered immediately with the same 401 under `details.reason: 'source-busy'` without being verified or recorded (no guess evaluated, no budget consumed, its own single credential-free `warn` the operator's flood signal), so the check-then-record race D-125 warned about is structurally impossible and neither a queue nor memory can grow with hostile traffic. **The slept delay is clamped to `MAX_LOGIN_DELAY_SLEEP_MS` (240 s)** — comfortably inside the construction's `HTTP_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT_MS` (300 s, D-109's socket-inactivity bound, whose own rule demands it exceed the longest legitimate silent handler; an unclamped sleep would be answered by a connection reset, not the slow answer §15.2 demands), pinned against the bound by a unit relation test — and the default sleep's timer is **unref'd**, so an in-flight delay never holds the event loop open past §5.6's bounded shutdown (`runServer` drains, never exits; pinned by a `hasRef()` probe). The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, REQUEST_CHANGES with 2 P1 / 2 P2 / 3 P3) forced exactly these: the first design's unbounded same-source queue and referenced timer are folded away in the same commit. The budget is **invisible on the wire** — delays, never rejections: an over-budget attempt (failed _or_ successful — the check precedes the verify, so a verified password still pays the delay its source earned) answers the same envelope an under-budget one would; no rate-limit code (D-125 retired it), no `Retry-After`. A wrong password — or a passwordless configuration, through D-122's totality guard — answers the **registry-default `UNAUTHENTICATED` 401** with `details.reason: 'invalid-credentials'`, the code the D-134 UI notice branch intercepts, with **exactly one credential-free `warn` per failed attempt** (`{ source, outcome: 'failure' }`; the marker password never in a body or log line). Success mints through `createSession` (D-123) and sets the cookie with D-126's attribute object — `Max-Age` mirroring the row's effective expiry, `Secure` over the request's transport facts — beside the 200 body `{ session: { expiresAt } }` (epoch-ms, the D-139 family rule). **Logout** composes `revokeSession` + the `clearSessionCookieAttributes` twin and answers `{ revoked }` idempotently; under `required` a cookieless or dead-cookie logout answers the guard's 401 before the handler. **D-126's refreshed-sighting open point is resolved as no-re-issue**: the §15.1 seam stays `(request) => boolean` — the wiring is D-124's predicted one-lambda as the named factory `createSessionAuthenticator({ repository, now })` — so valid sightings refresh the ROW while the COOKIE keeps its mint-time 7-day `Max-Age` (weekly re-login at most; the alignment-preserving alternative is the recorded successor path). The composition root (D-140) wires the factory as `auth.isAuthenticated` — closing the deliberately-unwired seam — beside the `authRoutes` block over the reloader-read live `passwordHash` thunk (a `passwd` run applies to the next login, §6.4), so a `required`-mode deployment authenticates from the first start. The D-144 OpenAPI artifacts updated mechanically as its consequences named: both operations in `scripts/openapi-document.mjs` (login public with the body-carrying mutations' 413/415 set, logout session-bound and bodiless), the `auth` tag, `docs/openapi.json` regenerated, the drift guard's `Required<ApiRoutesOptions>` boot wired. Evidence: `test/unit/auth-routes.spec.ts` (the authenticator arms and the D-126 transport resolution), `test/integration/auth-routes.spec.ts` (19 tests: exact cookie attributes on HTTP and proxied-HTTPS legs, digest-only storage with the whole-database raw-token scan, the registry-default 401 message and the warn record's exact key set with BOTH markers — the correct password and the attempted candidate — absent from every body and log line, every §13/§15.3 error arm incl. the construction-limit 413, the live-hash flip, the full §15.1 matrix, the no-re-issue pin, the fake-clock budget incl. the transport-safe clamp, window expiry, success reset, per-source isolation, and the exclusion proof), `test/integration/composition-auth.spec.ts` (the production-construction proof over temp config + SQLite).

- ECharts metric charts on the overview (task 175, spec §14.4, §13, §8.4; decision D-148): the `/` page gains one history chart per quota metric below the metric cards, rendered by Apache ECharts — a devDependency (`echarts` 6.1.0, mirroring `vue`/`vue-i18n`/`vue-router`: the shipped tarball carries the prebuilt bundle) imported **modularly** exactly as spec §3's table mandates (`echarts/core` + the `LineChart`/`GridComponent`/`TooltipComponent`/`LegendComponent`/`CanvasRenderer` pieces via one `use([...])` registration in `ui/src/charts/runtime.ts`, the only module that knows the library exists). **The renderer choice is D-127's**: canvas with `tooltip.renderMode: 'richText'` — ECharts' SVG renderer and HTML tooltips style through the `style=` attribute the CSP blocks, while the canvas painter and rich-text tooltip draw without DOM styling, so no policy amendment was needed. **One unit per value axis, by construction**: the pure option builder (`ui/src/charts/series.ts`) takes exactly one view mode and one unit and emits exactly one y-axis — there is no input shape that could mix units; the percent/absolute toggle (native radios, one group per card) renders only where the data itself carries both views, percentage-only metrics stay on their bounded 0–100 `%` axis with no absolute axis inferred, and the used/remaining pair distinguishes itself by legend text and solid-vs-dashed lines (never color alone). **The wire kinds all render**: raw points verbatim (invalid rows' nulls stay null, never zero), §12.2 gap empty points as all-null line breaks (`connectNulls: false`), and D-081 downsampling buckets as their `first`/`last` endpoints — real observed points, the honest minimal render for the common case (the default 7-day window at the default cadence exceeds the 1500-point budget and arrives bucketed; task 176 may elaborate the buckets into extrema bands on the same adapter). **The D-147 residual is closed**: a `truncated` range report whose clamp is a ≤ 60 s sliver (server-clock horizon vs browser-clock `from`) stays quiet; a real truncation names where the retained window starts, and a `rejected` range states that instead of an empty chart. **Theme integration is D-133's**: chart colors come only from `getComputedStyle` `--qm-*` token lookups — two dedicated tokens (`--qm-chart-used`, `--qm-chart-remaining`) join all three theme blocks — and the option computed reads `resolvedTheme`, so a mode flip re-reads the cascade and re-sets the option live; the x-axis is the numeric epoch-ms axis §14.4 prescribes (ECharts' time axis is local/UTC only) with `Intl.DateTimeFormat` labels in the configured display zone (`formatChartDate` in `ui/src/format.ts`; task 177 owns tick placement). The page's per-metric loads are D-147's hand-off executed (`MetricHistoryChart.vue` owns one `useApiCall` per metric card, bounds recomputed per dispatch; the refresh affordance bumps a token the cards watch — a failed refresh keeps the previous chart rendered beside its notice, D-135's rule, while an identity/range change clears the kept copy so one window never renders for another), every string lives under `overview.chart.*` in the catalog, each chart's `role="img"` accessible name carries the metric with its latest facts, and the charts region's grid floors at `min(100%, 24rem)` so §14.1's 360 px viewport never gains horizontal overflow (pinned by a narrow-viewport e2e cell). Evidence: `test/component/chart-series.spec.ts` (the pure adapter — kind mapping incl. the bucket first/last and 1-member dedup, availability and view-mode resolution incl. the percentage-only pin, the sliver threshold incl. its strict-excess boundary, and the option builder — single-yAxis unit segregation, the 0–100 percent axis, null gaps, window bounds, tooltip/axis formatters, token wiring, symbol density), `test/component/metric-history-chart.spec.ts` (mounted over the injected chart-runtime seam with the production token reader: dispatch grammar, toggle behavior, the states, failure + named retry, live theme restyle, dispose/recreate lifecycle), the updated `test/component/overview-view.spec.ts` (per-metric dispatch, the charts seam, the refresh +1-per-metric), and the extended `test/e2e/theme.spec.ts` chart-contract cell (the new tokens carry verbatim colors in every mode). ECharts itself never boots inside the test suite (zrender needs a real canvas 2d context; the injected seam keeps the option pipeline honest instead) — recorded as D-148's deliberate test boundary.
- Rolling-range controls on the overview (task 174, spec §14.4, §10, §13; decision D-147): the `/` page gains the five §14.4 history-range filters — 24 hours, 3 days, 7 days (default), 14 days, 30 days — at the seam D-137 reserved between the identity card and the metrics region. **The control is a radio group** (fieldset + legend, one shared `name`): native keyboard support (arrows move, Tab reaches the group) and every retention-disabled option carries its explanation as real text beside its label ("Exceeds the N-day history retention") — §14.1's never-color-alone rule met by the disabled state plus the sentence, never styling. **Retention-awareness** reads the settings view the page already loads (`app.historyRetentionDays`); an unknown retention (load pending or failed) disables nothing — an unknown fact asserts nothing, and the server's own D-139 range report stays the authority — while a selection that becomes disabled once retention arrives falls back to the §14.4 default (always selectable: §11.3's retention minimum equals its duration). **The boundary rule (D-147): no zone anchors a request.** The chosen range is passed to the server as D-139's grammar — ISO-8601 UTC `Z` boundaries recomputed on every dispatch (`to = now`, `from = now − duration`), D-068's exact absolute-duration arithmetic mirrored client-side (`ui/src/history-range.ts`, drift-pinned — union and table, both directions — against `src/util/rolling-ranges.ts`, the `API_MUTATION_HEADER` precedent: the vocabulary is UI-local, nothing from `src/**` crosses). The client gains the `history`/`events` families (`api.history.get`, `api.events.list`) with type-only schema imports, exactly as D-139's consequences recorded (`healthyApiBody` grows the two arms with them), and the page wires both range-keyed loads: the account-level §12.2 timeline keys on the validated target and the range, the metric series additionally on the first quota metric key (no snapshot ⇒ no metric vocabulary ⇒ no series request); both re-run on selection and range changes, render nothing yet (the charts are 175/176, which inherit the wired loads), and present failures through the shared notice with their own distinctly-named retries. A refresh beside the control re-runs the account's data loads with a fresh "now" — the affordance D-137 handed to this task, discharged. Evidence: `test/component/history-range.spec.ts` (the drift pins, the retention matrix incl. the equality boundary, the ISO bounds' exact spans across a DST transition), `test/component/history-range-controls.spec.ts` (the control matrix: structure, labels, unknown-retention, disabled × explanation, selection round-trip, inertness, theme-independence smoke incl. a no-hardcoded-color source scan), the extended `test/component/overview-view.spec.ts` (the D-137 seam placement, the default 7-day dispatch round-trip in D-139's grammar, the no-metric rule, the range-switch re-dispatch with disabled inertness, the late-retention fallback, the selection-switch re-dispatch, both failure arms with recoveries, the refresh re-anchoring), the client's per-family round-trips in `test/component/api-client.spec.ts`, and the retention-7 axe scan of the disabled surface (reason texts asserted, both themes) in `test/e2e/a11y.spec.ts`. The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, REQUEST_CHANGES with 1 P1 / 8 P3) was folded in before landing: the P1 — the a11y suite's route-interception cells 404'd the newly-wired events load — fixed by the standing healthy history/events bodies; six P3s entered (the exhaustive-`never` label arm, the fallback's polite live-region announcement, the healthy-per-route fixture catch-alls, the retention-7 e2e cell, the equality-cell wording in D-147, the load-count wording), and the two remaining trade-offs are recorded as D-147's known residuals (the equality-cell clock-skew truncation sliver, and the refresh's superseded-in-flight series reload under a changed metric vocabulary).
- Generated OpenAPI documentation (task 163, spec §13, §18; decision D-144): the published API description lives at `docs/openapi.json` (presented by [docs/api.md](docs/api.md)) and is **generated from the same TypeBox contracts the routes validate through** — every request/response schema in the document is the JSON projection of an exported `src/schemas/api/*` object, every error response is derived from the frozen code registry (`API_ERROR_DEFINITIONS`, D-113: code → status + default message, body = the closed `ApiErrorBodySchema` envelope), and the mutation-header name/method set and session-cookie name are imported from their owning modules (`API_MUTATION_HEADER_NAME`, `STATE_CHANGING_METHODS`, `SESSION_COOKIE_NAME`), so the document cannot drift from the wire contract at any of those seams. OpenAPI **3.1.0** with the draft-2020-12 dialect (TypeBox's native output; 3.1 schema objects are 2020-12, so projections embed verbatim). The generator is the `generate-schema.mjs` pattern: `scripts/openapi-document.mjs` (the builder — pure data over a literal route inventory holding the operation metadata fastify route options do not carry: method, path, summaries, per-route error codes, publicness) plus `scripts/generate-openapi.mjs` (the writer, `OPENAPI_DIR`-aware per the container uid invariant), run through `npm run openapi:generate` / `make openapi` (the `make schema` stream-back pattern); `info.version` tracks `package.json`, and `docs/release.md`'s checklist now names the post-bump regeneration the freshness arm demands. **The drift guard** `test/unit/openapi-published.spec.ts` pins the hand-maintained inventory against the real thing — it boots the production `apiRoutes` scope on a bare fastify instance with every route family wired (a `Required<ApiRoutesOptions>` satisfaction, so a future family left unwired fails typecheck) to registration-only throwing seams (no sockets, no SQLite — `onRoute` collects method/path/schema/config), and fails `make check` on: a stale artifact (committed ≠ builder), an undocumented route, a documented-but-missing route, a schema mismatch (the document's path/query/body/response schemas must equal the projections of the schemas the live routes declare — a re-wired route fails even when method and path still match), a non-exact registry error entry (description, status, envelope body), a missing construction-guaranteed base code (VALIDATION/INTERNAL/FORBIDDEN everywhere, UNAUTHENTICATED off the public route), a publicness mismatch (the live `PUBLIC_AUTH_ROUTE_CONFIG` set vs the document's `security: []` overrides), a mutation-header mismatch (§15.3's four methods exactly), or a missing root session-cookie requirement. The builder's own rejection arms (duplicate operationId, duplicate method+path, unregistered code, status collision) are pinned by `test/unit/openapi-document.spec.ts` over injected inventories, beside the publication rules (a defaulted property documents optional — the `useDefaults` wire truth — and the mutation-header placement). All four guard drift directions were proven on scratch mutations (a phantom path, a deleted path, a mutated body schema, a dropped security override — each failing with its arm's message), not committed. §13's auth routes are documented when they land (U-041): the guard's undocumented-route arm and the wiring pin force the inventory update in the same commit, which is the point. Packaging/publication of the reference in the npm package remains task 192's deliverable. The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, APPROVE with 5 P2 / 7 P3) was folded in before landing: ten findings fixed (the `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` code sets on the body-carrying mutations, the exact-description enforcement and base-set derivations, the future-family wiring pin, the defaulted-property rule, the release-checklist step, the root-security pins, the header-value `\\S` pattern, the imported method set, the duplicate-operation rejection, the builder spec), one P3 idea declined with reasons ($ref-deduplicating the error envelope — inline embedding is the deliberate, comparison-friendly shape, and duplication in a generated artifact costs nothing semantically).
- Cross-endpoint API security and contract integration suites (task 164, spec §13, §15, §17.1; decision D-145): four sweep specs + one shared harness close the gaps the thirteen per-family `api-*.spec.ts` suites and `composition.spec.ts` leave — every §13 endpoint proven TOGETHER through the **production composition** (`composeApplication`, D-140: real temp `config.json`, real migrated `node:sqlite` database, real `/api` scope with all guards; only the provider adapter doubled, via the no-network guard's standing exception), driven by `fastify.inject`. `test/helpers/composedApi.ts` is the harness (start/stop lifecycle with failure cleanup, the legitimate-client vs exact-headers request builders, the shared envelope/header/mask assertions, history seeding through the real write path, and a mirror of the composition's startup-secret logger registration). `api-sweep-contract.spec.ts` (16 tests) walks the §13 route table's happy paths (status codes, JSON transport, §15.5 headers, the constant key mask, the range family's report arms) and pins today's U-041 boundary — the unbuilt `/api/auth/*` endpoints answer the catch-all 404 envelope once the §15.3 mutation legs pass. `api-sweep-security.spec.ts` (21 tests) is the §15 matrix across endpoints: the UNAUTHENTICATED 401 on every protected endpoint (incl. the mutation routes) under `required` mode as it behaves today (the sessions-backed `isAuthenticated` is absent until U-041 — peak-hours had no §15.1 pin at all), §15.1's 403-with-instructions for proxied passwordless requests, every state-changing route rejecting the missing custom header / cross-origin Origin / hostile Sec-Fetch-Site / non-JSON content type, the Host allowlist on every endpoint (the public health route included — public is an auth exemption, not a Host one), the guard ORDER on a real mutation with no-state-change evidence, the §15.5 header set on the mutation successes and every error class (incl. 414), and the 64 KiB body limit on the real bodied routes (413 over the bound; a headerless oversized body answers the §15.3 verdict, never 413; the at-bound body is admitted by the transport and judged by the schema). `api-sweep-errors.spec.ts` (6 tests) proves every reachable error class (VALIDATION's query/body/range arms, NOT_FOUND's unknown-account and unmatched-route arms, the four FORBIDDEN reasons, UNAUTHENTICATED, 415/413/414) answers the closed D-113 envelope with a registry code, a non-empty message, and no marker or temp-path anywhere in the body. `api-sweep-secrets.spec.ts` (8 tests) is the §17.1 marker-secret scenario across the surface: three key markers on the shared lifecycle (file-stored, wire-created, history-account) plus a settings-PUT password, with stored-state positive controls (the file, the database rows — the password's control is the stored scrypt hash, the plaintext never persists); none on any echoing endpoint's body, outcome, or error envelope, none in any log line (positive-controlled sink), the key EMBEDDED in stored provider-sourced text (plan name, error message) asserted to arrive `[REDACTED]`, never dropped (§7.4); the §13 mask/boolean contract pinned positively; and two dedicated lifecycles for a runtime key rotation (entry-specific positive control) and an env-resolved key. Order independence is executable: shared lifecycles serve only read-only requests, every mutating test owns its lifecycle, and the suites pass under `--sequence.shuffle`. The suites deliberately leave per-route domain contracts, INTERNAL escalation via store doubles, the full §15.1 bind×mode cell matrix, guard byte-pins, and the raw-wire Host arms to the existing per-family suites, and record two exclusions (the accounts surface's `planName` is null by construction — no writer populates the row's column today; the harness mirrors the startup-secret registration because it must inject a sink-capturing logger) — D-145 records the boundaries; the spec names avoid the `test:time` filter vocabulary (D-097).

- §13 peak-hours endpoint (task 162, spec §9.3, §13, §11.3, §10; decision D-142): `GET /api/peak-hours?accountId&from&to` lands as `src/server/api/peak-hours.ts` inside the `/api` scope behind the D-128 optional-options rule (`ApiRoutesOptions.peakHours` — the store seam, the repository, the §6.4-live providers-section reader, the §6.4-live retention reader, the clock; absent, the path answers the scope catch-all's 404), with the contracts in `src/schemas/api/peak-hours.ts` — completing §13's route table. The route is pure composition over the landed services: task 114/115's `calculatePeakHoursIntervals` owns the interval math, the §9.3 DST policy, and the D-073 partition (never re-derived), task 123's `boundHistoryRange` owns the retention bound, and task 161's `parseRequestedRange`/`rangeReportOf` own the §13 range parsing and report — this route is §13's third named consumer of both, reused verbatim. **The response** carries the resolved `providerId` (the configured entry wins; a storage-only archived account reads it from its row — the D-128 both-halves verdict), the schedule facts, the retention range report, and the `kind`-discriminated `peak`/`off-peak` intervals partitioning the effective range chronologically — exact epoch-ms boundaries, the window's id/name/multiplier, and D-073's `yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm` local times of the emitted boundaries in the schedule zone (§9.3's output requirement; the browser renders them verbatim and never calculates windows independently). **The timestamp rule D-139 staked is inherited and D-073's interim `toUtcIso` serialization note is superseded** (D-142): ISO 8601 UTC requests with a mandatory explicit offset or `Z`, epoch-ms responses — one format per direction; `invalid-timestamp`+`param`, `inverted-range`, and NOT_FOUND ride the family's D-113-frozen classes. **Retention is inherited** (§13 names peak-hours in its range/retention sentence): the 200-with-report — intervals are computed over the effective range only, `rejected` answers empty intervals with the horizon named, and the schedule facts still echo in that arm (they are configuration, not range output). **The no-schedule arm** (§9.2, never an invented schedule): a provider without a `providers.<id>` entry answers `timezone: null`/`offPeakMultiplier: null` with empty intervals — the same "not configured" fact `/api/providers` states; a configured schedule with empty windows is NOT that arm (it computes the whole range as one off-peak interval, §9.2's own unverified-schedule shape; the UI's message reads `/api/providers`, which serves `windows`). **The span cap**: retention never clamps `to` (D-082), so a far-future end can exceed the calculator's `MAX_QUERY_SPAN_DAYS`; the route maps that refusal to VALIDATION/`range-too-wide` with `maxDays`, the bound single-sited in the service — and, the review round's P2 (deepseek-v4.1-flash, APPROVE with 1 P2 / 1 P3, both folded in), a truthful pre-check rejects the epoch-ceiling neighborhood (±8.64e15 ms minus a two-day headroom covering the zone offset, the guard day, and the `24:00` next-midnight resolution) with `range-unsupported`, so a narrow range whose instants no zone conversion can render is never misreported as too wide — pinned by test at `+275760-09-12` itself. The composition passes the options (D-140's `HttpServerOptions.routes` seam — the wiring the composition root landed for), so the endpoint answers in the production construction. Done-condition evidence ("server-generated intervals match the configured timezone/DST tests"): `test/unit/api-peak-hours-view.spec.ts` wraps the task-114/115 DST-matrix timelines (New York's 23/25-hour days, Havana's midnight gap with the shifted-boundary local times, Lord Howe's 30-minute gap, Chatham's quarter-hour gap, Kolkata's half-hour offset, the §9.1 production week) in the served envelope, validates them through the compiled response schema, and deep-equals epochs and local times against the hand-derived expectations; `test/unit/api-peak-hours-schema.spec.ts` pins the closed contracts, the interval union, the query grammar, and the no-drift rows (the range report is history's own schema object; the calculator's output validates against the interval schemas); `test/integration/api-peak-hours.spec.ts` (18 tests) runs the route through the real `/api` scope over migrated disposable SQLite — the §9.1 week with exact epochs/local times, DST days on the wire, retention truncation/rejection/live-reads, both schedule arms, the storage-only account, every error class, the degenerate range, and the no-half-wired 404; `test/integration/composition.spec.ts` proves the production construction answers (the no-schedule arm in the family walk, the configured schedule's intervals in a dedicated test). The three peak-hours-named specs join the §17.1 time-suite manifest (`test:time`'s substring filter selects them), so CI runs them under all nine `TZ` cells — verified green under `Asia/Kathmandu`.
- Composition root and production route wiring (task U-040, spec §6.2–§6.4, §13, §7.2, §5.6; decision D-140): the empty `buildComponents()` of task 060 now delegates to the new `src/server/composition.ts` — `composeApplication()` resolves the §6.1 directories, enforces the §6.3 permissions eagerly (before the secret-bearing file is ever read), loads through `loadConfigWithEnv` (§6.2's refuse-to-start with the pipeline's value-free problems), and assembles the §5.6 component list `[single-instance-lock, storage, http-listener]` with **every landed API route family wired**: the previously-404 paths (`/api/accounts*`, `/api/status`, `/api/quota`, `/api/providers`, `/api/settings`, `/api/history`, `/api/events`, the accounts test) now answer through the real construction (`HttpServerOptions.routes`, the composition-root seam beside D-121's auth projection — absent members keep the no-half-wired rule for the bare construction and its 404 pins). The two consumer-owned seams land in `src/server/config-stores.ts` over one **serialized config-file owner**: every mutation of either store is a read-modify-write over the RAW parsed file JSON — never the environment-merged effective configuration, whose verbatim persistence would bake `QUOTAMETER_*` values into `config.json` (D-130's write-side obligation) — validated through `parseConfigWithEnv` before any write, written atomically (`writeConfigFile`), and applied through §6.4's reload so the last valid configuration stays effective when anything refuses (§6.2); the settings half persists only the mutable scalars the request ATTEMPTED (the payload's new `attempted` set — a change validated against an older snapshot cannot revert a field a concurrent request already applied), never environment-owned fields, never the sections a PUT cannot own, hashes a supplied `pendingPassword` itself, and revokes every session once the write and its reload both succeeded (§15.2/D-124). **The config-sync** (D-128's pre-sync gap) mirrors configured accounts into storage rows at storage start and after every accounts-changing reload (missing rows inserted with the shared clock's stamp, label drift copied into the row's mirror, archived rows untouched per D-061). **The §7.2 suspension holder** (`src/services/account-suspensions.ts`, the D-064 state) feeds `suspensionOf` for the status rows and BOTH release conditions: the composition registers each adapter wrapped by `observingManualTests()` (`src/server/providers.ts`) — release-on-request before the attempt, re-entry with a fresh 60-minute window on a `credentials-rejected` rejection — so a refused manual test shows `Auth rejected` on `/api/status` and a **successful manual test lifts it**, and the composition-wrapped reload reports every configured account's key identity on accounts changes, so a key rotation releases an episode without a manual test. The repository the routes receive is a lazy facade over the storage component's start-time open (the data directory exists only after the lock's start, D-017 — storage opens and migrates inside its own component; a failed checkpoint on stop stays retryable). **A configured account may now be keyless** (D-140 (8), the review round's P1): the "exactly one secret source" cross-field rule is relaxed to "at most one", because §5.3 step 3's skip path lands in the web UI and the landed D-128 create contract makes `apiKey` optional — through the production store the strict rule turned a keyless create into a 500 (both landed accounts suites ran against doubles and never saw it); the keyless configured account displays §7.3's `Not configured`. Deliberately unwired, recorded in D-140: SIGHUP → reload (the D-024 readiness seam), the sessions-backed `isAuthenticated` (the login endpoints are the D-125/D-126 plan gap), and the polling engine (its task owns polled-cycle dispatch; this holder stays the composition's seam). Done-condition evidence: `test/integration/composition.spec.ts` through the production construction over temp config + SQLite (only the provider adapter doubled) — the previously-404 families answering, the config-sync across two lifecycles, create/update/archive and settings PUTs persisting through the real file (marker secrets present in the file, absent from every response body and log line — env-resolved keys included, environment-owned fields never baked, the scrypt hash stored, sessions revoked), the keyless §5.3 skip path answering 201 and reading `not-configured`, the suspension shown `auth-rejected`, lifted by a successful test and by key rotation, and the §6.2 refusals — beside the store-level matrix in `test/integration/config-stores.spec.ts` (absent-file base, malformed/non-object refusal, duplicate and unknown ids, refuse-before-write with a byte-identical file, reload-rejection propagation, `apiKeyEnv` resolution arms, concurrent-mutation serialization, the attempted-only and env-skipping settings writes), the unit pins `test/unit/account-suspensions.spec.ts` and `test/unit/server-providers.spec.ts`, the updated `server-main.spec.ts` bootstrap pins (the §5.6 names/order, the no-configuration refusal, the default-assembly run over a reserved port), and the config-invariant re-pins in `test/unit/config.spec.ts` / `test/unit/config-validation.spec.ts`.
- Init-side password collection (task U-042, spec §5.3 step 4, §15.2, §15.1; decision D-141): `quotameter init` now offers the optional web password on fresh runs in both modes, completing §15.2's "set the password via `init`" promise that D-101/CLI README had deferred to the scrypt tasks. **Interactive** (`src/cli/init-password.ts`): one muted question pair after the §5.3 step-3 questions and before the account's connection test — loopback binds (the §15.1 default) ask optionally (the exactly-empty first answer skips; completing without a password keeps `auth: { mode: 'auto' }`, no hash), non-loopback binds ask required-shaped per §5.3 step 4 (an empty answer is the §15.2 length complaint; three rejected pairs cancel the run). The pair mechanics are `passwd`'s own `askNewPassword` (D-118) behind additive options (a custom first prompt, the skip) plus a `skipped` outcome — one prompt-flow implementation for both password setters, `passwd`'s no-options call shape unchanged (and type-narrowed through an overload). **Non-interactive**: the new `--password-env <variable>` value option (in `INIT_VALUE_OPTIONS` and the dispatch table) mirrors `--api-key-env` exactly — environment by name, never a `--password` value flag (§15.4's process-listing rule), the same name checks (shape pattern, no `QUOTAMETER_` prefix, own-property set, blank value = unset per D-011), the §15.2 minimum checked on the raw untrimmed value, and no problem ever containing the argument or the value. **Storage**: the handler hashes through the new `InitDeps.hash` seam (production `hashPassword`, D-118's encoding) and submits only the opaque hash through `validateSettings` — `passwd`'s exact convention (the `pendingPassword` channel stays with its future UI consumer); the plaintext exists only between the prompt/env read and the hashing call, never inside `InitAnswers`, any output line, or the file. When the data directory still holds a database (a configuration deleted or re-pointed while the database survived), storing the password **revokes its sessions first** in `passwd`'s fail-secure order (review round P1: "fresh" is fresh about the configuration file only — revoke before the write, abort on failure, report what was revoked). The fresh summary gains a state-only `Password` row (`set (only a scrypt hash is stored)` / `none`); the §5.3 step-4 note renders only when no password was set. **Re-runs collect no password** (D-101's four settings questions stay four): changing a password is `passwd`'s contract — its fail-secure session-revocation ordering — and a pending re-run keeps its passwd remedy; the D-101 pending write remains reachable through `--yes` without `--password-env` (the init-now, passwd-later journey). Done-condition evidence: `test/unit/cli-init-password.spec.ts` (the mode matrix over the shared flow, the exact 11/12-character boundary, the untrimmed value pin), the extended `test/unit/cli-init.spec.ts` (hash-seam hand-off, skip → `auto`/no-hash, mismatch restart, required-abort, EOF cancel, hash failure, the revocation none/revoked/failed arms, re-runs asking nothing), `test/unit/cli-init-yes.spec.ts` (`--password-env` tables incl. the no-echo, boundary, and untrimmed pins, plus the handler wiring), `test/unit/cli-passwd.spec.ts` (the additive options), `test/unit/cli-init-persist.spec.ts` (hash assembly), and the real-launcher suites — piped password runs with marker secrets asserted absent from stdout, stderr, and the written file (whose `auth.passwordHash` is `scrypt$`-shaped; the loopback skip leaves `auth: { mode: 'auto' }`), the PTY typed/paste runs (the typed password never echoes, the file carries only the hash), the `--yes` happy/unset/too-short/re-run/stray-flag routes, and the surviving-database revocation over a real migrated SQLite database with seeded sessions.

- §13 history and events endpoints (task 161, spec §12, §13, §11.3, §10; decision D-139): the two range-bearing routes land as one plugin inside the `/api` scope behind the D-128 optional-options rule — `GET /api/history?accountId&metric&from&to&maxPoints` (the retention-aware metric series with §13 downsampling and §12.2 gap empty points) and `GET /api/events?accountId&from&to&metric` (the unified §12.2 timeline: resets, gaps, failed cycles, stopped-service regions as one time-ordered kind-discriminated array). **The timestamp reconciliation D-130 left open is closed (D-139):** the request `from`/`to` are ISO 8601 UTC strings with a mandatory explicit offset or `Z` (parsed through the landed `epochMsFromIso` — offset-less strings answer VALIDATION/`invalid-timestamp` with the offending parameter, `from > to` answers `inverted-range`), while every response timestamp stays the epoch-ms integer of the landed vocabulary — requested and effective ranges echo as epoch-ms, one format per direction. **Retention reporting:** the §13 "reject or explicitly truncate … and state that in the response" arm is closed as the 200-with-report — `range.outcome: 'truncated'` serves the clamped bounds with the horizon named, `'rejected'` answers the empty series with the horizon and null effective bounds, both arms one response contract; the window is read per request (`historyRetentionDays`, §6.4-live). **The history wire:** raw points (`kind: 'point'`, §8.4 values with honest nulls on invalid rows), overfull series as buckets (`kind: 'bucket'` — D-081's min/max/first/last preservation with the stored reset instants and detected gaps fed as boundary data, so markers and gap edges survive aggregation by the landed rule), and §12.2's explicit empty points (`kind: 'empty'`) at gap midpoints; `unit`/`limitType` ride from the newest in-range row (null when empty — an unknown metric key is an empty series, never an error: the vocabulary is adapter-owned), and the response carries the effective resolution (`resolutionMs`, the widest allocated slice width — fractional by construction), `downsampled`, and `sourcePointCount`. **The events wire:** reset items serve the D-046/D-074 detection evidence verbatim, gap items carry D-075's fields, failed cycles carry task 120's indicator evidence with the message passed the same §7.4 exact-occurrence secret redaction as `/api/status`'s `lastError` (a marker in a stored error renders `[REDACTED]`), and stopped regions carry task 120's clamped edges; the optional `metric` filter narrows only the per-metric kinds. **The runs-evidence read:** one margin-widened raw facade read (`EVIDENCE_MARGIN_MS` = the widest §12.2 silence judgment plus one worst-case cycle span) supplies the run-stamped interval timeline (D-069's per-run `interval_seconds`, collapsed to changes) that the gap detector judges by, and the bounding records that anchor task 120's range-edge silences — and when that read finds nothing (the range sits inside an outage longer than the margin), two bounded probes recover the nearest bounding runs wherever they are, so the full-range stopped region still renders; pre-horizon rows are evidence only, never served. **The review round's P1 folds (kimi/k3.1-pro):** the downsampler aggregates the VALID points only, with invalid rows re-inserted as their own point entries — an invalid row inside a gap used to glue the gap's edge points into one segment and defeat §13's must-not-be-lost boundary — and the empty-point merge flushes by the gap's midpoint (timestamp-ordered even when an in-gap entry passes the midpoint, guarded for the degenerate 1 ms gap). Tests: `test/unit/api-history-schema.spec.ts` (closed contracts, kind unions, query grammar, no-drift rows against the downsampler's budget constants, task 123's outcome vocabulary, and the status surface's outcome list), `test/unit/api-history-view.spec.ts` (parsing, timeline, views, merge incl. the midpoint ordering and the invalid re-insertion, report, redaction, ordering — with explicit timeouts on the heavy seeds per D-094), `test/integration/api-history.spec.ts` and `test/integration/api-events.spec.ts` (end-to-end through the real `/api` scope over migrated disposable SQLite, seeded through the live write path — both §12.1 reset conditions and the false-positive avoidance, gap threshold/cadence/invalid-point exclusion, downsampling boundary preservation and extrema equality incl. the invalid-row-inside-a-gap case, the §13 default budget of 1500, retention truncation/rejection/live-read, the full-outage stopped region beyond the margin, the archived-storage-only readability pin, error classes, the §15.1 seam, no-half-wired 404s, and the §15.4 marker discipline).

- Empty and initial states (task 173, spec §14.3, §14.6; decision D-138): a fresh install never renders a blank or broken page — every state the overview can open in renders guidance. **The page-level no-accounts state** upgrades from 172's one-line placeholder to a titled guidance block ("No accounts yet" + the skip-the-key hint) whose `RouterLink` to `/accounts` is the actionable next step (axe-scanned in the real browser through Playwright route interception in `test/e2e/a11y.spec.ts` — the preview server has no API, so the fresh-install bodies are served by `page.route`; the technique is recorded for 174+ reuse). **The account-level attention notice** (`ui/src/components/AccountAttentionNotice.vue` + the pure classifier `ui/src/account-attention.ts`): when a fact keeps collection from running, the notice below the identity card says why and what to do — precedence mirrors §7.3's own derivation order with the UI lifecycle fact outermost (`archived` → `disabled` → keyless), so the notice can never contradict the status badge rendered from the same wire facts; archived explains the terminal lifecycle (polling stopped, history retained, manage/purge on Accounts), disabled links to re-enabling, and the keyless branches read the two wire halves for what each is worth (the review round's P1): `hasKey` on the accounts surface is **declaration-level** (a source is configured, §6.3's exactly-one rule), so the reachable `Not configured` shape — a declared-but-unresolved `apiKeyEnv` key, `hasKey: true` on the wire — is recognized through the §7.3 status row's `not-configured` and gets the server-side remedy (set the variable and restart, deliberately **no link**: §6.3 makes environment keys read-only through the API, and nothing on the linked page fixes it); a status row that is absent vouches for nothing, so no keyless notice renders beside it; the schema-total sourceless fallback (`hasKey: false` on an active account — no current composition produces it) links to the replace-key action. **The no-snapshot wording** becomes attention-aware: under a blocker, 172's "values appear after the first successful poll" would promise a poll that never runs, so the metrics region states "collection is not running" instead and the actionable advice lives once, in the notice; a healthy pre-first-poll account keeps the waiting sentence beside its `Collecting` badge and `Never` line. The empty-state wording harmonizes with the accounts page's own empty state (task 170) while keys stay namespace-distinct (D-132). Evidence: `test/component/overview-view.spec.ts` gains the fresh-install matrix (fresh init without accounts; the declared-but-unresolved environment key incl. the absent-status-row no-claim pin; the sourceless-fallback keyless shape; disabled; archived without and with retained history; healthy pre-first-poll incl. the pre-sync `createdAt: null` shape; the registry-empty degradation cell) — each cell pins BOTH halves of the done-condition, its surface renders AND no `api-error` notice does — plus the initial-load and quota-pending lines (never before pinned), the classifier's truth table, and the §7.3 precedence pin; the e2e scans axe-check both guidance surfaces in both themes (route interception serving the fresh-install bodies, including the real registered provider) and prove the CTA a live SPA link to `/accounts`.
