Loading stays 172's shape (the initial-load and quota-loading lines, `Unknown` status slots) — pending already distinct from error/empty under D-134's semantics.

- Account overview cards (task 172, spec §14.3, §14.6, §8.4, §7.2; decision D-137): the `/` page becomes the §14.3 surface — the selected account's identity card (provider name, account label, plan with an explicit `Unknown` when absent, the §7.3 status badge, §7.2's last successful collection and last error with `Never`/`None` for their absences) plus one card per metric of its last-valid quota row set (D-077: each card shows its own collection stamp, because rows of one account may come from different snapshots). **The label resolution (D-130's recorded loose end)**: §14.3's per-metric "label" is §8.4 adapter-owned data, so `QuotaProvider` gains an optional `metricCatalog` (`{metricKey, label}` pairs; validated at `register` when present), the Z.ai adapter exports `ZAI_METRIC_CATALOG` single-sourced from its recognition table, and `GET /api/providers` serializes each adapter's catalog as a `metrics` array (always an array, empty when none declared) — the UI resolves labels by `(providerId, metricKey)` with the raw metric key as the documented fallback, so a new provider arrives with its labels and no UI rewrite (§8.1). **Honesty (the done-condition)**: each of used/remaining/limit renders its absolute value with an explicit unit, else its percentage (`42%` — §14.3's "or percentages"), else an explicit `Unknown` — never zero; a genuine zero renders as `0 credits`, pinned against the null case with hostile zero-plausible fixtures (`used: null` beside `remaining: 0`). Percent-only metrics stay percentages (no inferred token counts), invalid metrics render their `invalidReason` with no value lines (§8.4), an absent reset omits the line ("if available"), and every time renders absolute in the configured display time zone (from `/api/settings`; a failed load degrades to the browser zone beside its notice) plus relative, computed at data arrival. **The page**: five loads (accounts, providers, status, settings, and the selection-keyed quota re-run on every switch), the shared selection store with its D-135 reconciliation rule and a second selector, clear-on-switch (one account's values never render as another's), per-load failure notices with retries and last-good copies — never a fake empty state. Statuses render as text + tone + CSS-drawn shape (§14.1: never color alone) over new `--qm-color-ok*`/`--qm-color-warn*` tokens in all three theme blocks; times and numbers format through the new pure `ui/src/format.ts` (`Intl`-based, the 174+ charts reuse it). Evidence: `test/component/overview-view.spec.ts` (the §8.4 shape matrix, the eight-status badge table, label catalog + key fallback, selection/reconciliation/switch pins, per-load failure surfaces), `test/component/format.spec.ts`, the server-side rows (registry catalog validation, providers contract + serialization with and without a catalog, the adapter's catalog≡identities pin), the shell specs updated for the real page, and e2e: the smoke overview assertion becomes the honest no-API degradation, a per-mode axe scan of `/` joins the a11y suite, and the csp-shell policy probe moves to `/login` (the overview is now API-loading; that construction wires no data routes — the D-135 `/accounts` precedent, restoring `/` is the composition task's seam). The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, 1 P1 / 2 P2 / 9 P3) folded in before landing: an absent status row (load in flight or failed) renders its slots as `Unknown` — "Never"/"None" state facts only a loaded row vouches for (P1); the quota target is list-validated (a stale stored id dispatches nothing, no flashing 404) and a zero-metric snapshot is told apart from no snapshot (P2); plus the strengthened status table, the stale-selection and mixed-fallback matrix pins, registry padding/order hardening, the empty-error-message tail, the relative-time year bucket, and distinct retry names.

- Masked write-only secret fields, closed as a verify-and-close audit (task 171, spec §6.3, §14.2, §15.4; decision D-136): no implementation gap — the accounts/settings/status surfaces, the typed client, and the task-170 UI affordances all conform — but the done-condition's "UI/API tests prove the key is never returned to the browser" had an unpinned composition seam: the integration marker suites never involved a UI-side consumer, and the component fixtures were key-free by construction. The closing artifact is `test/integration/accounts-real-surface.spec.ts`: the real API client (the object the accounts page installs) runs a full §14.2 management session plus one refused create against the real route stack — serializers, §15.3 guards, the §13 error handler, migrated `node:sqlite` storage, provider registry — through a fetch-seam bridge that captures every exchange; marker secrets ride the write path (the store double holds the exact value at create time and after rotation, and the provider double sees the current key) while no captured response body, request URL, client-resolved value (successes and the thrown error's payload alike), or log record (non-empty by the refused exchange) contains them, the markers leave the browser only through the create POST and replace-key PATCH bodies, and the constant mask `********` rides both wire and parsed display state. `accounts-view.spec.ts` gains the refused-submit clearing pins (the write-only field is pinned emptied after both a rejected create and a refused replace-key). The three-link proof map (wire, parsed state, rendered DOM) is documented in `ui/README.md`, with a pointer from `src/server/README.md`; D-136 also records the pattern boundary (the component project's happy-dom environment container cannot host the server's `node:*` module graph, so the bridge lives in the integration project).
- Account selector and account management UI (task 170, spec §14.2, §8.2, §5.3; decision D-135): the `/accounts` page becomes the real management surface over the §13 accounts API (159/D-128) — every §14.2 verb works through the documented client (`useApi()` + one `useApiCall` per request, D-134), with the destructive purge gated behind a typed confirmation. **The page architecture** (`ui/src/views/AccountsView.vue`): the two loads (accounts list, available providers), a labeled `<select>` account selector whose options carry the label plus a text `(archived)` marker (never color alone), the selected account's `AccountManageCard`, and `AccountAddForm`; every successful mutation reloads the list once — the server's list is the single source of truth and the reload is what keeps the selection coherent. **The card** (`ui/src/components/AccountManageCard.vue`) shows the identity facts (provider display name from `/api/providers`, active/archived, polling on/off, key state — the §13 mask verbatim, "not set", or the environment read-only notice) and one action group per verb, each with its own scoped `ApiErrorNotice`: connection test (`POST :id/test`, the outcome sentence plus the provider's sanitized §13 `message` rendered immediately in a `role="status"` region — a schema-validated success body, inside D-134's trusted-cast boundary; the button disables without a key, the `no-key-configured` refusal it would earn), rename (inline form prefilled with the current label — the label is not a secret — `PATCH {label}` alone), polling enable/disable (`PATCH {enabled}` alone), replace key (`type="password"` + `autocomplete="new-password"`, never prefilled, never echoed, cleared after every attempt, `PATCH {apiKey}` alone; not offered for environment-sourced keys — §6.3's read-only rule the facts row states instead), archive (a two-step inline confirm whose body states the terminal semantics, D-061; `DELETE` with **no** query at all — the `purgeHistory` flag is absent), and purge (the §8.2 separate explicit action: the account's exact label must be typed — the gate trims both sides, so a padded label can never make an account unpurgeable from the UI, and labels this UI creates are sent trimmed — the confirm button disabled until the match, and only then `DELETE ?purgeHistory=true`). Confirm panels are inline, not modal dialogs — keyboard-reachable by construction, with focus moved to the safe control on open (the cancel button for archive, the typing gate for purge), and a panel's cancel `reset()`s its call so a previous attempt's failure notice cannot re-announce itself on re-open (`useApiCall` gains the dismissal seam `reset()` — clear data/error/pending and supersede any in-flight run). §8.2's terminal semantics shape the card: an archived account renders its rename/polling/replace-key/archive controls gone and the test button disabled (the server refuses `account-archived` on every mutation) with only the purge available. A successful key replacement clears the previous connection-test verdict — it described the credential that no longer exists. **The load-failure rule**: a failed load is never a fake empty state — the view keeps the last successful list (a failed reload leaves the previous surface beside the notice and a catalogued retry; a failed initial load renders neither selector nor empty state, just the notice and the retry), and the add form's no-providers sentence renders only for a successfully empty providers list. **The add form** (`ui/src/components/AccountAddForm.vue`): provider select from `/api/providers`, label, and the optional §5.3 key (write-only; omitted from the POST body entirely when empty); blank label/blank-key guards refuse client-side with field errors and no request; no `id` field — the server generates (D-128). **The selection store** (`ui/src/account-selection.ts`): the cross-page selected account as a factory+plugin+inject composable (the D-131…D-134 shape, not Pinia — §3 keeps deps minimal), persisted best-effort under the `localStorage` key `quotameter.selected-account` (absent/empty reads as null); the store knows nothing but the id — consumers reconcile against their own list data (keep the stored id while listed, else the first account, else null — the accounts page re-runs it on every completed load, and it is the documented seam task 172's overview consumes through `useAccountSelection()`). All strings live under `accounts.*` in the catalog in the same commit (the placeholder `accounts.status` dies with the page's placeholder); no new design tokens — the danger pair from 169 covers the destructive affordances, and every state pairs text with color. Evidence: `test/component/account-selection.spec.ts` (persistence round-trip, null/empty/throwing reads, best-effort writes, plugin seam, default-storage wiring) and `test/component/accounts-view.spec.ts` (30 tests over a miniature accounts backend on the injectable fetch seam — per-action wire pins: POST body with/without key, PATCH bodies exactly one field each, the bare archive DELETE URL vs `?purgeHistory=true` **only** after the typed match, cancel paths dispatching nothing, focus landing on the safe controls, reconciliation on load/switch/purge/empty, the environment-key and archived affordance matrices (incl. a purge-from-archived wire pin), envelope/network failures through the shared notice, the failed-load rules (no fake empty state, the retry re-dispatching the load, a failed reload keeping the last good list, a failed initial load leaving stored selection untouched), the padded-label purge regression, the cancel/re-open no-stale-notice pin, and the write-only discipline — masked inputs, empty first paint, no key material in the rendered DOM); `test/component/account-selection.spec.ts` (persistence round-trip, null/empty/throwing reads, best-effort writes, plugin seam, default-storage wiring); the `reset()` seam pinned in `test/component/api-error-wiring.spec.ts` (clears state without a dispatch, supersedes an in-flight run); the shell specs updated for the real page (the shared per-route healthy-body helper in `test/helpers/api.ts`, the accounts empty-state pin in `app.spec.ts`, the `accounts.status` catalog pin retired); e2e: the new per-theme axe scan of `/accounts` (its honest no-API degradation surface under `vite preview`), and the csp-shell deep link moved to `/settings` — task 170 made `/accounts` an API-loading page and that construction wires no accounts routes, so the §13 scope's honest 404 envelopes are console noise unrelated to the policy (restoring `/accounts` there is the composition task's documented seam).
- API client and error presentation (task 169, spec §13, §14; decision D-134): the UI's typed data layer over the same-origin §13 API, shared by every page. `ui/src/api/client.ts` (`createApiClient`, pure TypeScript) exposes one method per landed endpoint family (`health`, `status`, `quota`, `providers`, `accounts`, `settings`) with request/response types taken from the server's own TypeBox contracts through type-only imports — zero runtime coupling, nothing schema-shaped enters the browser bundle. Every state-changing method attaches §15.3's `X-Quotameter-Request` header (UI-local constant pinned against the server's by test) and types its body `application/json` exactly when it sends one (the body-bound content-type leg, D-117); every call runs under a 15 s deadline with optional caller cancellation, the two abort sources composed manually so a deliberate `aborted` stays distinguishable from the client's own `timeout`. All failures collapse onto one `ApiClientError` class discriminated by a five-kind taxonomy — `network` / `timeout` / `aborted` / `unexpected` (a body that is not the §13 envelope: an HTML proxy page, invalid JSON, a foreign JSON error shape) / `rejected` (the envelope, with `code`, `status`, and the payload); the deadline and the caller's signal are composed manually onto one AbortController and stay armed through the headers phase and the body read alike (a stalled body is bounded, a mid-body cancellation honored — the review round's P1). The presentation contract: only catalog sentences render — `ui/src/components/ApiErrorNotice.vue` (an inline `role="alert"` region, `api-error` testid) maps kind and envelope code onto `errors.*` catalog keys with a fallback for unknown codes, while the envelope's `serverMessage`/`serverDetails` never reach the DOM (unvouched provenance; a hostile-envelope marker test pins the leak gate) and `Error.message` stays a fixed per-kind sentence. The Vue seam follows the factory+plugin pattern (D-131/D-132/D-133): `createApiClientPlugin()`/`useApi()` provide/inject the client, `useApiCall()` tracks `data`/`error`/`pending` per request (rethrowing non-taxonomy errors — code bugs stay loud; a deliberate `aborted` cancellation and a superseded run both resolve quietly without touching error state), and the composition root installs it beside the router, i18n, and theme. The four placeholder views carry the canonical wiring against the public health probe — the done-condition: errors handled consistently across pages through the one surface — until their content tasks (170+) replace the probe with real calls; the `UNAUTHENTICATED` branch is the documented seam where task 180's login redirect hooks in. A `--qm-color-danger` token pair joins both theme blocks for the notice. Evidence: `test/component/api-client.spec.ts` (per-family success round-trips, the mutation-header matrix bodied and bodiless, envelope/non-envelope/network/abort/timeout taxonomy incl. the stalled-body deadline, the mid-body caller abort, the signal passed into fetch, the already-aborted short-circuit, the timer/listener cleanup pin, and the hostile-shape envelope-recognizer table, query serialization incl. a deliberate `purgeHistory=false`), `test/component/api-error-notice.spec.ts` (per-kind and per-code catalog sentences with the registry↔catalog drift pin, unknown-code fallback, the hostile-envelope and transport-error marker gates), `test/component/api-error-wiring.spec.ts` (the shell at all four routes renders the shared notice on failure and none on success, the plugin seam, the `useApiCall` state machine incl. the aborted-is-not-an-error policy and the superseded-run pins), and the updated shell spec; e2e (smoke, theme, a11y, csp-shell) green over the rebuilt bundle.
- Theme selection: `system`/`light`/`dark` with browser-storage persistence (task 168, spec §14.1; decision D-133): the UI's three §14.1 modes are live-switchable from a labeled select in the shell header and persist across reloads. The mechanism is a `data-theme` attribute on the document element that `ui/src/assets/base.css` maps onto the `--qm-*` tokens — attribute absent means `system` (the `prefers-color-scheme` block, guarded by `:not([data-theme])`, governs), `light`/`dark` set it and outspecify the media block, so an explicit light overrides an OS dark preference; flipping the attribute re-cascades the tokens with no reload and no inline style (D-127 stays green, csp-shell re-verified over the rebuilt bundle), and `color-scheme` rides the same blocks so UA widgets follow the mode. The new `ui/src/theme.ts` owns the state: the `createAppTheme({storage?, media?})` Vue plugin (installed in the composition root beside the router and i18n factories; injectable seams, the D-131/D-132 pattern), the `localStorage` key **`quotameter.theme`** (values `system`/`light`/`dark`; absent or invalid reads as `system` silently), best-effort persistence (a storage that throws on access or write degrades to a session-scoped theme, never a broken shell), and `resolvedTheme` — the computed the 175/176 charts later subscribe to, reading colors only through `getComputedStyle` token lookups (the tokens are the single color source; `light-dark()` was rejected precisely because it would hand charts an unresolved wrapper). The control's four strings live under `app.theme.*` in the catalog (D-132 discipline). Evidence: `test/component/theme.spec.ts` (default system, valid/invalid/throwing storage reads, write round-trip, per-mode attribute application incl. system-removal, live `resolvedTheme` flips through the media seam, explicit-mode override of OS flips, best-effort persistence, disposal on unmount, `isTheme` table, missing-plugin throw), the shell spec's control tests (options, default, live attribute flip through the real select), and `test/e2e/theme.spec.ts` (computed backgrounds flip live with token pins and a no-reload sentinel, the raw custom-property reads that pin the chart contract — a verbatim color per mode, `light-dark()` regression and dark-block drift included — explicit light over OS dark, system follows emulated OS flips, the documented storage key read positively, reload persistence, invalid stored value → system with no write-back) beside the per-mode a11y scans now in `test/e2e/a11y.spec.ts` — explicit light, explicit dark (each with the `data-theme` state asserted before axe runs), and system under `colorScheme: 'dark'` emulation, closing D-129's deferred dark-mode axe pass.

- vue-i18n English catalog and the no-raw-strings guarantee (task 167, spec §14.1, §4.1; decision D-132): every user-facing UI string now lives in the typed catalog module `ui/src/i18n/en.ts` and renders through `t()` — the application title, the primary-nav labels and its `aria-label`, the four page headings, and the placeholder status lines (the smoke contract's `app-status` text included, byte-identical through the catalog). `ui/src/i18n/index.ts` provides the `createAppI18n()` factory (Composition mode, `locale`/`fallbackLocale` `en`), mirroring `createAppRouter`'s test-seam pattern and installed beside it in the composition root; the catalog is nested by surface (`app`, `nav`, one namespace per route page) with flat dot paths at the call sites, so each content task owns its page's keys and the placeholder entries die with their pages. The task-036 ESLint exception is **removed** — `@intlify/vue-i18n/no-raw-text` runs with no ignore list **and gains the rule's `attributes` option**, so a static literal in a text-bearing attribute (`aria-label`, `title`, `alt`, `placeholder`, `label`, `abbr`, and the remaining text-bearing ARIA attributes — a closed list) is a lint error while bound `:attr="t('…')"` expressions stay legal; the lint contract `test/unit/vue-i18n-lint.spec.ts` pins both halves through the real ESLint binary, including that the former bootstrap spellings now fail. The test half of §14.1's rule is the new `test/component/i18n-catalog.spec.ts`: completeness in **both directions** (every `t()` key used in `ui/src` exists in the catalog; every catalog key is used — no dead keys, no empty namespaces), static-literal lookups only (a dynamic `t()` argument fails — it would be invisible to both directions), and the attribute surface D-131 recorded as the lint rule's blind spot, pinned over the real components with a no-drift check against the ESLint rule's own list — plus the disguise lint cannot see: a bound attribute whose expression is exactly a string literal (`:title="'Save'"`) fails the spec. Source scans are comment-aware (block, line, and HTML comments are stripped quote-sensitively, so `https://…` inside strings survives while documentation mentions of `t(…)` cannot fake a usage — both behaviors pinned by helper tests). `vue-i18n` lands as a devDependency mirroring `vue`/`vue-router` (the shipped tarball carries the prebuilt bundle; spec §3 keeps runtime dependencies minimal), and the runtime is CSP-neutral message-AST interpretation — proven by the green csp-shell e2e (zero `securitypolicyviolation` records) over the rebuilt bundle, with the smoke/a11y assertions unchanged. Later UI tasks add keys under their owning page's namespace in the same commit as the consuming component; the vue-i18n message-format specials (`{`, `}`, `@`, `|`) must be literal-escaped (`{'@'}`) — both conventions documented in `ui/README.md`. The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, APPROVE, 0 P0/P1, 3 P2/3 P3) folded in before landing: the lint `attributes` option and the closed-list wording, the bound-literal arm, `fallbackLocale` asserted, quote-aware comment stripping, the empty-namespace guard, and the dead `EnCatalog` export removed; key-level `t()` typing (`DefineLocaleMessage` augmentation) was deliberately not adopted — the both-directions spec is the enforcement, and compile-time key narrowing is recorded in D-132 as a later task's option.
- §13 status, providers, quota, and settings endpoints (task 160, spec §13, §7.2–§7.4, §8.4, §9.2, §6.2–§6.4, §14.5, §15.2; decision D-130): the four read-heavy §13 route families land as three endpoint plugins registered inside the `/api` scope behind the D-128 optional-options rule — absent dependencies, the paths keep answering the scope catch-all's 404 envelope (no half-wired surface; pinned per family). **`GET /api/status`** (`src/server/api/status.ts`): service state (the answering process is `ok`) with the effective §7.1 interval plus every account's §7.3 status and §7.2's two most-recents — the status is task 111's `deriveAccountStatus` over `readPollingRunEvidence` verbatim (nothing re-derived), the account set is the D-128 union of configuration entries and storage rows (an archived account stays visible and derives `disabled`, its history retained per §8.2), and the last error is the newest non-success run found by six bounded, indexed outcome-filtered probes (the D-114 CLI method, no facade change) with its message additionally passed through an exact-occurrence redaction of the account's resolved secret (§7.4's rule, the D-114 precedent made server-side — a contract-violating provider residual renders `[REDACTED]`, pinned with a marker seeded into a stored error). **`GET /api/quota?accountId=`** (same plugin): the §13 "latest successful snapshot" in task 122's reading — header facts from `getLatestSnapshot`, metric rows from `getLatestValidMetricValues` (D-077: the last **valid** value per metric key, each row carrying its own collection stamp, an invalid newer row never erasing a metric), validity/derived-field/§8.4 vocabulary verbatim, the §7.3 status riding as freshness context, and the honest empty state (`metrics: []`, null facts, never zero per §7.2); an account unknown to both halves is the 404, an entry-only or archived account is served (reads are not refused). **`GET /api/providers`** (`src/server/api/providers.ts`): the registry listing (available types in registration order) joined with the live configuration's §9.2 schedules — `peakHours: null` when none is configured (never an invented schedule), config-only providers not listed (they are not available types), the response nesting the configuration contract's own `PeakHoursConfigSchema` so the two cannot drift. **`GET`/`PUT /api/settings`** (`src/server/api/settings.ts`): the §6.4 seam — the safe view annotates every scalar with `{ value, source, restartRequired }` (§14.5's markings; `source: 'environment'` is the §6.3 read-only marking, `passwordConfigured` is the mask-and-boolean for the hash, and no view object can express a secret — the closed-contract tables pin it), and the PUT updates the §6.4 live-applicable vocabulary through the D-022 boundary (`validateSettings` → `parseConfig`, the loader's own rules and field-addressed value-free problems) with three stable FORBIDDEN refusals: `environment-overridden-read-only` (§6.3), `restart-required-field` (§6.4's trio is identified in GET and refused in PUT — those settings change through the file plus `quotameter restart`, D-130's literal reading of §13's "settings allowed for runtime modification"), and `password-hash-not-settable` (the plaintext `auth.password` is D-022's one sanctioned channel; the pending password reaches the store seam for the composition to hash, never a response). The configuration half is the third D-121 consumer-owned seam: `SettingsStore` (`read`/`apply`) is defined at the endpoint and left to the composition root over the reloader + atomic write + §6.4 change application; the PUT response reports D-026's `applied`/`restartRequired` (a pending hand-edited restart change surfaces through the startup diff, never this request's doing), and `StatusRouteOptions` adds the two §6.4-live per-request seams (`pollingIntervalSeconds`, `suspensionOf`) so reloads move the freshness window without re-registration. Done-condition evidence: `test/unit/api-status-schema.spec.ts` (closed tables, the §7.3-token and failure-outcome no-drift rows — the latter compile-time exhaustive), `test/unit/api-providers-schema.spec.ts`, `test/unit/api-settings-schema.spec.ts` (the no-secret closed guard, the body grammar, the D-026 vocabulary drift rows against `RELOAD_FIELDS`), `test/unit/api-status-view.spec.ts` and `test/unit/api-settings-view.spec.ts` (the pure compositions, incl. the attempted-fields ≡ `ENV_OVERRIDES` no-drift row and the blank-value table), `test/integration/api-status.spec.ts` (over a real migrated disposable `node:sqlite` database), and `test/integration/api-settings.spec.ts` + `test/integration/api-providers.spec.ts` (in-memory seam/registry doubles through the real `/api` scope) — contract shapes, the §15.1 seam's authorization paths, error classes (unknown ids, smuggled query/body fields through the D-128 strict scope Ajv, the three refusals, INTERNAL on a store failure), the §6.4/§6.3 done-condition pins, and the §15.4 marker discipline (account key, password, and hash never in any response body; the stored-error and plan-name `[REDACTED]` pins). The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, APPROVE with 4 P2 / 5 P3 — all folded in) added: the blank-value refusal (a supplied-but-blank-after-trim field answers `VALIDATION`/`blank-value` instead of riding D-022's prompt-skip semantics into a success-shaped no-op), the `sources` payload on `SettingsStore.apply` (the write-side obligation: the configuration handed over is environment-merged, and the composition's file write must skip the marked fields — otherwise a `QUOTAMETER_*` value would be baked into `config.json`), the shared `strictValidatorCompiler` in the new `src/server/api/validation.ts` (one construction site for the D-128 validator; 159's frozen accounts module joins at its next owning change), the §7.4 plan-name redaction in `/api/quota`, the one-clock-per-response hoist in `/api/status`, and the exhaustive compile-time outcome pin.
- Vue Router and the application shell (task 166, spec §14; decision D-131): the UI gains its route table and persistent layout. `ui/src/router/index.ts` defines §14's four pages — overview (`/`), account management (`/accounts`), settings (`/settings`), and login (`/login`) — as named routes with lazy-loaded components (code splitting keeps the shell light and the output CSP-shaped: each view is an external hashed chunk), plus a catch-all redirect to the overview (§14 prescribes no not-found screen). The `createAppRouter(history)` factory exposes the memory-history seam the component tests drive. `App.vue` becomes the shell — site header with the application title and the three-section primary nav (the login entry is auth-state dependent and stays task 180's seam), and the single `main` landmark the router renders into; `main.ts` installs the router and mounts after the initial navigation resolved, so a direct deep link paints the right view immediately. The four `ui/src/views/*.vue` are honest placeholders ("Not implemented yet.") awaiting their content tasks (170+, 172–174, 179, 180); the bootstrap status line and its `app-status` testid move from the shell into the overview view, keeping the smoke/a11y/csp-shell e2e contracts green unchanged. Direct navigation and the SPA fallback are pinned twice: in-memory router tests (`test/component/router.spec.ts`: each path resolves to its named route, direct navigation reaches every page, unknown paths redirect) and shell mount tests (`test/component/app.spec.ts`: nav surface, per-route view rendering, link-click navigation with `aria-current`), plus a browser probe in `test/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` (deep link `/accounts` renders its view; nav click switches to `/settings` without a reload) over the built app on top of the existing csp-shell deep-link proof.
- Vue 3 + Vite application foundation (task 165, spec §3, §14.1; decision D-129): the placeholder `ui/` becomes the real frontend application base. `src/main.ts` is the single composition root the later UI stages plug into (Vue Router + shell layout 166, the `vue-i18n` English catalog 167, theme selection 168); `src/App.vue` stays the minimal shell root with the same visible strings and testids, so the ESLint raw-text exception and the smoke/a11y/CSP e2e contracts test the same mount across the rewrite; the new `src/assets/base.css` carries the design tokens — `--qm-*` custom properties with a `prefers-color-scheme` dark block, the `system` half of the 168 theme seam — plus document-level base styles on the local system font stack (spec §14.1: no third-party fetches). The production build is **CSP-shaped by configuration and by contract** (executing D-127's binding on the UI implementation): `ui/vite.config.ts` sets `build.assetsInlineLimit: 0` (Vite's default 4096-byte limit inlines small assets as `data:` URIs, which `img-src`/`font-src 'self'` refuse) and `build.emptyOutDir: true` (DIST_DIR sits outside the Vite root, where Vite refuses to clean on its own — stale hashed assets would accumulate servable in the container volume), both pinned against the resolved config by the new `test/component/vite-config.spec.ts`; and `scripts/verify-build.mjs` grows from existence-checking to shape-checking: the emitted `index.html` must carry no inline `<script>` without `src`, no `<style>` block, no `style=` attribute, no inline event handler, no `javascript:` URI, and no `data:` URI; stylesheets must carry no external `url()` reference; and the JavaScript bundles are scanned for `data:` URIs too — Vite's asset inlining lands JS-imported assets inside the JS chunk, the exact regression `assetsInlineLimit: 0` prevents — so a configuration or plugin regression that reintroduces a D-127-blocked shape now fails `make build` instead of surfacing as a browser CSP violation. Fixing the script also fixed a latent vacuous check: `fs.promises.glob` returns an async iterable, so the original `matches.length === 0` (`.length` is `undefined`) could never fire — a build missing its JS/CSS bundles had passed verification since the script landed. The new `test/integration/verify-build-script.spec.ts` (real child processes over synthetic dist trees, the tz-verify-script pattern) pins the whole contract: happy tree and a same-origin stylesheet `url()` exit 0; every missing-artifact and inline/`data:`/external shape exits 1 with its message. Done-condition evidence: `make build` emits hashed external bundles into DIST_DIR with verify green, and the e2e suite runs green over that build — `smoke.spec.ts` (shell renders), `a11y.spec.ts` (h1 contract), and `csp-shell.spec.ts` booting the real Fastify construction with zero `securitypolicyviolation` events; the component mount suite passes unchanged. `ui/README.md` documents the stack, build flow, DIST_DIR invariant, contributor CSP rules, and the strings/language rule.
- §13 accounts endpoints (task 159, spec §13, §8.2, §6.3, §15.4; decision D-128): the five accounts routes — `GET /api/accounts` (the masked management list: the union of configuration entries and storage rows, id-ascending, with `hasKey` + the constant content-free `keyMask` and never a key value), `POST /api/accounts` (create with a write-only key: client-or-generated `acc-<hex>` id, optional key per §5.3's "add it later", storage-row-first write order with a compensating delete when the configuration write fails), `PATCH /api/accounts/:id` (§13's three mutable fields — label/enable/key — with `FORBIDDEN`/`account-archived` for the terminal lifecycle and `environment-key-read-only` for `apiKeyEnv`-sourced keys, §6.3), `DELETE /api/accounts/:id` (§8.2's split: `retireAccount` archive by default, idempotent; `purgeAccount` purge on exactly `?purgeHistory=true`, with D-061's per-table evidence counts in the response — including the gate-sanctioned `raw_responses` **count**, never a payload), and `POST /api/accounts/:id/test` (the provider `validateCredentials` run through the registry's branded `AccountContext`, answering `200` with D-052's `ok`/`rejected`/`unavailable` vocabulary whenever the test ran, `FORBIDDEN`/`no-key-configured` without a key, and `INTERNAL` for unclassifiable failures). The review round (glm-5.3#max — the primary timed out and kimi's quota window was spent; one round, REQUEST_CHANGES with 1 P1 / 4 P3) folded in before landing: DELETE's unknown-account verdict now decides over **both halves** (entry and row), so a configuration-only account — listed by GET, renamed by PATCH — archives by its entry removal and purges with a zero-evidence report, and a failed post-purge entry cleanup is retryable through the API instead of stranding a ghost entry behind a 404 (the P1); a foreign-`kind` escalation pin joined the test double's vocabulary (a P3); the create-race residual (two concurrent same-id POSTs — the loser answers INTERNAL over the raw constraint error) is declined with reasons and documented at the create site (single-user §15.1 service; mapping it would message-match SQLite's wording); the tasks.md flip is the main session's by the cycle's dual-mode rule. **The configuration-side seam:** keys and `enabled` live in `config.json` (§6.3 — never the database), so the endpoints' configuration half is the injected `AccountConfigStore` interface defined at the consumer (`src/server/api/accounts.ts`, the D-121 `RequestAuthenticator` precedent) — the routes are complete against the seam and the production implementation over `writeConfigFile` + §6.4's change application is composition-root work; the storage half reuses the facade and lifecycle modules verbatim. The scope plugin (`src/server/api/index.ts`) registers `accountRoutes` only when the construction passes the new optional `ApiRoutesOptions.accounts` — the unwired construction keeps the catch-all 404. **Write-only keys hold structurally:** every response serializes through closed TypeBox contracts (`src/schemas/api/accounts.ts`, the D-111 compiled-serializer guard generalized — a handler bug cannot put the key on the wire), and the strict scope-local validator compiler (`removeAdditional: false`) rejects unknown body fields the framework default would silently strip. No new error-registry codes (D-113 frozen): refusals ride `VALIDATION`/`FORBIDDEN`/`NOT_FOUND` with stable `details.reason` values. Done-condition evidence: `test/unit/api-accounts-schema.spec.ts` (the closed-contract tables + the id-charset no-drift pin against the configuration pattern, now exported as `ID_PATTERN`), `test/unit/api-accounts-view.spec.ts` (the pure merge matrix — which side owns which field, the honest-gap views, the mask's no-derivation pin), and `test/integration/api-accounts.spec.ts` (51 tests through the real `/api` scope over a real migrated temp SQLite database: per-endpoint contract, authorization — the §15.1 seam as it exists, fail-closed 401s in `required` mode, the proxy 403, the composed seam-true arm, the §15.3 mutation guards on the new routes — error classes incl. the purge-confirmation semantics with seeded §11.2 history, the compensation arm, the healed ghost-entry retry, and the marker-secret discipline across every flow), plus the `raw_responses`-count gate widening in `test/unit/raw-response-gate.spec.ts` (the D-069 one-file precedent, D-128's reasons recorded).
- §15.5 security headers and CSP (task 158, spec §15.5, §14.1, §15.6, §17.1; decision D-127): the task-146 construction (`src/server/http.ts`) now registers `@fastify/helmet` — the dependency D-109 declared but deliberately left unregistered — with an **exact-list configuration**: the emitted security-header surface is precisely the four things §15.5 names, nothing more. **The strict CSP** is `default-src 'none';script-src 'self';style-src 'self';img-src 'self';font-src 'self';connect-src 'self';base-uri 'none';form-action 'none';frame-ancestors 'none';object-src 'none'` — fail-closed (every fetch type without an explicit allowance is blocked), no `'unsafe-inline'`/`'unsafe-eval'` anywhere ("without inline scripts": Vite emits referenced hashed files and the runtime-only Vue build needs no eval), not one non-`'self'` source ("without external sources"), and no nonce machinery (nothing inline exists to whitelist). **Nosniff and no-referrer** are configured explicitly, and **every helmet default the spec does not list is disabled** — HSTS (TLS terminates at the §15.6 reverse proxy; the service never speaks TLS itself, and an app-set `includeSubDomains` would reach through a passing proxy into subdomains the operator never delegated), `X-Frame-Options` (framing policy lives once, inside the spec'd CSP, as `frame-ancestors 'none'`), the COOP/COEP/CORP/agent-cluster/prefetch/Adobe headers, and helmet 8.3's still-default legacy-IE pair (`X-Download-Options`, `X-XSS-Protection: 0` — found empirically and disabled with the rest). **`Cache-Control: no-store` is the API-only rule** and rides its own root-scope hook on the §13 `/api` path boundary (the single-sited `isApiRequestPath` predicate the not-found handler's SPA split shares — query-stripped and percent-DECODED, because fastify's `request.url` is the raw wire path while the router matches the decoded one, so `/%61pi/health` is an API path on the wire (the review round reproduced the raw-path gap); `/apiary` stays a UI deep link), registered ahead of the Host guard so gate-rejected API requests carry it too; static responses keep `@fastify/static`'s own default (`public, max-age=0` — revalidate-always over ETags, so an upgraded build never serves a stale shell), and `sendApiError` keeps its error-path `no-store` (the envelope legitimately appears outside `/api` — API-only-mode frontend 404s, SPA-containment 403s — and on the router-level `frameworkErrors` path that bypasses hooks). The health route's task-147 route-scoped header is gone, generalized by the hook. **The layering was verified empirically on the pinned runtime, not modeled**: helmet's hooks (plugin registered ahead of the construction's `addHook` calls) run FIRST in the root chain — the Host guard's thrown 403s carry the full set — leaving router-level `frameworkErrors` the only helmet-less class, which still answers the §13 envelope with `no-store`; both facts are pinned so a fastify/helmet ordering change surfaces instead of shipping silently. **The browser proof that the UI still runs** is the new `test/e2e/csp-shell.spec.ts`: the existing e2e suite drives `vite preview`, which never passes through the application server — so the new spec boots the real `buildHttpServer` over the built UI (DIST_DIR-aware) and loads it in a real Chromium, asserting the exact header values on the main response and an empty `securitypolicyviolation`/console-error record; a CSP check on the preview server would have asserted nothing. Evidence: `test/integration/security-headers.spec.ts` (exact-value matrix: API success/error/401-verdict/HEAD, static index/JS/CSS/deep-link, the `/api`-boundary edges `/api`, `/api?x=1`, `/apiary` and the percent-encoded `/%61pi/health`/`/%61pi`, API-only mode, and the two layering pins, with every unlisted helmet header asserted absent beside a COMPLETE header-name allowlist on the representative API response), the flipped registration pin in `test/integration/server-http.spec.ts` (helmet registered; `@fastify/rate-limit` still deliberately not, D-125), and `test/e2e/csp-shell.spec.ts`. The policy binds the future UI (165+): no inline `style=` attributes in markup — and no library styling through the style attribute, which ECharts' default HTML tooltips and SVG renderer do (apache/echarts#19570/#19398), so the chart tasks must use `tooltip.renderMode: 'richText'` + the canvas renderer or amend the policy with `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'` (§15.5 bans inline SCRIPTS only) — no `data:` URIs, which Vite's default 4 KiB `assetsInlineLimit` produces for small assets (the policy requires `assetsInlineLimit: 0`), no workers or cross-origin WebSockets — any such need is a successor decision amending the directive set and its byte pins. Review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, 0 P0/P1): the percent-decode fix, the complete-allowlist tripwire, and the ECharts/`assetsInlineLimit` constraints above are its accepted P2s; its P3-1 (setting the three headers inside `sendApiError` for the router-level rejection arm) was declined — CSP-literal duplication or an import cycle for a JSON body nothing renders; the exception stays documented and pinned.
- §15.2 session cookie flags (task 157, spec §15.2, §15.6; decision D-126): the new `src/services/session-cookie.ts` lands the fourth §15.2 primitive of the future `AuthService` beside the credential, session, and rate-budget modules — everything the future login/logout endpoint needs to serialize the `Set-Cookie` header as one frozen attribute object spread into `@fastify/cookie`'s `reply.setCookie` (the plugin task 146 registered for parsing). **`HttpOnly` and `SameSite=Strict` are unconditional** (§15.2's own words, no exception cells); the cookie's identity is pinned as `SESSION_COOKIE_NAME` (`quotameter_session`) and `SESSION_COOKIE_PATH` (`/api` — §13's prefix, so the cookie never rides a static-asset or SPA request); the value passes through verbatim (the base64url token is RFC 6265 cookie-octet-safe, D-123's pin, now asserted on the wire). **`Secure` mirrors exactly one question — is the client-facing leg of the request HTTPS?** (`isClientFacingHttps` over `SessionCookieTransportFacts`): the request's own TLS socket, else the leftmost `X-Forwarded-Proto` element, else the leftmost RFC 7239 `Forwarded` entry's `proto` parameter (quote-aware splitting — a quoted `for` value may carry `,` or `;`, RFC 7239 §5.2's own example, and neither may cut an entry off its `proto`; the review round's P2), else HTTP — the leg the server itself saw, because §15.6 terminates TLS at the reverse proxy and the service carries no certificate configuration. The configuration contract defines **no public-URL or proxy field** (§6.2's schema covers `server`/`app`/`auth`/`logging`/providers/accounts), so the spec's "public URL/reverse-proxy configuration" resolves from the request itself (D-126), and the bind host has no vote: a non-loopback bind is still plain HTTP, and `Secure` on an HTTP cookie protects nothing (the token rides the same wire) while breaking the session bootstrap (a `Secure` cookie is not stored or sent over plain HTTP). Fail direction: a forged `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` on a direct plain-HTTP request can only make the attacker's own cookie unusable, and §15.1/D-121 already force the password on every request carrying a proxy indicator. **`Max-Age` mirrors the row's effective expiry at set time** (`sessionCookieAttributes({ transport, expiresAtMs, nowMs })`, `ceil((expiresAt − now)/1000)` whole seconds, rounded up so the client cookie never dies before the row D-124's verdict judges — the server-side verdict stays the gate; the fresh login cookie carries `Max-Age=604800`, the 7-day inactivity bound); re-issuing the cookie on a refreshed sighting is the endpoint's documented composition, and an endpoint that never re-issues merely caps the cookie at 7 days (a re-login, never an out-of-bounds session). Fail-fast: a non-integer epoch-ms input throws a `TypeError` naming the field (the D-090/D-123 family), and `nowMs >= expiresAtMs` throws — an already-expired session gets its cookie **cleared**, never set: `clearSessionCookieAttributes(transport)` is the logout twin (`Max-Age=0`, identical attributes, so the deletion addresses the cookie that was set). The module owns no endpoint (the login/logout routes are the plan gap D-125 recorded) and no logger (nothing can log a token; zero-console pin). Done-condition evidence — "cookie attributes are tested under HTTP and HTTPS/proxy setups": `test/unit/session-cookie.spec.ts` holds the pure tables (the full transport matrix incl. multi-hop leftmost decisions, XFP-over-`Forwarded` and TLS-over-headers precedence, empty/garbage/quoted values, the unconditional pins, the exact/ceil `Max-Age` table, the fail-fast guards, the clear twin, the zero-console pin), and `test/integration/session-cookie.spec.ts` pins the wire through the full task-146 construction with the real `@fastify/cookie`: probe routes composing the module exactly as the endpoint will (transport facts resolved from the request, a minted token as the value) assert the serialized `Set-Cookie` attribute set under plain-HTTP loopback (no `Secure`), proxied HTTP (`X-Forwarded-Proto: http`, no `Secure`), proxied HTTPS (`X-Forwarded-Proto: https` and `Forwarded: proto=https`, `Secure`), multi-hop lists over Node's folded header view (leftmost decides), direct TLS (`Secure` with no headers), the logout clear (`Max-Age=0` with/without `Secure` per transport), and the value's verbatim round-trip back through `request.cookies`. `src/server/http.ts`, `src/server/api/auth.ts`, `src/services/sessions.ts`, `src/services/README.md`, and `src/server/README.md` change comments/docs only.
- §15.2 login rate limiting (task 156, spec §15.2, §17.1; decision D-125): the new `src/services/login-rate-limit.ts` lands the per-source failure budget the future login endpoint composes — "Rate-limit authentication to no more than five failed attempts per source address within 15 minutes, then apply increasing delays." `createLoginRateLimiter()` holds one in-process map keyed by an **opaque source string** (never parsed; the endpoint resolves the request's source address) and exposes the two halves of the endpoint flow: `delayBeforeAttempt(source, now)` — a read-only query answering `0` while fewer than five failures sit inside the **sliding** 15-minute window (half-open expiry: a failure exactly window-old is out, the D-124 boundary discipline), otherwise the **increasing delay** `1 s × 2^(n−5)` capped at the window length (the §7.2 doubling family at login scale; delays, never rejections — §15.2's own word: the endpoint sleeps, then processes, so a legitimate user who mistypes five times still logs in, later, while a serially attacking source's guessing rate collapses to the delay cadence (the endpoint holds the cadence under concurrency by serializing per source — D-125's consequence for that task)) — and `recordAttempt(source, 'success' | 'failure', now)`, which reports the processed attempt's outcome: a failure appends its timestamp (pruning what the window already expired), a success **resets the source's budget** (a verified password ends the guessing). The delay is computed from history, before verification — §15.2 budgets _failed_ attempts — so the check-then-record ordering is the honest one, and concurrent attempts racing inside it can only under-delay a burst whose failures have not landed yet; the next attempt sees the full count. **Bounded memory with behaviorally exact counting** (the growth contract, stated in D-125): per source only the newest saturation-count timestamps are retained — in-window-ness is monotone in time order, so the in-window failures are always the newest ones, and every count at or above the delay formula's saturation point (the smallest `n` whose delay equals the cap, derived from the exported constants and pinned against the retention bound by test) answers the capped delay, making the bounded history identical to an unbounded one — under the same sweep — for every count and clock reading: the retention trim sorts by stamp value first, so the retained set is always the largest stamps by value and a regressed record cannot skew which stamp drops (pinned against the raw formula for counts 0–100 plus a regressed-clock trim case the review round contributed); map-wide, a sweep at most once per window drops sources whose failures have all expired, so the map never holds more than the sources active in roughly the last two windows (the sweep's deletion side is behaviorally invisible — an expired entry answers zero and counts from zero whether deleted or retained; its keep side is pinned observably). State is in-memory only; a restart clears every budget. Like its §15.2 siblings the primitive is **logger-free and credential-free by construction** (inputs: source key, outcome label, `now`): §15.2's "log failed attempts at `warn` without logging passwords" is the endpoint layer's documented contract — one `warn` record per failed attempt carrying the source address and outcome only, never the candidate password, the stored hash, or any session token (§15.4; the endpoint's marker-secret test will own that pin) — and the module owns **no timers** (delays are returned numbers; the sleep belongs to the endpoint, keeping the module deterministic under fake clocks) and **no clock of its own** (`now` is an explicit argument, D-021; non-integer readings fail fast with a field-naming `TypeError` before any state change, the D-090 family; a regressed wall reading is trusted — it can only widen the counted window, the fail-closed direction). Done-condition evidence: `test/unit/login-rate-limit.spec.ts` (23 fake-clock tests) pins the budget (attempts 1–5 undelayed, sixth onward delayed), the exact doubling sequence with the cap takeover, formula-identical answers for every count (the retention-exactness pin), the value-sorted trim under a regressed clock, the ±1 ms window edge, the partial slide (failures expiring one by one), per-source isolation in both directions (opaque keys), the success reset (full and partial budgets, unknown-source no-op), the pruning record, the sweep (deletion observable through fresh counting, retention of live entries through continued counting), the clock discipline (fail-fast before state changes; regressed clock fail-closed), and the zero-console pin (§15.4/§16: a module that cannot log cannot leak a credential through one). No HTTP surface ships here — the login/logout endpoints and cookie flags are later tasks (no plan task owns the login endpoint yet — a plan gap D-125 records for the main session); `src/server/api/auth.ts`, `src/services/sessions.ts`, `src/server/http.ts`, `src/server/README.md`, `src/server/api/error.ts`, and `test/integration/server-http.spec.ts` change comments only (stale pre-156 story corrected: the rate-limit dependency has no owning task left) (the `@fastify/rate-limit` dependency stays unregistered: the generic per-request 429 limiter cannot express a failed-attempt budget with increasing delays — whether it stays declared is an open maintenance question for the main session); no public API, format, or event changes.
- §15.2 session expiry and revocation (task 155, spec §15.2, §17.1; decision D-124): the request-side half of `src/services/sessions.ts` lands beside task 154's creation — the module now judges and revokes what it mints, over the same task-088 facade (`getSession`, `touchSession`, `deleteSession`; nothing in `src/storage/` changed). **The verdict**: `authenticateSession({ repository, token, now })` is the per-request composition the future `RequestAuthenticator` wiring wraps — digest the presented cookie (`hashSessionToken`, the module's own discipline: the raw wire token never reaches storage), look the row up, and judge `expires_at` on **half-open validity** `[created_at, expires_at)` (D-124's pinned boundary: a request at exactly the expiry instant is rejected, one ms before it is accepted — and the verdict's test is the interval's upper endpoint alone, `now < expires_at`; the lower endpoint names the canonical row's birth, with a clock reading before `created_at` accepted and its refresh shrinking the expiry, the fail-closed direction — pinned by a regressed-clock test. The half-open reading is the one under which every accepted sighting refreshes to a strictly future expiry, and under which an inclusive boundary would let a sighting at exactly `created + 30 d` rewrite `expires_at = now`, a refresh to an already-dead expiry; the strict-`<` `deleteSessionsExpiredBefore` of §11.3's cleanup still deletes only rows dead before `now`, so a boundary-dead row waits for the next cleanup — hygiene lag only, the verdict is the gate). The row's single effective-expiry column already folds both §15.2 bounds, so `now < expires_at` is the whole validity test; on the valid arm the sighting is recorded — `touchSession` writes `last_seen_at = now` and `expires_at = effectiveSessionExpiry(created_at, now)` — sliding the 7-day inactivity window to `now + 7 d` while the 30-day absolute cap never extends (the single-sited formula over the schema's exported constants is the same arithmetic the shipped CHECKs enforce, so the write cannot violate them). The verdict variants carry the evidence: `expired` names which bound fired (`rule: 'inactivity' | 'absolute'`, read off the row's own expiry — `'absolute'` when `expires_at = created_at + 30 d`), and `unknown` is the single fail-closed answer for every absent row — never minted, logout-revoked, password-revoked, or §11.3-cleaned, states storage cannot distinguish and the 401 must not try; a refresh whose `touchSession` reports the row gone downgrades to `unknown` too — nothing can interleave on the server's own synchronous connection, but the `quotameter passwd` CLI revokes every session over its own separate connection while the server runs, so the arm is live cross-process and a revocation must beat an in-flight sighting, never lose to it. The verdict performs no write on the rejected arms — expired-row deletion stays §11.3's cleanup (D-061). **The revocations**: `revokeSession({ repository, token })` is logout — digest the token, delete the one row, answer whether one existed (idempotent, and the sibling sessions stay live); password-change revocation ("changing the password revokes all sessions") stays where task 144 landed it — `revokeAllSessions` in `src/cli/passwd.ts` over the facade's `deleteAllSessions` — with the verdict side now agreeing executably that nothing survives it (a service-level wrapper was considered and rejected: it would only forward one facade call, adding a second name, no logic). The module stays synchronous (D-058), logger-free (§15.4 — executably pinned across the new functions too), and D-021 throughout: a non-integer `now` fails fast with a field-naming `TypeError` before any storage call, and repository failures propagate unchanged. The login/logout endpoints, rate limiting (156), and the cookie's wire flags (157) stay later tasks' scope (`src/server/api/auth.ts` changes comments only, naming the landed primitive its seam composes). Done-condition evidence ("fake-clock tests cover both expiry rules and revocation"): the extended `test/unit/sessions.spec.ts` over a behavioral in-memory facade — the inactivity rule alone (idle past 7 d inside the 30 d bound), the absolute rule over recent activity (a 5-day-old sighting rejected at the cap), the half-open boundary pinned for both rules one ms apart, the sliding refresh and the never-extending cap with the exact `touchSession` arguments, the regressed-clock acceptance whose refresh shrinks the expiry, unknown tokens, the revocation-won-the-race downgrade, logout (by digest, idempotent, sibling-sparing), password-change revocation through `deleteAllSessions`, fail-fast and failure-propagation arms, and the zero-console pin — plus `test/integration/sessions-authentication.spec.ts` over real migrated databases: a 29-day fake-clock walk sliding the window daily and pinning the cap from day 23, the day-30 rejection of a session seen one day ago (`rule: 'absolute'`), the 8-day-idle rejection with the row left in place (the verdict never deletes), the half-open boundary on the real schema, refresh writes passing the 087 CHECKs with the §15.4 marker scan (all four byte-form encodings, the creation suite's parity) repeated after refreshes, and both revocations through the facade (`revokeSession` and the exact `deleteAllSessions` call passwd makes). The module measures 100% coverage on all four axes without joining the D-085 pinned critical-module keys (the D-118/D-122/D-123 precedent).
- §15.2 session creation and storage (task 154, spec §15.2, §11.2; decision D-123): the new `src/services/sessions.ts` mints the §15.2 cookie token and owns its store-only-the-digest discipline — the session half of the future `AuthService`, beside task 144/153's `password-hash.ts` (D-118/D-122), over the landed task-087/088 storage (the `sessions` table's hex-shape and expiry CHECKs, `SESSION_INACTIVITY_TIMEOUT_MS`/`SESSION_MAX_AGE_MS`, and the `insertSession` facade — nothing in `src/storage/` changed). **The token**: `generateSessionToken()` draws 256 bits of `node:crypto` randomness into the **base64url** wire form (43 unpadded URL-safe characters) — the encoding D-056 recorded as a precondition, because a lowercase-hex cookie would be shape-identical to the SHA-256 digest and no SQL constraint could tell a leaked raw token from the identity column; the suite pins executably that a minted token can never take the digest's shape — 43 characters against the CHECK's 64. **The digest**: `hashSessionToken(token)` is SHA-256 over the **wire string** — the cookie value exactly as a request presents it — so task 155's per-request lookup is `hashSessionToken(cookieValue) → getSession` with no decode step and no decode-failure arm, and the digest is byte-identical to the `sha256(<string>).digest('hex')` shape the schema CHECK enforces. **The row**: `createSession({ repository, now })` inserts the §11.2 tuple through the repository — `created_at` and `last_seen_at` at the injected instant (`last_seen_at` born equal to `created_at`: the login that mints a session is its first sighting, D-056) and `expires_at` from `effectiveSessionExpiry(createdAt, lastSeenAt)`, the new single site of D-056's formula `min(last_seen + 7 d, created + 30 d)` computed from the schema's own exported constants so the writer and the shipped CHECKs can never drift — a fresh session expires at the tighter 7-day inactivity bound, and task 155's sighting refresh reuses the same helper after `touchSession`. The function returns `{ token, session }` — the raw token's **only** disclosure, exactly once, to the caller that sets the cookie (157 owns the flags); the module holds no logger, so the §15.4 "never in logs" rule is by construction and executably pinned (zero console calls during creation), it is synchronous like the repository it writes through (D-058), takes its clock as an explicit `now` argument (the D-021 seam, `deriveAccountStatus(input, nowEpochMs)` pattern — 155's fake-clock tests pass readings directly), rejects non-integer timestamps — fractional or non-finite — with a field-naming `TypeError` before any token or row exists (§11.1's integer epoch milliseconds, kept ahead of the STRICT INTEGER columns' opaque REAL refusal), propagates repository failures unchanged (the named CHECKs are the write gate, D-058), and retries no mint — the per-pair collision probability of two independent 256-bit tokens is 2^-256 (the birthday bound sits near 2^128 mints for a ~50% risk), an event retry ceremony cannot make more testable. The login endpoint, expiry enforcement, revocation-on-password-change, and rate limiting stay tasks 155–156 (`src/server/api/auth.ts` changes comments only, naming the landed primitive its seam composes). Done-condition evidence: `test/unit/sessions.spec.ts` (17 tests — the wire shape over hundreds of mints incl. the deterministic never-digest-shape pin, the digest's known answer against `node:crypto` itself, the D-056 expiry table on the imported constants incl. the absolute-bound clamp and the safe-integer throws, and the creation contract over a capturing repository stub: inserted `NewSession` field-exact, the returned record mirroring the row, distinct tokens per creation, clock-seam stamps, failure propagation with no partial row, and the no-logging pin) and `test/integration/sessions-creation.spec.ts` over real migrated disposable databases (the marker discipline as the executable done-condition — after creation, **every cell of every table** from `sqlite_master` is scanned and contains neither the token nor its base64/hex/latin1 byte forms, while the one `sessions` row positively stores `sha256(token)` with the §15.2 creation timestamps, the insert itself proving the 087 CHECKs accept the writer's shape; the §15.2 lookup round-trip incl. the unknown-digest miss; and the two-session independence with the marker scan repeated). The module measures 100% coverage on all four axes without joining the D-085 pinned critical-module keys (the D-118/D-122 precedent).
- §15.2 password verification (task 153, spec §15.2, §15.1; decision D-122): the missing half of `src/services/password-hash.ts` lands beside task 144's hashing (D-118) — one module now owns the whole §15.2 credential format. **`verifyPassword(stored, candidate)`** takes the stored hash exactly as the configuration holds it (`stored: string | undefined` — the optional `auth.passwordHash` field passes straight through, and runtime junk beyond it answers `false` through the same guard) and parses the stored self-describing encoding strictly (exactly four `$`-fields, the one `scrypt` algorithm id, the canonical parameter grammar `N=…,r=…,p=…,keylen=…` — canonical key order, plain decimal, no leading zeros — canonical padded base64 for salt and hash with a re-encode round-trip that rejects non-canonical trailing bits and everything Node's permissive decoder would silently forgive, and internal consistency: the decoded hash byte length must equal the `keylen` the string claims), then re-derives the candidate under **the parameters the string itself carries**, never `SCRYPT_PARAMETERS` — D-118's forward-compatibility rule made executable: a future cost raise changes only what newly set hashes use, and every already-stored hash keeps verifying (pinned with a hand-built `N=16384, keylen=32` encoding whose derivation the test recomputes itself). The derivation runs inside two exported budgets: `VERIFICATION_MAXMEM_BYTES` (1 GiB of memory — sixteen times today's 64 MiB requirement, so any plausible raise verifies unchanged while a corrupt or hostile stored string can never talk the verifier into unbounded allocation: `node:crypto` refuses beyond `maxmem`, and the refusal classifies as a mismatch) and `VERIFICATION_MAX_PARALLELIZATION` (1024 — the CPU twin `maxmem` cannot provide: `p` multiplies compute without touching memory, and an unbounded `p` in a corrupt string could pin a libuv thread for hours, measured `p=4000` ≈ 21 s on the pinned runtime; the producer writes `p=1`, so 1024 is generous). **Constant-time discipline:** the comparison is `timingSafeEqual` over the derived key bytes, never over strings, with the equal-length precondition guaranteed _before_ any secret is touched (the parse rejects self-inconsistent lengths; the encoding's length facts are public — whoever can read them already has the `0600` file), and there is no early exit on candidate content: every candidate under a parseable stored hash costs exactly one full derivation, wrong passwords included. **Fail closed, never throws:** an absent/empty stored hash, any malformed shape (unknown or miscased algorithm id, wrong field count, off-grammar parameters, non-canonical base64, `keylen` disagreement), and parameter sets `node:crypto` refuses (a non-power-of-two `N`, a set beyond the ceiling) all answer `false` — an unverifiable credential is a wrong password to this function, never an error and never an accept. Parameter _semantics_ stay with `node:crypto` as the single authority (the parse owns structure plus the module's own two budgets — no mirrored validation table to drift); the operator-facing diagnosis of a corrupt configuration belongs to the config layer. **No §15.1 seam wiring, by design:** the `RequestAuthenticator` seam authenticates _requests_, which carry session cookies (154/155) — the password is presented only at the login endpoint, which stays public through `PUBLIC_AUTH_ROUTE_CONFIG` and consumes this primitive there; the seam's docblock and the server README now say exactly that. The done-condition ("password verification and invalid-password paths are tested") is pinned executably by the extended `test/unit/password-hash.spec.ts` (24 new cases): the round-trip against the module's own `hashPassword` (accept/reject), unicode accept/reject, the string-parameters pin, a seventeen-row malformed-encoding table (empty, unknown and miscased algorithm ids, missing/trailing fields, swapped order, unknown key, non-numeric, leading-zero and zero values, non-canonical / non-quantum / non-zero-trailing-bits / empty base64 fields, `keylen` disagreement, `p` beyond the CPU ceiling), the absent (`undefined`) and JS-boundary (`null`, number) stored-hash rows, and both `node:crypto` refusal arms (`N=1`; a parameter set beyond the memory ceiling, derived from the exported bound so the pin follows it) — deterministic (no clocks, fixed salts where determinism matters), each invalid path asserted through `resolves` (fail closed is also never-throws).

- §15.1 auth-mode selection (task 152, spec §15.1, §17.1; decision D-121): the new `src/server/api/auth.ts` decides, per request under `/api`, **whether authentication is required** — the mode-selection half of §15.1, deliberately split from credential verification (task 153's scrypt, 154/155's sessions, 156's rate limiting plug into the exported `RequestAuthenticator` seam: one injected boolean the guard consults only on verdicts demanding credentials, defaulting fail-closed to "nothing is authenticated" until they land). **The verdict** is a pure, fail-closed `classifyAuthMode` over four facts — the configured `auth.mode` (§6.4 restart-requiring, captured at construction, the D-120 precedent), the bind's loopback-ness (`isLoopbackHost`, the single owner of that semantics), password presence (read per request through a thunk: §6.4/D-026 classify `auth.passwordHash` live, so a `passwd`-set password takes effect without a restart), and the §15.1 proxy indicators (`Forwarded`, `X-Real-IP`, any `X-Forwarded-*` — presence-only and value-blind over the header names, so Node's duplicate fold cannot hide an indicator; the D-120 duplicate-Host scan needed `rawHeaders` because there any occurrence must reject, here any single occurrence suffices). `auto` + loopback bind + no indicator stays passwordless (a configured password does not tighten `auto`'s own "direct loopback requests do not require a password"); every other cell with a password — `required` mode unconditionally, `auto` on a non-loopback bind, `auto` behind a proxy hop — answers the §13 envelope's 401 class (`details.reason: "authentication-required"`); and the proxied request against a passwordless server answers §15.1's own **403 with instructions** (`details.reason: "proxy-without-password"`, the message naming the two ways out: `quotameter passwd` or direct loopback access — reachable only as `auto` + loopback, since `required`/non-loopback without a password refuses at startup in the config layer; those shapes still fail closed to 401 if ever constructed anyway). **Placement** follows D-120's pin: the guard registers as the `/api` scope's third `onRequest` hook, after the Origin and custom-header guards and under the root-scope Host allowlist — the observable order is pinned on the wire (a hostile-Host proxied request answers `disallowed-host`, a cross-origin proxied mutation answers the Origin verdict, a header-less one the custom-header verdict, and only then the auth verdict). **The public health route** (`GET /api/health`, §13) opts out through a route-config marker (`PUBLIC_AUTH_ROUTE_CONFIG`, read from `request.routeOptions.config` — available to `onRequest` hooks on the pinned runtime, inherited by the auto-generated HEAD twin), the same contract the future login endpoint uses; it stays 200 in every mode, address, and proxy state. The frontend shell and SPA fallback stay outside the layer (D-121): the §14.6 login page must load before credentials can be presented, and no protected surface exists there. `HttpServerOptions` gains the optional `auth` projection (`{mode?, passwordConfigured?, isAuthenticated?}` — defaults are the config layer's own) derived in the construction beside `host`; the full address × proxy-header × password × mode matrix is pinned executably by `test/unit/api-auth-mode-guard.spec.ts` (the indicator family incl. lookalikes, the 16-cell classifier table, the hook's throws, the seam and live-password-thunk arms) and `test/integration/api-auth-mode.spec.ts` (the wire matrix through the task-146 construction: byte-pinned 403/401 envelopes, each §15.1 indicator alone, pass-through evidence, the health pin incl. HEAD and proxied, the login-shell/SPA arms under `required`, the guard-order pins, and §15.4/§16 no-echo/zero-log).
- §15.3 Host allowlist validation (task 151, spec §15.3; decision D-120): every request the server answers — API routes, static assets, SPA deep links, and the API-only not-found arm alike — now carries its `Host` header validated against `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`, and the configured host (`server.host`), closing the DNS-rebinding/Host-header family §15.3 names, in all modes including passwordless loopback access. The layer lives in the task-149 §15.3 seam `src/server/api/security.ts` (`createHostAllowlist` + the pure, fail-closed `classifyHostRequest` + the `createHostGuard` factory hook) but registers at **root scope** in the task-146 construction (`src/server/http.ts`) — the one §15.3 check whose done-condition binds frontend routes as well as API routes, and outermost by fastify's hook chain (a hostile-Host request answers the Host verdict before the `/api` scope's Origin/custom-header guards or any handler — verified on the pinned runtime). **D-120's resolutions:** the comparison is over authority NAMES with ports unpinned — §15.6's recommended SSH tunneling remaps ports freely (`-L 9999:127.0.0.1:8787` sends `Host: localhost:9999`), while the rebinding threat is a name confusion, which name comparison fully defeats (default-port equivalence `localhost` ≡ `localhost:80` still holds; the listening port is never pinned); the configured host is injected at construction time through the existing `HttpServerOptions.host` — correct by the config model itself, §6.4 pins `server.host` as restart-requiring, so it cannot change under a running listener (a wildcard `0.0.0.0` bind therefore admits only its literal name, now with one construction-time `warn` naming the reachable shapes — concrete-address bind, SSH tunnel, allowlisted proxy hop; IPv6 binds work in both configured spellings, normalized into the bracketed wire name with zone ids percent-encoded; and the loopback-bound proxy forwarding the public Host is the one legitimate shape this single-host surface cannot express — recorded as the explicit gap for task 157's §15.2 public-URL setting to close, with the hosts-file-pin recipe (configure the public name as `server.host`, pinned to loopback in `/etc/hosts`) as the working shape until then, the shape `api-origin.spec.ts`'s proxied arm pins); and absolute-form request targets are adjudicated too — on the real wire the router matches their extracted path while `request.url` keeps the full attacker-controlled URL (verified live; `fastify.inject` normalizes the shape away, so those arms pin over real sockets), so the target must carry an allowlisted name equal to the Host name (RFC 9112 §3.2.2). The runtime gaps the guard closes beside the spec text: an HTTP/1.0 request without a Host is accepted by the parser (rejected here as `missing-host`; the Host-less HTTP/1.1 shape is answered 400 by llhttp before fastify — the documented boundary), duplicate Host entries are folded onto the first value by Node but survive in `rawHeaders` (rejected as `duplicate-host`, the RFC's MUST), and unbracketed IPv6, invalid ports, multi-colon shapes, and duplicate-joined values fail closed as malformed names (the authority parser is structure-first — a trailing default port cannot collapse `localhost:8080:80` onto a real name). Rejections answer the §13 envelope's `FORBIDDEN` class — `{"code":"FORBIDDEN","message":"Request host rejected","details":{"reason":…}}` over a closed five-value reason vocabulary (`missing-host`, `duplicate-host`, `disallowed-host`, `disallowed-target`, `target-mismatch`), echoing nothing the client sent (§15.4) and logging nothing (§16). The method-blind matrix (safe methods guarded exactly like mutations — the rebinding page reads the frontend through GET), both serving-mode twins, the precedence pins over the landed guards, and the raw-socket wire arms are pinned in `test/integration/api-host.spec.ts`; the pure table (allowlist builder, every IPv6/case/port/malformed shape, the absolute-form verdicts, the hook's duplicate scan) in `test/unit/api-host-guard.spec.ts`; the landed suites' injections keep their allowlisted Hosts (`api-origin.spec.ts`'s proxied arm now builds the server with the public name configured — itself the configured-host arm of the allowlist).
- `quotameter doctor` (task 145, spec §5.5; decision D-119): the new `src/cli/doctor.ts` replaces the last-but-one top-level dispatch stub (only `run` remains) — the diagnostics command whose product is the **check-result contract** the done-condition itself names ("checks return actionable messages and a stable exit status"). Every check yields one report row with a closed vocabulary (D-119): `pass` (the established fact), `fail` (a problem needing operator attention; the summary plus detail lines indented under the row name the next step — a quotameter command or a system remedy), `unavailable` (the check could not run — timedatectl on a systemd-less machine, an unreachable manager, an inconclusive credential check — honest, and never failing the exit code), and `skip` (a precondition was not met; the failing row carries the remedy). **The exit code is 1 exactly when at least one check is `fail`** (or the pre-flight owner/directory resolution failed, rendered as the standard failure lines with no report), otherwise 0 — doctor is the attention-flagging command (the flutter/brew-doctor convention), so the states `status` (D-114) deliberately never fails on — a stopped service, an occupied port, an absent configuration — are `fail` rows here with their next steps: different command, different job. **The eleven §5.5 checks each compose the layer that owns their fact, never re-derived**: the Node.js runtime against `MINIMUM_NODE_VERSION` (lazily imported from `src/server/main.ts`, D-098's mirror of `engines.node`, so the dispatch graph stays light; an unparseable version fails closed) and `node:sqlite` availability through the new `src/storage/sqlite-availability.ts` probe (the layout gate keeps every `node:sqlite` import form inside `src/storage/`, and the dynamic hop keeps the CLI graph free of storage — the probe's ExperimentalWarning on every doctor run is intrinsic to §5.5's check, which can only answer availability by importing the built-in); the configuration through the service's own `loadConfigWithEnv` pipeline (absent fails with `quotameter init`, invalid lists the pipeline's field-addressed problems, env-only states its provenance); the §6.3 permissions **diagnostically only** — modes `0700`/`0600`, owner uid vs the service owner's, no symlink, right type, with the `chmod`/`chown` remedies rendered (doctor never chmods: `enforceConfigPermissions` is the fixing half, and a diagnostic that mutates its subject lies about what it found); the configured display zone through the clock module's IANA gate; the database through the read-only storage sibling plus **`PRAGMA quick_check`** (absent = skip "not created yet", a refused open fails with D-114's hot-journal hint, problems quote the first rows); per-account connectivity through the §8.1 `validateCredentials` contract with init-account's secret-scrubbing classifier (rejected → fail with the replacement remedy, inconclusive → unavailable — D-052's distinction, disabled → skip, an `apiKeyEnv` missing from this shell → the D-112 residual, and the review round's totality guards: a schema-valid-but-blank label or key is a fail row — whitespace is never a credential or label, D-011 — and an account naming a provider this build does not carry is never probed with the bundled adapter, §15.4); the service rows through D-110's disk probe plus the §5.4 parser — **stale `ExecStart` Node/CLI paths against `serviceExecPaths()`** fail with `service install` as the regeneration remedy, beside the unit-kind structural validator (the system unit against the owner-scoped `ReadWritePaths` pair) and both-installed detection (the 139 mistake) — and the exported D-114 `planManagerProbe`/`describeManagerState` for the manager state (not active is a fail with `quotameter start`, sudo-prefixed for a regular user facing a system unit) with D-112's lock read answering the unit-less machine (a live holder passes as the foreground run naming its pid, absent or stale fails with the install/run steps); port occupancy through a real ephemeral bind of the configured `host:port` (no `ss`/`lsof` subprocess — D-107's well-known-path reasoning; in-use passes only when the running service holds it, a refusal fails with the unprivileged-port advice, the unbindable address errors fail as configuration defects, and a privileged run binding a port below 1024 fails because the unprivileged owner cannot — the review round's caveat);
