§15.1's network/authentication consistency — valid configurations render the loopback/remote × auto/required × password classification (the parser already refuses the inconsistent ones), invalid ones get the D-101 placeholder-hash probe (via the additively exported `withPlaceholderHash`) through `parseConfigWithEnv`, so the password-pending state fails with its precise `quotameter passwd` remedy while any further defect stays the Configuration row's list; and the system clock through `timedatectl show --property=NTPSynchronized --property=NTP` parsed as `Key=value` (never the locale-dependent human table; a local bounded-capture runner with a kill-timeout, because `spawnServiceCommand` ignores stdout by design and a wedged D-Bus must not hang the diagnostic) — unsynchronized or synced-but-NTP-inactive fails with `sudo timedatectl set-ntp true`, an absent timedatectl is honestly unavailable. **`--raw <account>`** (§7.4, D-067) appends, after the checks, the account's retained sanitized payloads through the task-103 read path (`readRawDiagnostics` + `renderRawDiagnostics`) over the same read-only database handle — the CLI-only exposure the raw-response gate enforces structurally; the section is informational (never changes the exit code), a blank account id is a handler failure naming the correct spelling, a missing database or an unknown account renders its honest sentence (the database is the source — payloads may outlive a removed account's config entry), a failing read renders its failure sentence instead of aborting the report, and the handle is released in a `finally` on every path (the review round's P1 — an appendix must not be able to lose the report). The done-condition is pinned executably: `test/unit/cli-doctor.spec.ts` drives the full matrix seam-by-seam (every check × every D-119 status, the renderer byte-pinned, the timedatectl/quick-check/bind-error/classifier tables, the production opener against real directories, the real-socket bind probe, and §15.4's marker discipline — the configured key and hash never rendered, a key a contract-violating provider echoes back scrubbed to `[REDACTED]`) and `test/integration/cli-doctor.spec.ts` runs the real launcher — the bare machine (exit 1, the init and install/run next steps), the healthy machine over a real migrated SQLite database with a real §5.6 lock naming a live child (exit 0, the foreground pid in the report, the seeded §7.4 payload invisible without `--raw`), the env-key residual, the seeded `--raw` payload, the invalid configuration with its passwd remedy, and `--help`; `cli-dispatch.spec.ts` drops `doctor` from the still-stubbed list and `cli-raw-diagnostics.spec.ts`'s dispatch pin now reaches the real handler.

- `quotameter passwd` (task 144, spec §5.2/§15.2; decision D-118): the new `src/cli/passwd.ts` replaces the top-level `passwd` dispatch stub — the command that sets or changes the web password, composed of landed layers plus one new module, the §15.2 hash encoding. **The encoding** lives in the new `src/services/password-hash.ts` (the half of the future auth service the schema's `auth.passwordHash` docblock already assigns it): `hashPassword(password)` derives through `node:crypto`'s async scrypt with a fresh 16-byte random salt per hash, a 64-byte key, and the documented work parameters `N=65536, r=8, p=1` (frozen and exported as `SCRYPT_PARAMETERS`, with the explicit 128 MiB `maxmem` Node's 32 MiB default would refuse), and encodes self-describingly as `scrypt$N=65536,r=8,p=1,keylen=64$<salt base64>$<hash base64>` — the parameters travel with the hash, so task 153's verifier parses them from the stored string and a future cost raise never invalidates stored hashes. **The command is interactive-only** (§5.2 declares no options, and a `--password` value flag would put the secret in the process listing — the §15.4 rule that kept `--api-key` out of init): the password is asked twice through the prompter's muted `askSecret`, a blank/too-short first answer and a mismatching confirmation each draw one secret-free stderr complaint and restart the pair (up to three attempts, passwd(1) style; the minimum is the D-022 boundary's own exported `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH`, validated on the raw untrimmed value), and closed input or Ctrl-C cancels with nothing changed. **The write is never privileged and never clobbers**: D-105's owner resolution plus D-108's owner-scoped directories locate the configuration; a run with privileges held is refused to the owner's regular account before anything is asked (the atomic write replaces the inode, so a root write would leave the owner's file root-owned `0600` and unusable for the account §5.1 requires the service to run as); D-101's detection classifies the file (absent → refusal pointing at `quotameter init`, invalid → problems verbatim with nothing changed, existing → the D-022 `validateSettings` boundary merges the hash over the canonical base and re-validates, so what is written is exactly what the loader accepts — including completing the §15.1 password-pending state init leaves behind, which the service then starts on); and D-101's `sameDetectionState` re-read guard runs before any effect. **Fail-secure ordering (§15.2 "changing the password revokes all sessions")**: sessions are revoked through the repository's `deleteAllSessions` (a read-write connection of the owner's data directory, behind an existence probe that fails closed — only `ENOENT` reads as absence, so an unsearchable data directory is a loud failure that aborts with the password unchanged, never a skipped revocation reading "no sessions"; review round P2) **before** the configuration write; a failed revocation aborts with the password unchanged, and the safe-direction residual (revoked, then an unwritable configuration) is reported exactly: sessions revoked, password unchanged, run passwd again. The success report distinguishes set from change, names the file the scrypt hash lives in, states the revoked-session count (an empty sessions table reads like no database at all — "No stored sessions to revoke."), and — when D-112's lock read finds a live holder — carries the reload advisory (`quotameter reload`; `auth.passwordHash` is live-on-reload by D-026's classification, so a running service keeps authenticating with the old password until it reloads). The done-condition is pinned executably with a marker password asserted absent from every stdout/stderr line in every scenario the marker flows through and from the written file, whose `auth.passwordHash` is `scrypt$`-shaped — in `test/unit/password-hash.spec.ts` (format, salt randomness, the known-answer recomputation from the exported constants), `test/unit/cli-passwd.spec.ts` (the full seam-driven matrix), and `test/integration/cli-passwd.spec.ts` (the real launcher: real piped prompter, real scrypt, a real migrated SQLite database whose seeded sessions are gone after the run).

- `quotameter logs` (task 143, spec §5.2/§16; decision D-116): the new `src/cli/service-logs.ts` replaces the top-level `logs` dispatch stub — the convenient `journalctl` wrapper §16 names as the diagnostics surface for the Pino JSON logs journald collects, composed of landed layers plus one new mechanism, the streaming passthrough. **Scope follows the installed unit** (D-110's disk discovery, the 140 rule): a system unit runs `journalctl --no-pager … -u quotameter.service`, a user unit `journalctl --user … -u quotameter.service`; both installed is the 139 mistake — D-108's privileges rule selects and the shared cross-unit note (now exported from `service-control.ts`) goes to stderr **before** the stream, where a post-follow line would be lost under the entries. **Reading is unprivileged** (D-105's vocabulary names journalctl queries service-owner operations): the system journal is read in-process whatever this invocation's privileges are — an OS-level refusal renders the exact `sudo journalctl --no-pager --output=short-precise -u quotameter.service` remedy and names the `systemd-journal`/`adm` access groups — and a privileged run facing a user unit is refused to the regular account (root's `--user` query would filter root's journal, never the owner's). The 140/142 capability gates mirror over: no systemd means no journal at all (journald is part of it), and an unreachable user manager gets the exact `journalctl --user -u quotameter.service` command for the next login session. **Foreground-run honesty** through D-112's lock read (`readServiceLock` + `isProcessAlive`), consulted only on the nothing-installed path: a live lock holder is a foreground `quotameter run`, which has **no journal** — the guidance says so plainly, names the pid, and explains the messages live on the terminal the run was started from; an installed unit's journal is read regardless of running state (journals persist across stops), so the lock is not consulted there, and an unreadable/invalid lock costs one stderr line beside the not-installed steps, never the steps themselves. **The convenience defaults (D-116 — §5.2/§16 name none)**: `--no-pager` always (the wrapper keeps the user's scrollback), `--lines=100` default (`-n/--lines` takes a whole count ≥ 1 or `all`, the `--restart-sec` grammar), `--output=short-precise` default (`-o/--output` validates against a closed enum of journalctl's stable human-facing formats — a typo is an immediate self-explaining exit 1, not journalctl's own error), and `-f/--follow` opt-in. **The passthrough runner** `runJournalctlPassthrough` (new — `spawnServiceCommand` ignores stdout, unusable for a viewer): spawns the built command with piped stdio, forwards stdout chunks **raw** to `process.stdout` as they arrive (no line reassembly — follow mode is a live stream), captures stderr bounded (the house `STDERR_CAPTURE_CAP`, now shared additively from `service-setup.ts` beside `firstDiagnosticLine`) for the one-line failure detail, and installs SIGINT/SIGTERM forwarders that signal the child and wait — a Ctrl-C ends a follow as **success** (the `docker logs -f` convention), the forwarders are removed when the child closes, and a resolved-promise's first value makes the error/close arms race-free. journalctl missing is detected through the spawn error arm (ENOENT), rendering "journalctl is not available" with the ships-with-systemd explanation — no PATH probe. The review round (deepseek, REQUEST_CHANGES with 1 P1 / 2 P2 / 6 P3) sharpened the passthrough and the failure guidance, all folded in before landing: a closed output pipe (`quotameter logs | head` — EPIPE on `process.stdout`) is caught by run-time stream-error listeners and ends the run with the same stop-early semantics as a Ctrl-C (child terminated, exit 0) instead of crashing on an unhandled stream error (the P1; non-EPIPE stream errors become the failure detail and kill the child); `write`-reported backpressure pauses the child's stdout until the sink drains — `--lines=all` over a slow pipe cannot queue the whole journal in memory — with an exit-time resume guaranteeing `close` on a paused stream; a **second** forwarded signal escalates to SIGKILL so a wedged child cannot hold the terminal hostage; the stderr capture is sliced chunk-exactly to the cap (the pure `appendCappedStderrChunk`, table-tested) instead of overshooting by one chunk; a non-empty captured stderr is flushed on a successful exit (journalctl's own warnings must not vanish; failures keep quoting the first line); and the failed-read remedy is rebuilt from the vector that ran, so the user's `-f`/`-n`/`-o` flags carry into the suggested `sudo journalctl …`/`journalctl --user …` command (the raw byte path is also documented as the one exception to the `CliOutput` line contract, in `output.ts`'s docblock and the README's output-contract section). One P3 was declined with reasons: a rejecting injected `runViewer` seam propagates to the dispatcher's exit-1 failure arm rather than being caught in the handler — exactly the landed 140/141/142 shape (their `runCommand`/`validate` seams are equally unguarded); the unused `viewerThrows` harness arm the finding also flagged was removed. **Exit codes (D-116)**: 0 exactly when journalctl ran and exited 0 or was interrupted through a forwarded signal (no chatter either way — the stream is the product, and `out`/`err` stay empty on success); 1 for every honest failure with the failure on stderr and actionable steps on stdout. Done-condition evidence: the scope × capability × viewer-outcome matrix (nothing installed × five lock states including the live foreground run, system/user/both × manager gates, viewer ok/interrupted/spawn-failed/ran-failed with the exact command in the failure line, flags rendered into byte-pinned vectors, owner problems/throw, unusable home) is seam-driven in `test/unit/cli-service-logs.spec.ts` beside the parser tables, the bounded-capture helper table, and the real-child passthrough tests (stdout forwarding order and split-line content exactness, the exit-status and first-stderr-line detail arms, the stderr cap and the success-exit stderr flush, the SIGKILL-without-forwarding arm, the missing-binary arm, the EPIPE-as-interrupted and stream-error arms, the backpressure gate with its drain replacement, the second-signal SIGKILL escalation, forwarder and stream-listener removal restoring the counts); the real-launcher routes — nothing installed (exit 1, exact stderr lead, install steps), a real §5.6 lock naming a live U-001 child (the foreground-run guidance names the pid), `--help` documenting all three flags, and the follow-mode interrupt through a driver child running the real runner (readiness handshake, SIGINT to the driver forwarded to the grandchild, marker written, `{"ok":true,"interrupted":true}` outcome, exit 0) — are pinned in `test/integration/cli-service-logs.spec.ts`; `cli-dispatch.spec.ts` drops `logs` from the still-stubbed list.

- Required custom-header and JSON-content-type checks for state-changing API calls (task 150, spec §15.3, §13; decision D-117): every `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` under `/api` must now carry the dedicated nonstandard header **`X-Quotameter-Request`** (single-sited as the exported `API_MUTATION_HEADER_NAME` in `src/server/api/security.ts` — the future UI client and every §13 mutation consumer set this one constant), and a request that carries a body must type it `Content-Type: application/json` (the base media type compared exactly, before the first `;`, trimmed and lowercased — charset parameters are the parser's business; `+json` suffixes and `text/json` are not the type). The guard is the second §15.3 leg on the task-149 seam: the pure, fail-closed `classifyCustomHeaderRequest` over `{method, customHeader, contentType, contentLength, transferEncoding}` plus the `customHeaderGuard` `onRequest` hook the `/api` scope registers in `src/server/api/index.ts` **strictly after the Origin guard**, so a cross-origin mutation always answers the Origin verdict (D-115's layering: nothing downstream is consulted for a request an earlier layer rejected) and only an Origin-passing mutation answers the header/content-type classes. **The resolved semantics (D-117)**: presence of the header with a non-empty value is the whole requirement — the value is deliberately uninterpreted, because a cross-origin browser cannot set _any_ non-safelisted header without a successful CORS preflight and CORS stays disabled (§13), which is also why a fixed literal would add no security; a missing or empty header answers `403 FORBIDDEN` with `details.reason: "missing-custom-header"` and an actionable message naming the header; a body typed anything else — including the three HTML form enctype values and a body with no Content-Type at all — answers `415 UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE` in the registry's default shape, **byte-identical to fastify's native media-type rejection**, so the 415 class keeps exactly one wire body regardless of which layer rejected (D-113's one-vocabulary rule). The content-type leg binds body-carrying requests only (derived in the hook from `Content-Length`/`Transfer-Encoding`): a bodiless mutation — `DELETE /api/accounts/:id`, logout — has no payload to type, and requiring the header unconditionally would make it inexpressible, verified live on the pinned runtime (fastify answers a bodiless request that _claims_ `application/json` with its own empty-body `400` `FST_ERR_CTP_EMPTY_JSON_BODY`); only the exact `0` reads bodiless — fastify treats `Content-Length: 00` as a body present, so the guard does too (fail closed) — and a bodiless request's stray `Content-Type` stays the transport's business (its default `text/plain` parser accepts the empty body; an unregistered type answers fastify's native 415 — the same envelope, byte-identical by design, so the guard's preemption covers body-carrying mutations while the native mapping stays live behind it); an unparsable or duplicate-joined content-length fails closed into the content-type check. The layered defense holds because the dedicated header — the unforgeable leg — applies to every mutation regardless of body. Like the Origin guard, the verdict consults exactly the five wire signals, never server state (mode-independent across auth modes and both static-serving modes, pinned), covers every route in the scope including the catch-all and method mismatches (a hostile client learns nothing about the route table), runs before any body parsing (a rejected mutation is never read; the transport classes still answer once the guard is satisfied — byte-pinned), logs nothing (§16), and echoes no client-controlled value (§15.4). The done-condition ("missing header or wrong content type is rejected") is pinned executably: `test/unit/api-custom-header-guard.spec.ts` holds the pure table (safe-method immunity, every rejection shape × all four methods, the body-carrying derivation incl. fail-closed unparsable lengths, charset/case/padding accepts, the joined-split-header arms) and `test/integration/api-custom-header.spec.ts` pins the wire through the full construction — byte-pinned 403/415 envelopes per reason with `no-store`, the lookalike-header rejections (`X-Quotameter-Requests`, `X-Quotameter`, `X-Requested-With`), pass-through evidence via the scope catch-all's 404 on all four methods bodied and bodiless, the Origin-guard precedence pin, the transport-class ordering pin (a `text/plain` body answers the guard's 415 before the parser; the same malformed body under `application/json` reaches the parser's 400), the empty-JSON-body transport fact, the static-mode twin, the bare-`/api` boundary, §15.4/§16 discipline — beside the landed suites' mutation arms (`api-origin`, `api-errors`, `api-health`, `server-http`), which now carry the dedicated header (bodiless) or header + JSON content type (bodied) exactly as the future §13 clients will.
- `quotameter status` (task 142, spec §5.2/§7.3; decision D-114): the new `src/cli/service-status.ts` replaces the top-level `status` dispatch stub — the report the §5.3 summaries, install outcomes, and control successes already direct users to, composed of landed layers only. **Each fact comes from the layer that owns it**: the installed scope from D-110's disk discovery (both unit kinds on disk renders as the mistake task 139 warns about, in the report's own Installed line); the running state from D-112's lock read (`readServiceLock` over the owner's data directory, liveness via `isProcessAlive` — run-mode independent, answering "running, at which pid?" for a systemd unit and a foreground `quotameter run` alike, where a manager query could only answer "installed"); the manager view as an **unprivileged** `systemctl [-–user] is-active quotameter.service` through task 137's command seam (a privileged run never asks a `--user` manager — root's is not the owner's, D-105 — and prints the regular-account step instead; systemd-less and session-less machines get honest `unknown` lines; and a non-zero exit carrying a stderr diagnostic — spawn failure or unreachable bus — renders `unknown (<diagnostic>)` and exits 1, never a false `not active`: only a command that ran and answers states the unit's activity); the account list from the service's own `loadConfigWithEnv` pipeline. **Per-account facts** run task 111's `deriveAccountStatus` (§7.3's vocabulary through `ACCOUNT_STATUS_LABELS`) over `readPollingRunEvidence` storage reads, with the two inputs a separate process cannot observe fixed to their honest proxies (D-114): the D-064 suspension passes `INITIAL_SUSPENSION_STATE` (the `auth_error`-run branch keeps `Auth rejected` truthful — D-070's recorded stance for exactly this consumer), and `configured` resolves `apiKeyEnv` against this invocation's environment (the D-112 residual stance for CLI-side mirrors of service-side reads). **§7.2's two most-recents** render independently: the last collection is the latest snapshot's §8.4 stamp, the last error the newest non-success run (six bounded, indexed outcome-filtered probes) with its outcome label and the stored message — sanitized by construction (D-048) and additionally passed through an exact-occurrence redaction of every §6.3 secret source (file-stored `apiKey` values, environment-supplied key values, and the password hash → `[REDACTED]`, §7.4's rule mirrored privately; the public `sanitizeRawResponse` is payload-shaped — JSON walk + 64 KiB cap). **§10 time discipline**: every timestamp renders through the clock module's zoned formatters in the configured display zone with the zone's name (`2025-11-01 13:26 (Europe/Berlin)`), never a machine-local reading; `now` enters through the injectable clock seam (D-021). **Storage**: `openStatusStore` opens an existing database only, **read-only** through the new additive storage sibling `openReadOnlyStorageConnection` (`node:sqlite`'s layer rule keeps the read-only knowledge inside `src/storage/`, pinned by a no-write/seam-precedence block in the connection suite) — the command never creates one (a read-only handle cannot, closing the probe-then-open window), never writes pages (no hot-journal recovery as another user under `sudo`), and still passes the §11.1 pragma verification because a quotameter database is already WAL (verified on the pinned runtime; the accepted residual: a crashed writer's hot journal needs a writer first and reads as an honest failure); an absent file is empty evidence (the §7.3 no-run paths answer Collecting/etc.), an unreadable one a stderr failure with the accounts section marked unavailable — and the existence probe runs before any storage module loads so `node:sqlite`'s ExperimentalWarning belongs to invocations that actually open a database, not to every launcher invocation through the dispatch table (the raw-diagnostics.ts discipline; without it every `--help` and stub exit would carry the warning on stderr). §6.3's env-only mode reports its provenance as `valid — from environment overrides, no configuration file`, never implying a file exists. **Exit codes (D-114)**: 0 exactly when every probe _answered_ — stopped, not installed, no accounts, and no data are states, not failures (a bare machine renders a full useful report and exits 0); 1 when a probe could not answer (owner problems, unusable home, unreadable/invalid lock, invalid configuration, storage failure) — the report still renders everything established, with the failure lines on stderr; the handler never throws. Done-condition evidence: the installed-scope × manager × running × configuration × storage matrix (system/user/both/neither, lock absent/live/stale/unreadable/invalid, manager active/inactive/unknown × reason, config valid/env-only/absent/invalid, store absent/open/failed/throwing, the manager's could-not-answer arm, §7.3 statuses Fresh/Stale/Collecting/Disabled/Not configured/Auth rejected/Format changed/Unreachable, the last-error selection and HTTP-status fallback, marker-secrets absent from every line and a marker seeded into a stored error rendering as `[REDACTED]`, §10 zone rendering with Asia/Kolkata against the UTC container) is seam-driven in `test/unit/cli-service-status.spec.ts` beside the pure-helper tables and the production opener against real directories (a corrupt database refused); the real-launcher routes — the bare machine (exit 0), the full report over a real configuration and a real migrated SQLite database, the invalid configuration (exit 1, service facts kept), and a real §5.6 lock naming a live pid — plus the in-process opener-over-seeded-database read are pinned in `test/integration/cli-service-status.spec.ts`; `cli-dispatch.spec.ts` drops `status` from the still-stubbed list.

- Origin and Fetch Metadata checks (task 149, spec §15.3, §13; decision D-115): every state-changing request (`POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`, §13's set) under `/api` is now screened by the Origin/Fetch-Metadata guard before any handler or body parser runs — the first of §15.3's request-security layers, active in every auth mode and both static-serving modes (the verdict consults exactly three request signals, never server state). **The new `src/server/api/security.ts`** is the §15.3 seam: the pure, fail-closed `classifyOriginRequest` over `{method, origin, secFetchSite, authority}`, the `originGuard` Fastify `onRequest` hook the `/api` scope registers in `src/server/api/index.ts` (it applies to every route in the scope including the catch-all, so unmatched and method-mismatched mutations are screened too), and the shared pieces task 150 (custom header/JSON content type, over the same `STATE_CHANGING_METHODS`) and 151 (Host allowlist, over the same `normalizeAuthority` yardstick) build on. **The D-115 verdict table**: safe methods pass untouched; a present `Origin` must parse as an http/https tuple origin (else `malformed-origin` — `file:`/`data:`/`mailto:` values carry no origin authority, and every empty-host http/https form is rejected by the URL parser itself, so no empty-host equality exists), must not be the literal `null` (else `opaque-origin`), and must carry the request's own `Host` authority after case normalization and a scheme-aware default-port strip — only the Origin scheme's own default (`:80` for http, `:443` for https) is stripped, so a browser's never-serialized default port and a proxy's explicitly written one compare equal while the opposite scheme's default stays identity (`http://a.example:443` never collapses onto a port-80 authority); a present `Sec-Fetch-Site` must be `same-origin` or `none` — `same-site` is rejected beside `cross-site` and malformed values (same-site ≠ same-origin: a sibling subdomain or another port is still a cross-origin mutation); both signals present must independently pass (a browser never lets them disagree); both absent passes, because only browsers emit them on mutations — the curl/CLI client, whose residual old-browser gap is closed by 150's mandatory custom header that no HTML form can set while CORS stays disabled. The Origin scheme is deliberately not compared: behind §15.6's documented TLS-terminating proxy the browser's Origin is `https://` while the internal hop is HTTP, and the `host:port` authority is the CSRF-relevant boundary (the deployment prerequisite — the proxy must forward the client-visible authority as `Host`; nginx needs `proxy_set_header Host $host`, Caddy preserves it — is recorded in D-115). **Rejection is the §13 envelope's `FORBIDDEN`** — the registry's registered CSRF class — with the stable message `Cross-origin request rejected` and a closed `details.reason` vocabulary (`opaque-origin`, `malformed-origin`, `origin-mismatch`, `cross-site-fetch`) that names the failed check without echoing attacker-controlled input (§15.4). Verified on the pinned fastify 5.12.7: the hook is async because fastify's hook runner advances on the returned promise (a sync hook that never calls `done` stalls the request), a throw from the scoped hook reaches the root-scope error handler, and `fastify.inject` defaults the `host` header to `localhost:80` — the authority normalization handles the default port on either side. The bare `/api` (no trailing segment) stays with the root not-found arm: no route exists there, so routing itself rejects the mutation. Done-condition evidence: `test/unit/api-origin-guard.spec.ts` holds the pure table (every `Sec-Fetch-Site` token × all four mutation methods, authority normalization incl. the scheme-aware default ports/IPv6/case with the opposite-scheme non-collapse pins, the non-http/empty-host Origin rejections, opaque/malformed/incomparable Origins, disagreement precedence, safe-method immunity, the joined-split-header arm) and `test/integration/api-origin.spec.ts` pins the wire through the full construction — byte-pinned 403 envelopes per reason with `no-store`, pass-through evidence via the scope catch-all's 404 (a class the guard can never produce), the disagreeing-headers matrix, the guard-before-body-parsing ordering (a cross-origin mutation with a malformed or over-limit body answers 403, a same-origin control answers the 400/413 transport classes), mode-independence with the static fixture (SPA deep links still serve), the bare-`/api` boundary, and §16's zero-records discipline for rejections; both suites green with `make check`. Review round (deepseek, 0 P0 / 0 P1 / 2 P2 / 5 P3): all seven findings accepted and fixed in the same commit.

- `quotameter reload` (task 141, spec §5.2/§6.4; decision D-112): the new `src/cli/service-reload.ts` replaces the top-level `reload` dispatch stub — the command that triggers §6.4's change application without a restart and answers the done-condition's question (accepted or rejected). **The running service is discovered through the §5.6 single-instance lock, single-sited through the landed module**: the handler reads `lockFilePath(<owner's data directory>)` (imported read-only from `src/server/single-instance.ts`; the payload parse mirrors the lock module's private parser, and the liveness probe is its exported `isProcessAlive`) — the lock exists exactly while a service instance holds the data directory, for **every run mode** (a systemd unit and a foreground `quotameter run` alike), and answers both "running?" and "at which pid?" in one read, where a `systemctl kill`-scoped alternative would only reach installed units and probe _installed_, not _running_. The owner-scoped §6.1 directories reuse task 139's `ownerUnitDirectories` (now additively exported — `HOME` forced to the owner's passwd home under sudo, `QUOTAMETER_*` overrides honored), so a `sudo quotameter reload` finds the owner's lock, never root's. **The accepted/rejected oracle is a pre-flight validation (D-112)**: the command re-runs `loadConfigWithEnv` — the exact pipeline function the service reloader calls on SIGHUP (D-023/D-026) — over the same §6.1 configuration path in its own process; a rejected file is reported with every pipeline problem verbatim (one prefixed stderr line each, D-018 field-addressed and value-free) and **no SIGHUP is sent**, because §6.2 pins the service-side consequence (keep the last valid configuration) as a guaranteed no-op; a valid file is signalled (`process.kill(pid, 'SIGHUP')`, errno classified by a pure classifier: `ESRCH` → the service exited meanwhile, `EPERM` → the sudo/owner-account steps) and the success report carries the §6.4 advisory — live settings apply without a restart, `server.host`/`server.port`/`auth.mode` need `quotameter restart` — without fabricating the service's own applied-vs-pending-restart classification (only its reloader can diff against the in-memory configuration; the authoritative record is the service's log line, surfaced by `quotameter logs`, task 143). Not-running states are §5.2's helpful next step (absent lock, or a stale lock from a dead pid — the crash leftover §5.6's next start clears automatically — both print the start/foreground guidance), and an unreadable or invalid lock file is a loud failure with the lock module's own remedy wording; exit 0 exactly when the configuration validated and SIGHUP was delivered. Done-condition evidence: the handler matrix (owner problems/throw, unusable home, absent/stale/unreadable/invalid lock, rejected-with-problems and the no-signal contract, accepted, all three signal-failure arms, discovery-before-validation ordering) is seam-driven in `test/unit/cli-service-reload.spec.ts` beside the payload-parse table and the production seams against real directories (including a kernel-genuine `EACCES`) and real child processes (delivery, `ESRCH` for a reaped pid); the real-launcher routes in `test/integration/cli-service-reload.spec.ts` pin all three outcomes end-to-end — not-running guidance, a rejected configuration whose problems print and whose live signal-holding child receives **no** SIGHUP, and the accepted route where the child's SIGHUP marker appears after the launcher exits 0 (U-001 readiness handshake before any signal, D-087-generous marker wait); `cli-dispatch.spec.ts` drops `reload` from the still-stubbed list.

- The common API error envelope (task 148, spec §13, §15.4, §15.5, §16; decision D-113): every error the server produces — thrown application errors, fastify's schema-validation and transport failures, and every 404 — now answers the one §13 shape `{"error":{"code":"…","message":"…","details":…}}` with **stable, machine-readable codes** from the new registry `src/schemas/api/error.ts`: `VALIDATION` (400), `UNAUTHENTICATED` (401), `FORBIDDEN` (403), `NOT_FOUND` (404), `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` (413), `URI_TOO_LONG` (414), `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE` (415), `INTERNAL` (500), each with its HTTP status and a default leak-free message; the same module carries the closed TypeBox envelope schema (`additionalProperties: false` at both levels, the code field a literal union pinned to the registry keys by test so the two tables cannot drift) that task 163 generates OpenAPI from. The behavior lives in `src/server/api/error.ts`: **`ApiError`** is the throw-side extension point the later Stage-12 tasks plug into (auth modes 152, Origin/header checks 149/150, rate limiting 156, endpoint handlers 159+ — throw it from a handler or hook and the envelope carries it, registry status and default message included, `message`/`details` overridable for §15.1's "403 with instructions" shape); **`classifyApiError`** is the pure trust boundary between "anything thrown" and the wire — `ApiError` passes through, fastify's schema-validation errors (`error.validation`, verified on the pinned runtime 5.12.7) project onto `VALIDATION` with `details.validation` entries of `{ where, path, message }` (client- and schema-derived text only), the verified native fastify transport codes (`FST_ERR_CTP_BODY_TOO_LARGE` → `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, `FST_ERR_CTP_INVALID_MEDIA_TYPE` → `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE`, `FST_ERR_CTP_INVALID_JSON_BODY`/`FST_ERR_CTP_EMPTY_JSON_BODY`/`FST_ERR_CTP_INVALID_CONTENT_LENGTH` → `VALIDATION`, and the router-level bounds `FST_ERR_BAD_URL` → `VALIDATION` and `FST_ERR_MAX_PARAM_LENGTH` → `URI_TOO_LONG`) map onto the registry (the 413 and body-parse classes reach the handler even for unmatched routes; the 415 class needs a matched route, an unmatched one answers the 404 envelope first), and **everything else resolves to `INTERNAL`** — unknown errors, foreign `statusCode`s, non-Error throwables — with the generic message, so an unknown-provenance message (a path, a secret) never reaches the wire; the strict-classification rule is the §13/§15.4 message discipline, and adding a class is a coordinated registry+schema+test change. `buildHttpServer` registers the handler at **root scope** (one error shape for API and non-API failures alike — the not-found handler's 404 branch joins it, replacing task 146's interim verbatim fastify-default body D-109 documented; an unmatched route is not an error in fastify, so the 404 arm lives in `setNotFoundHandler` with `details:{method,path}` — the client's own request, query stripped), and every envelope response carries §15.5 `Cache-Control: no-store` and the framework's default JSON content type. The "one error shape" claim is a property of the construction's full wiring, review-verified on the wire: three error paths bypass a root error handler alone, and each is closed — the same handler is wired as fastify's **`frameworkErrors`** callback (router-level rejections, e.g. a malformed percent-escape path like `/api/%zz`, otherwise get fastify's hardcoded non-envelope body carrying the unstable native code and no `no-store`); post-landing verification against a built UI then exposed a fourth bypass — find-my-way reports an over-long route parameter (the 414 class) only when the failed match leaves no other candidate, so the SPA wildcard swallowed it into a plain 404 in static mode while API-only mode answered 414 — closed by the `/api` scope's own **catch-all route** (`unmatchedApiClassification`, registered for every method so it outranks the static wildcard for `/api` paths): unmatched requests answer the NOT_FOUND envelope, or URI_TOO_LONG when any decoded path segment exceeds the router's parameter bound (`API_MAX_PARAM_LENGTH`, single-sited with the construction's `maxParamLength` option — the same yardstick find-my-way applies to parameters); `@fastify/static` registers inside a **containment scope** whose local handler maps the `send` library's client rejections (a `%00` byte in the path — foreign 403/404s with no framework code) onto `FORBIDDEN`/`NOT_FOUND` envelopes with generic messages, instead of the strict rule's false 500 INTERNAL plus one error-level log record per request; and the SPA fallback requires **`index.html` itself** to exist, so a partial build degrades to the envelope 404 rather than `sendFile`'s ENOENT (which fastify answers with a plain-text basic 404 plus a warn line). The INTERNAL envelope is generic by construction: `ApiError` refuses `message`/`details` for the INTERNAL code at the throw site and the classifier re-forces the registry shape, so a hand-written internal message cannot reach the wire. **§16 logging discipline**: only the `INTERNAL` class logs — one `request.log.error({ err })` record through the redacting logger's Layer-A serializer (type/message/stack of the real cause, server-side only); the 4xx classes log nothing. The done-condition (validation, auth, not-found, internal — tested stable codes) is pinned byte-exactly in `test/integration/api-errors.spec.ts` through the full task-146 construction (`fastify.inject`; test-only routes registered before the boot — one through an encapsulated plugin scope — drive the validation, auth-class, and internal arms; the D-111 test-only-route precedent) — the 404 envelope (method mismatches, query forms, bare `/api`, the `/apiary` boundary and SPA fallback intact, API-only mode), schema-validation details, the 413/415/parse-class transports, the router-level `frameworkErrors` arms (`%zz` and a malformed-UTF-8 path → `VALIDATION` in both modes, and the over-long-segment → `URI_TOO_LONG` **mode matrix**: API-only and static-fixture, GET and POST/DELETE, param-route and no-param paths, plus the decoded-segment yardstick edge — a raw-180/decoded-60 escape still routes), the static containment arms (a non-API `%00` path → `FORBIDDEN` through the containment handler, an API-prefixed one → the same 404 envelope in both modes since the catch-all owns it, and a partial build → the envelope 404 — all with zero log records), a plugin-scope error reaching the root handler, `ApiError`-thrown 401/403/404, and the generic 500 whose body provably carries none of the thrown error's message while the sink holds exactly the one error record with it; `test/unit/api-error.spec.ts` pins the pure classification matrix (including the malformed-throwable and foreign-statusCode arms) and `test/unit/api-error-schema.spec.ts` the closed-envelope Ajv contract with the literals≡registry drift guard; the task-146/147 suites' interim 404 pins are updated to the envelope as D-109/D-111 scheduled.

- `quotameter start` / `stop` / `restart` (task 140, spec §5.2; decision D-110): the new `src/cli/service-control.ts` replaces the three top-level dispatch stubs with §5.2's service-control handlers — the commands the §5.3 summaries and task 139's install outputs already direct users to. **The manager scope follows the installed unit, not the invocation's privileges**: the handlers discover what exists through task 139's unit-file exists-seam — an installed system unit (`/etc/systemd/system/quotameter.service`) means system-scope `systemctl <verb> quotameter.service`, an installed user unit (under the owner's passwd home) means `systemctl --user <verb> quotameter.service`; a regular user facing an installed system unit gets the exact sudo-prefixed steps (`sudo quotameter start` plus the direct `sudo systemctl start quotameter.service`) instead of a user-manager attempt at a system service, and a `sudo quotameter start` facing an installed user unit is directed to the regular account (D-105: a privileged run never reaches into the user manager — its `--user` manager would be root's, not the owner's). Only when **both** unit kinds are installed (the state install warns is a mistake) do the invocation's privileges select (held → system, regular → user, D-108's rule), with install's cross-unit stderr warning after a successful command. Held privileges execute the system verb in-process (D-105's start/stop-system-unit vocabulary; `restart` is the pair against the same manager, no new vocabulary entry); unavailable managers are honest exit-1 failures with exact steps — a unit file on a systemd-less machine names the file and prints the foreground alternative (with the owner-account never-root note for privileged runs, task 137's shape), and an unreachable user manager prints the verb's exact session command for the next login. **Nothing installed is §5.2's helpful next step**: a stderr lead (`quotameter start: no systemd service is installed — nothing to start`) and stdout naming both install modes with their meanings (`sudo quotameter service install` — system service; `quotameter service install` — user service, no sudo needed), `quotameter init` as the one-step alternative, and `quotameter run` as the foreground escape; exit 1 uniformly for all three verbs (`stop` with nothing installed is not a no-op success the way uninstall-when-absent is — these commands were asked to act on a service that does not exist). No `is-active` verification runs after a verb: the manager command's own exit status is the contract (install's `is-active` is §5.3 step 7's prescribed privileged-flow check, and `stop` would need it inverted; D-110). `src/cli/service-install.ts` gained exactly one additive export — `resolveOwnerForCommand`, its deps parameter widened to any seam set carrying `resolveOwner` (both task-139 call sites unchanged), the D-108 additive-sharing precedent — so the control handlers share the owner-resolution reporting instead of forking it. The full matrix (every verb × installed kind × privileges × manager availability, the both-installed selection, the unusable-home edges, owner-problem and broken-database gates, byte-for-byte renderer pins) is seam-driven in `test/unit/cli-service-control.spec.ts`, and the one route the systemd-less container really produces — nothing installed, exit 1, guidance naming the install command — is pinned through the real launcher in `test/integration/cli-service-control.spec.ts`.

- The minimal public health response (task 147, spec §13, §15.1, §15.5; decision D-111): unauthenticated `GET /api/health` answers `200` with the byte-exact literal `{"status":"ok"}` and nothing else — no versions, uptime, memory/pid, storage or database state, account data, errors, or timestamps can appear in the response, because its shape is enforced at the serialization layer: the route serializes through the new closed TypeBox contract `src/schemas/api/health.ts` (`Type.Object({ status: Type.Literal('ok') }, { additionalProperties: false })` — §13's "Define every request and response through TypeBox/JSON Schema", the same definition task 163 generates OpenAPI from), so the compiled serializer emits exactly the declared key and structurally drops any other — and the `const` literal emits `'ok'` regardless of the input value, so the wire output is invariant under any handler result (verified on the pinned runtime, fast-json-stringify 7.2.0) — the done-condition ("no extra system diagnostics leak before authentication") holds by construction, not by handler discipline; a dedicated Ajv contract test pins the closed-object property, and a serializer regression test drives a test-only route carrying the same schema with diagnostic-carrying, wrong-valued, and hostile-keyed handler results — every one serializing to the byte-exact literal. The route lives in `src/server/api/health.ts` and registers inside the task-146 `/api` scope (`src/server/api/index.ts`) — the first Stage-12 endpoint to join the seam, and the one §13 endpoint that stays public in every auth mode (§15.1): nothing on it consults credentials, and §13's "except the public health response" binds the request-security tasks (149–152) to keep it reachable unauthenticated. `Cache-Control: no-store` is applied route-scoped now (§15.5 "for API responses" — this response is one; task 158's global security-header pass later generalizes it alongside CSP/nosniff/no-referrer); the content type stays the framework's default `application/json; charset=utf-8` (the spec prescribes no exact value), pinned so a Fastify default change is seen. The authenticated-diagnostics half of §13's row ("may include process/database diagnostics") is deliberately not implemented — the spec's "may" makes it optional, task 147 is the minimal public response, and extending health with diagnostics is a future task's own decision (D-111). Routing edges are pinned: GET-only (other methods find no route and answer task 148's §13 error envelope, which replaced the interim pre-148 default 404 shape D-109 had documented — both tasks ship in this Unreleased block), the auto-exposed `HEAD` twin (Fastify's default `exposeHeadRoute`) answers headers-only with no body — nothing can leak through it — the path is slash-exact (`/api/health/` is a 404), and query strings are ignored (no diagnostic mode hides behind parameters). Evidence: `test/integration/api-health.spec.ts` (byte-pinned body, single-key parsed shape, headers, query and credential invariance, the full method-routing table, the HEAD twin, trailing-slash exactness, one real ephemeral loopback socket through the `http-listener` component, and no request-log lines at `warn` — §16 via D-109's gate) plus the schema contract suite `test/unit/api-health-schema.spec.ts`.
- `quotameter service install` / `service uninstall` (task 139, spec §5.2/§5.4; decision D-108): the new `src/cli/service-install.ts` plugs both §5.2 handlers into the `service` group of the dispatch table, making the command task 137's system-unit plan directs users to (`sudo quotameter service install`) real. **Idempotent by construction** (§5.4 "must regenerate the unit"): install-when-absent and install-when-present run one identical sequence — deterministic generator rewrite (fresh absolute Node/CLI paths after an upgrade), `daemon-reload`, `enable --now` (a no-op on an enabled service) — with a pre-existing unit file only adding a `(regenerated)` marker and a restart note (a running service keeps the previous unit until `systemctl [–user] restart`); uninstall-when-absent is a `nothing to do` success with zero side effects. Routing reuses task 137's §5.3 step-7 matrix verbatim: privileges held on a systemd machine manage the **system** unit with D-105's privileged operations running in-process (write/remove `/etc/systemd/system/quotameter.service` with `User=<owner>`, `daemon-reload`, `enable --now`/`disable --now`, and the `systemctl is-active` verification §5.3 step 7 prescribes for the privileged route); a regular user manages the **user** unit entirely unprivileged (write under the owner's home, `systemctl --user`, task 138's detected linger guidance on the installed/completion outcomes). The system unit's `ReadWritePaths` resolve through the §6.1 precedence with `HOME` forced to the owner's passwd home — a privileged run never pins root's paths into a unit that runs as the owner (sudo's default environment stripping yields the owner's XDG defaults; `sudo -E`/`sudo QUOTAMETER_CONFIG_DIR=…` overrides are honored as explicit declarations), and the owner's home passes the shared injection guard. `--restart-sec <seconds>` is §5.4's configurable `RestartSec` made real, defaulting to D-106's `INIT_RESTART_SEC_SECONDS` by import (plain decimal whole seconds ≥ 1, the generator's own bound, problems JSON-quoting the value). Unavailable routes are honest failures with exact copy-pasteable steps (§5.2's helpful-next-step rule): install on a systemd-less machine refuses with the foreground alternative — privileged runs are pointed at the owner's regular account (§5.1 never-root) — and an unreachable user manager still writes the unit file and prints the session commands with linger guidance; uninstall on manager-less machines removes the file without manager commands and says so. Exit codes follow the command's own promise: install 0 ⟺ written + reloaded + enabled/started (system route: `is-active` confirms); uninstall 0 ⟺ file gone/absent and every executed manager command clean — a failed `disable`/`daemon-reload` is reported but never blocks the file removal. Cross-unit states warn (stderr note on install, the exact removal hint on a no-op uninstall) without blocking; owner problems (D-105) and unsafe owner names (D-106's guard) fail fast like init's; neither handler throws, and no configuration is read, written, or required — the command is independent of `init` structurally. Done-condition evidence: the four-corner matrix (install/uninstall × absent/present) on both unit kinds, every manager-command failure path, both unavailable routes, the owner gate, the `--restart-sec` table — seam-driven in `test/unit/cli-service-install.spec.ts` — plus the systemd-less routes the container really produces (refusal exit 1 + no files written, no-op uninstall exit 0, the flag failure, the help surface) through the real launcher in `test/integration/cli-service-install.spec.ts`; `cli-dispatch.spec.ts` drops both paths from the still-stubbed list. `service-setup.ts` gains only two additive exports (`failureLine`, `thrownMessage`) shared with the new module; task 137's suite passes unchanged.
- Fastify server construction (task 146, spec §3, §13, §15.5, §16; decision D-109): the new `src/server/http.ts` builds the server shell and wraps it as the `http-listener` lifecycle component the application shell starts and stops. **Construction set** — `fastify` with `loggerInstance` set to the redacting logger's Layer-A-protected Pino instance (§3 "Pino through Fastify"; every Fastify record flows through D-025's redaction) and §16's request-logging gate via a `RequestLoggingGate` (`LogController` subclass, the replacement for the deprecated top-level `disableRequestLogging` option — every per-request line appears only at `debug`, zero at `warn`/`info`, including the closing-server 503 record the stock controller always emits); the §15.5 64 KiB `bodyLimit` (413 above, exactly-64 KiB bodies pass); three explicit §15.5 timeouts — `connectionTimeout` 300 s (the socket-inactivity bound, applied by Fastify as `server.setTimeout`, observable as `server.timeout` — the review round corrected the first draft's "dead option" claim, probed on the wrong property), `requestTimeout` 60 s (receiving an entire request, the slow-client bound), and `keepAliveTimeout` 72 s (idle keep-alive); `forceCloseConnections: 'idle'` so idle keep-alive sockets cannot hold the close; `@fastify/cookie` registered (§3's enabling plugin for the §13 auth endpoints — parsing only, flags are task 157's per-response behavior); and `@fastify/static` serving `<package root>/ui/dist` with the §14.1 SPA fallback (GET deep links outside the exact `/api` route boundary — `/api`, `/api/…`, query forms — load `index.html`, so `/apiary` stays a UI deep link; unknown API paths keep the default 404 shape until task 148's envelope; a missing build directory degrades to an API-only server with one `warn` line — the frontend build is task 165's deliverable). `@fastify/helmet` and `@fastify/rate-limit` are **declared dependencies but unregistered**: their §15.5/§15.2 behavior is tasks 158/156's deliverable (pinned by test so an accidental early registration cannot pre-empt it). **Route seam** — `src/server/api/index.ts` is the registered `/api` plugin scope every Stage-12 endpoint (147+) joins; no endpoint ships here. **Lifecycle** — `createHttpServerComponent` builds the instance eagerly (wiring mistakes fail before startup), `start` listens (port 0 = ephemeral), and `stop` realizes §5.6's bounded in-flight-request wait inside the component: it races `fastify.close()` against the ten-second bound single-sited from `shutdown.ts` (same validation as the graceful drain) and forces completion via `closeAllConnections()` on expiry — D-109's resolution of the tracker-vs-close overlap (the http component stops before the shared drain in the documented registration order, and `fastify.close()` already serializes the request wait, so the shared tracker stays the polling-side mechanism; `shutdown.ts`/`app.ts` comments corrected to match); stop is idempotent while succeeding (one close across repeated and concurrent stops — pinned by close-count spies) and retryable after rejecting (U-018's component contract). Done-condition evidence in `test/integration/server-http.spec.ts` (18 tests; http.ts and server/api measure 100/100/100/100): `fastify.inject` answers without sockets; `QuotameterApp` and `runServer` start and stop a real ephemeral-port listener on 127.0.0.1 cleanly (exit 0 after SIGTERM); each construction item — plugins, body limit, timeouts, static serving, route seam — is pinned by a test; and the forced-close path is exercised against a hung handler over a real loopback socket with arrival made causal by a deferred resolved inside the route (D-087). CORS stays disabled (no `access-control-allow-origin` on any response, §13). Production dependencies `fastify@^5.12.7`, `@fastify/static@^10.1.8`, `@fastify/cookie@^11.1.5`, `@fastify/helmet@^13.2.0`, `@fastify/rate-limit@^11.2.1` added; prod `npm audit` stays below the high gate (two moderates in `@fastify/static`'s glob chain, its pattern-serving feature unused — we serve a fixed root).
- detected user-service linger guidance in `init` (task 138, spec §5.3 step 7's user-unit bullet; decision D-107): the new `src/cli/service-linger.ts` replaces task 137's unconditional linger note with **state-driven** guidance on exactly the outcomes where a user unit exists to keep alive — the installed user-unit outcome and both manual completion-steps fallbacks (unreachable user manager, failed enable command). **Detection reads the persistent linger flag file** `/var/lib/systemd/linger/<owner>`: where `loginctl enable-linger` records the state and where logind reads it back — the `Linger` D-Bus property of a user object ("shows whether lingering is enabled for this user", org.freedesktop.login1(5), the property `loginctl show-user --property=Linger` prints) is exactly this file's presence, and "the user linger state is persistently stored on disk" — the same well-known-path idiom the §5.3 step 1 capability probe uses (`/run/systemd/system`): no subprocess, no stdout parsing, no loginctl-in-PATH dependency, and no version-dependent `show-user` behavior for never-logged-in users. **The probe is tri-state and fails safe** (one `statSync`, errno classification): flag file present → `enabled`; `ENOENT` (missing file, or a missing flag directory — no user has a flag) → `disabled`; any other stat error (`EACCES` on a hardened path, …) → `unknown`, and **unknown is never treated as enabled** — it prints the same guidance as `disabled`, led by "The linger status for `<owner>` could not be determined, so it is treated as disabled." (advising `sudo loginctl enable-linger <owner>` when linger is already on is an idempotent no-op; withholding the advice when linger is off would silently stop the service at the owner's logout — the exact §5.3 consequence the guidance exists to prevent). **The rendered matrix**: `disabled` keeps task 137's sentence verbatim — `User services stop when their owner logs out. To keep the service running after logout or reboot, run:` with the exact `sudo loginctl enable-linger <owner>` indented on its own line; `enabled` prints `Linger is already enabled for <owner> — the user service keeps running after logout and reboot.` with no command. The step probes once through the injectable seam (`ServiceSetupSeams.probeLinger`, production `probeUserLinger`, wired from the new `InitDeps.probeUserLinger`) immediately after the successful unit write; the outputs where no user service can linger (systemd absent, the write-failure foreground fallback, the system-unit plan) carry no linger note and the probe never runs there (pinned executably with throwing probes), so 137's route matrix is untouched. The owner's name reaches the flag-file path only after the step's POSIX-grammar guard (`unsafeOwnerNameProblem`). Evidence: `test/unit/cli-service-linger.spec.ts` pins the classification matrix, the path composition, the real-filesystem classifier (present / `ENOENT` / `EACCES` through a 000-mode directory under the unprivileged container uid), the guidance text per state, and the production default's deterministic `disabled` on the systemd-less container; the extended `test/unit/cli-service-setup.spec.ts` drives on/off/unknown × installed/completion outcomes through `runServiceSetupStep`, the no-linger routes, and the init-seam wiring, with task 137's renderer assertions now passing the detected state.
- the `init` service-setup branch layer (task 137, spec §5.3 step 7; decision D-106): the new `src/cli/service-setup.ts` routes §5.3 step 7 for a resolved owner context (task 136's layer, D-105) against probed machine capabilities and owns the two fallback paths — **the no-sudo user-unit installation** and **the exact manual steps when service installation is unavailable** — wired into `init` as the step that follows a fresh run's configuration write (the `Service setup follows.` placeholder is gone). **Owner problems fail fast**: `init` resolves the owner first (§5.3 step 1's rootness check, before any detection, questions, or writes), printing every problem verbatim as its own `quotameter init:` stderr line, exit 1, nothing written and no prompter constructed — a root-without-sudo run no longer collects a configuration into root's XDG home. **Capabilities are pure path probes**: `systemd` = `/run/systemd/system` exists (the marker systemd itself creates), `userManager` = an own, non-empty `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` whose `systemd` runtime dir exists (`systemctl --user`'s own reachability signal; inherited/empty values never count). **The route matrix**: privileges held + systemd → the system-unit route (rendered as a plan naming `/etc/systemd/system/quotameter.service` with `User=<owner>`, daemon-reload, enable --now, the `is-active`/`/api/health` verification, and `sudo quotameter service install` as the completing §5.2 command — the privileged operations themselves are task 139's machinery and are deliberately not executed here); no sudo + systemd + reachable user manager → the **user-unit install without any privileged operation**: task 135's `buildUserUnit` content (`INIT_RESTART_SEC_SECONDS = 5`; D-103 keeps no generator default and 139 owns the flag), written to `userUnitInstallPath(owner.home)` and enabled with `systemctl --user daemon-reload` + `systemctl --user enable --now quotameter.service`, all through injectable seams (spawn executor keeping one bounded stderr line, mkdir+write); any failure is §5.3's "or installation fails" → the exact manual fallback (the two session commands when the file made it to disk, the foreground command with the real reason as the lead when it did not, the failure on stderr, exit 0 — and unit preparation is inside the same failure domain, so a hostile owner home is a fallback, never a crash); no sudo + systemd + unreachable user manager → the unit file is still written (no manager needed) and the steps are the exact session commands plus the linger note and the foreground alternative; no systemd → the manual-run command — `quotameter run` for a regular run, or, for a privileged run, initialization and execution from the owner's regular account (`quotameter init`, `quotameter run`): the sudo run's configuration is root-owned `0600` wherever the privileged environment placed it, so no regular account — the only kind §5.1 allows to run the service — can use it, and making the privileged flow's writes owner-readable is task 139's machinery. The owner's account name is guarded before it reaches copy-paste command text (`unsafeOwnerNameProblem`: a name outside the POSIX account grammar is refused with one stderr problem and no rendered commands), and the command runner bounds its stderr capture while streaming (8 KiB) before trimming to one line. The linger note is §5.3's minimum: the exact `sudo loginctl enable-linger <owner>` command with the logout/reboot reason, printed unconditionally because detecting linger status is task 138's feature. Every rendered command sits indented on its own line, copy-pasteable. Branch evidence: `test/unit/cli-service-setup.spec.ts` (35 tests) drives the whole matrix through the seams (including the written unit passing `validateUserUnit`, both enable-command failures, the write- and preparation-failure fallbacks with exit 0, the owner-name guard, owner-problem aborts in both interactive and `--yes` modes, and the production seams against real temp dirs and real child processes), while the branch that is real on the systemd-less container — the manual route — is pinned through the real launcher in `test/integration/cli-init-service.spec.ts` (piped stdin, `--yes`, and a real PTY via `script`): the steps render on a terminal, exit 0, nothing hangs, and no service files are written. Review round (kimi, 0 P0 / 3 P1 / 3 P2): all six findings accepted and fixed in the same commit — the actionable-privileged-manual-steps re-rendering (owner-account re-init; the privileged flow's config-path/ownership question recorded as task 139's in D-106), the preparation-inside-the-failure-domain restructure, the POSIX owner-name guard, the write-failure lead, the plan's wording/own-line command, and the streaming stderr cap.
- sudo/user ownership resolution as a pure, injectable decision layer (task 136, spec §5.3 steps 1 and 7; decision D-105): the new `src/cli/service-owner.ts` classifies the invocation (regular user, or root reached through sudo), resolves the service owner from the user database, and states which operations may use privileges — the seam tasks 137 (fallback paths), 138 (linger guidance), and 139 (service install/uninstall) consume; it performs no installations and no privileged operations itself, and everything is injectable (`getuid`, `env`, `passwd`) so tests drive every context without privileges, sudo, or systemd (the container and CI images run unprivileged). `resolveServiceOwner(seams)` returns `{ state: 'ok', context }` or `{ state: 'problems', problems }` (problems-not-throws, every problem in one pass). **Regular user** (`uid !== 0`): the owner is the first `/etc/passwd` entry with the current uid, and the name must resolve back to that same entry — duplicate account names with different ids make a generated `User=` ambiguous, because systemd resolves names by first database match (an earlier same-name alias at uid 0 could select root), so an ambiguous database is refused (review-round P0 fix); `privileges: 'none'` (§5.3 step 7's consent-to-invoke-sudo route is task 137's flow), and `SUDO_*` is deliberately never consulted — any user can export those names, so at a non-root uid they are untrusted leftovers (`sudo -u otheruser` lands here with the target account as the owner). **Root with `SUDO_USER`**: the owner is the account sudo recorded, `privileges: 'held'` (the privileged systemd operations may run in-process), and the sudo environment must verify against the user database — an own, non-empty property (`Object.hasOwn`, the init-yes.ts `process.env` precedent; inherited names never count), naming an existing non-root account (`SUDO_USER` of `root` — a sudo chain invoked by root — is a §5.1 refusal), with `SUDO_UID`/`SUDO_GID` present, plain-decimal, and equal to the account's database uid/gid; sudo itself guarantees that consistency, so any deviation (missing, malformed, disagreeing) means the environment is not a genuine sudo invocation, and deviations accumulate. A `sudo -s` root shell keeps exactly this consistent trio, so it resolves like a direct `sudo quotameter init`. **Root without a verifiable `SUDO_USER`**: problems with actionable guidance (sudo from a regular account, or a regular user's user-level service) — never a guessed account, since §5.3's only owner-determination mechanism for a root process is `SUDO_USER`. **There is no ok outcome whose owner is root** — the done-condition "the service does not run as root" — pinned both by exhaustive unit cases (unset/empty/inherited/unknown/root/zero-uid-alias `SUDO_USER`, each `SUDO_UID`/`SUDO_GID` failure shape, undeterminable uid) and by `test/unit/cli-service-owner.property.spec.ts`'s fast-check invariants over arbitrary databases, environments, and uids (never-root in every ok state; a regular user resolves their own uid; a root process resolves only the verified `SUDO_USER` account with privileges held). The user database is `/etc/passwd` parsed per passwd(5) by the pure `parsePasswdFile` (seven `:`-separated fields, non-empty name, plain-decimal ids within the exactly-comparable safe-integer range — values beyond `2^53-1` are skipped, and the same shared grammar gates `SUDO_UID`/`SUDO_GID`, since above it `Number` precision loss maps two different id strings onto one double and could verify a forged sudo id; malformed lines skipped — root-owned file, not ours to diagnose) with `readEtcPasswd` as the production default seam and first-match-wins lookups (getpwnam/getpwuid semantics; Node has no getpwnam binding and §5.1's supported targets keep local users there — NSS/SSSD directory users surface as the unknown-account refusal). Privilege routing is a frozen vocabulary: `PRIVILEGED_SYSTEMD_OPERATIONS` (install/remove system unit, `daemon-reload`, enable/disable, start/stop, `enable-linger`) with `isPrivilegedSystemdOperation` as the single classification point — everything else a service setup touches (configuration/data directories, the provider connection test, the user-unit install under the owner's home, status/journalctl queries) runs as the service owner.
- systemd user unit generation with structural validation (task 135, spec §5.3 step 7 / §5.4; decision D-104): the new `src/cli/systemd-user-unit.ts` is the user-unit twin of task 134's generator — pure content, no filesystem or systemd calls (installing/enabling/linger stay tasks 137–139). `buildUserUnit(input)` renders the user unit deterministically from `UserUnitInput { nodePath, cliPath, restartSecSeconds }` — deliberately no user, group, config/data-directory, or secret-carrying field. §5.4's unit-generic directives are kept verbatim (D-104: the spec's first sentence prescribes absolute `ExecStart=<node> <cli> run`, `Restart=on-failure` + configurable `RestartSec=<n>s`, `After=`/`Wants=network-online.target`, `Environment=NODE_ENV=production`, journald output for units; only the hardening sentence splits by manager kind), plus D-103's `Type=exec`, pinned `Description`/`Documentation`, and no `WorkingDirectory`. The manager-compatible adaptations are verified against systemd.exec(7): no `User=`/`Group=` ("only available for system services and not supported for services running in per-user instances" — the user manager already runs the service as the owning regular account, §5.1 by construction); the compatible minimal hardening set is `NoNewPrivileges=true` only (the namespacing directives the system unit carries — `PrivateTmp`, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ProtectHome=read-only`, `ReadWritePaths` — are unavailable to the unprivileged user manager; user-mode write protection is the application's own §6.3 permission discipline); and `WantedBy=default.target` (the user manager ships `default.target` and no `multi-user.target`; the kept `network-online.target` ordering is trivially satisfied there — benign). `USER_UNIT_NAME` (`quotameter.service`, same name as the system unit — separate manager namespaces) and `userUnitInstallPath(homeDir)` (§5.3 step 7's literal `~/.config/systemd/user/quotameter.service`, injection-guarded via the shared `assertUnitPathInput`) are exported for task 139. `validateUserUnit(content)` runs the user shape on the shared `checkUnitShape` engine extracted from `validateSystemUnit` in `src/cli/systemd-unit.ts` — a behavior-preserving refactor (task 134's suite passes unchanged) that also exports the check builders, `assertUnitRestartSecSeconds`, and the pinned unit strings, so the twin validates instead of forking: the system-only directives are not in the allowed key set (their presence — a system unit pasted into `~/.config/systemd/user/` — is an unexpected-directive problem, never silently reinterpreted), `WantedBy=` must be `default.target`, and any `Environment=` line other than `NODE_ENV=production` is rejected without echoing the divergent value (§5.4/§15.4 no-secrets, by construction and read-back). `test/unit/cli-systemd-user-unit.spec.ts` pins the exact-content snapshot, determinism, the structural absence of every system-only directive, the input-injection guards, the full validator matrix, and a two-way cross-check (each validator rejects the other twin's generated unit); the module measures 100% coverage on all four axes.
- systemd system unit generation with structural validation (task 134, spec §5.4; decision D-103): the new `src/cli/systemd-unit.ts` is the pure content half of service installation — generation, parsing, validation, with no filesystem or systemd calls (installing/enabling stays task 139's `service install`). `buildSystemUnit(input)` renders the system unit deterministically from validated inputs: absolute `nodePath`/`cliPath` pinned into `ExecStart=<node> <cli> run` (§5.4 — Node may live in nvm or NodeSource), a regular `User=` with optional `Group=` (never `root`, §5.1), `Restart=on-failure` with `RestartSec=<n>s` from a whole-seconds input, `After=`/`Wants=network-online.target`, `Environment=NODE_ENV=production`, the §5.4 hardening set (`NoNewPrivileges`, `PrivateTmp`, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ProtectHome=read-only`, `ReadWritePaths` limited to the §6.1 config/data directories), journald output, and `Type=exec` + `WantedBy=multi-user.target` (D-103: a stale Node/CLI path fails the start visibly, pairing with `doctor`'s stale-path detection). Inputs are validated before any content exists — relative paths, whitespace/control characters in interpolated values (the unit-injection guard: a newline could inject directives, and paths needing systemd quoting are a loud error, not a mis-parsed unit), `root` as user or group, and non-integer restart seconds are refused with field-naming messages; `SystemUnitInput` has no secret-carrying field at all, so §5.4's "never put secrets in unit files" holds by construction. `parseUnitFile` reads unit content back per systemd's documented grammar (systemd.syntax(7): comments, CRLF/CR, BOM, all whitespace after `=` ignored, section names verbatim between the brackets, backslash continuations where the backslash becomes a single space and comment blocks between continued lines are skipped, the escaped-backslash even-count rule) reporting malformed lines as numbered problem strings — the parser `doctor` (145) reuses on hand-edited installed units — and `validateSystemUnit` checks the §5.4 contract structurally: exact contract values, truthy boolean spellings, systemd time units, absolute `ExecStart` tokens and `ReadWritePaths` entries (never `/`, and exactly the generated config/data pair when the expected directories are supplied), `User=`/`Group=` neither `root` nor a numeric id (id 0 is root by another spelling), duplicate keys (systemd's silent last-wins hides corruption), unexpected sections — empty ones included — and directives (strict shape of the generated unit), and **any** `Environment=` line other than `NODE_ENV=production` (the read-back no-secrets rule, whose message deliberately does not reproduce the divergent value so a smuggled secret can never be echoed into diagnostics, §15.4). Input validation additionally rejects systemd metacharacters (`\`, `"`, `'`, `$`, `%`) in path fields — the generator does not escape them, and unescaped they change unit semantics — and `RestartSec` input is bounded by `Number.isSafeInteger` (a 1e21-style integer would render a time span the module's own validator rejects). Validation is structural by measured necessity: the dev container and CI images ship no systemd (`systemd-analyze` absent — probed), so the done-condition is the exact-content snapshot (same inputs → byte-identical unit, pinned) plus the invariant checker; the module measures 100% coverage on all four axes in `test/unit/cli-systemd-unit.spec.ts`. Task 135's user unit reuses the shared blocks (`renderUnitFile`, `assertUnitPathInput`, `assertUnitUserInput`); `SYSTEM_UNIT_NAME`/`SYSTEM_UNIT_INSTALL_PATH` (§5.3's `/etc/systemd/system/quotameter.service`) are exported for the installer.

### Changed

- `@fastify/rate-limit` dependency removed (task U-043, spec §3, §15.5, §15.2, §13; decision D-143): the package D-109 declared for a then-imagined HTTP-layer task 156 — and D-125 left ownerless when §15.2's budget landed as the services-layer primitive `src/services/login-rate-limit.ts` — is gone from `dependencies` (lockfile regenerated host-side with npm 11.9.0 via `npm install --package-lock-only`; the diff is deletions-only — the package entry plus the one dependency it orphaned, `ip-address`; its other three transitives remain, still required by `fastify` and the other plugins). The register branch was examined first and rejected scope-by-scope, from the spec text: the **login scope** — the one place the spec rate-limits anything (§15.2) — is D-125's and inexpressible by the package (§15.2 budgets _failed_ attempts and demands _increasing delays_; the plugin counts requests and answers 429s — and behind the §15.6 reverse proxy, where `trustProxy` is never enabled, every client shares the proxy's socket address, making a per-IP 429 limiter the shared-source denial-of-service lever D-125's delays-not-rejections choice explicitly rejected); a **general API scope** has no spec sentence asking for it (§15.5's own limits are the 64 KiB body size and the connection/request timeouts; §13 defines no 429; the spec's abuse answers are §15.1's auth modes, §15.3's Host/Origin validation, §15.6's TLS proxy/firewall/SSH-tunneling publication — plus the §15.2 login budget); and **any registration** would contradict the landed error contract (D-125 retired D-113's promised `RATE_LIMITED` registry code — "delays are not errors" — and a codeless native 429 is a foreign status D-113's strict classifier projects to INTERNAL 500, worse than nothing). §3's technology table names the package under "Required implementation", and D-143 records the removal as a **knowing deviation** from that row's letter — §3's own "keep runtime dependencies minimal" instruction, plus the absence of any rate-limit requirement outside §15.2, make an unregistered, unimported runtime dependency of a package that ships to end users the worse reading; the spec and the manifest now disagree on this one row, deliberately, and a future spec revision should drop the entry so they agree again. The construction suite's registration pin (D-109/D-127) is adjusted: the `hasPlugin('@fastify/rate-limit') === false` guard stays (the accidental-registration pin) and a **declaration pin** is added — `package.json` at the repo root carries no `@fastify/rate-limit` in any shipped dependency field (`dependencies`/`optionalDependencies`/`peerDependencies`) — so re-adding the package fails `make check` until the decision trail is updated (verified in both directions: the pin fails on a re-added line; a `src/` import of the uninstalled package would already fail typecheck). `src/server/http.ts` (docblock only), `src/server/README.md`, and `src/services/README.md` state the removal where they stated the deferral. No public API, format, or event changes.
- Safe initial account setup in `init` (task 133, spec §5.3 step 6; decision D-102): the optional initial account collected by tasks 130/131 is now **real**. On a fresh run whose answers carry a secret source, the new `src/cli/init-account.ts` assembles the persisted account — a deterministic id slug of the label (schema/SQL charset with separator head/tail trims including underscores, capped, fallback `account`), the provider's own id via the new composition-root factory `src/server/providers.ts` (`createInitialAccountProvider()`; `src/server/` is the one layer the package layout allows to import concrete adapters, and `cli` may import `server`, so the CLI stays provider-neutral), `enabled: true`, and exactly one §6.3 secret source (the typed key as `apiKey`, or `--api-key-env`'s variable as `apiKeyEnv` — a whitespace-only value counts as unset at the input boundary and as an explained unusable-value failure at test time) — runs the initial provider connection test through the §8.1 contract (`validateCredentials`, the secret sealed in a non-enumerable `AccountContext`), and **a failed test never aborts the run**: the failure is explained on stderr with its §7.2/§8.1 outcome class (`credentials-rejected` — the provider refused the key, or `provider-unavailable` — the check was inconclusive; the class is read structurally from the provider-neutral error's `kind`, an error without one classifies unavailable — hostile rejection shapes included, the `kind` read and detail stringification independently guarded — and the known secret is scrubbed from every detail line as defense in depth), then §5.3's "offer to continue with the account in an error state" is asked interactively (Enter/`y` keeps, `n` drops the account from this run's write, closed input cancels exactly like a closed question, and a Ctrl-C during the connection-test wait cancels through the prompter's new `interrupted()` latch — piped EOF after the last answer deliberately does not), while `init --yes` has no prompter and always keeps the account. The step runs before the single consented write, so a dropped account is simply absent from it and the D-101 stale-detection guard still sits immediately before the write. A label collected without any key stores no account (§6.3's exactly-one rule makes a keyless account invalid configuration) and says so in the summary; the summary's Account row now also states the connection-test outcome. The key value is asserted absent from every stdout/stderr line at every stage (prompt handling, validation, failure explanation, offer, success summary, decline path) in unit, in-process, real-launcher, and PTY suites, while present exactly where §6.3/D-004 put it — inside the `0600` configuration file.
