- Season-proof Luxon parity in the DST transition-day sweep (spec §17.1; decision D-079's caveat implemented): the every-minute Luxon-parity enumeration in `test/unit/peak-hours-dst.property.spec.ts` asserted strict equality on ambiguous wall readings, but Luxon resolves those from an offset guess seeded by the current instant — observed live when America/Havana crossed to standard time on 2026-11-01 and the suite went red with no code change (the zone's ambiguous midnight resolved to the second, equally valid occurrence for the whole winter). The sweep's scan oracle now records every occurrence per wall time: strict `toBe` parity stays for unambiguous and gapped readings (they converge seed-invariantly across the matrix), while ambiguous readings assert valid-occurrence membership, and a seeding regression pins the mechanism executably (Luxon `Settings.now` overridden to a DST-season and a standard-season seed reproduces both flips, with the authoritative `resolveLocalInstantDst` first-occurrence answer seed-free). The compat resolver's docblock now states the precise contract instead of the blanket "empirically equal across the matrix"; the authoritative §9.3 policy and all product behavior are unchanged.
- Idempotent `init` with existing-config consent (task 132, spec §5.3; decision D-101): `quotameter init` now classifies the resolved configuration file (§6.1 XDG paths) before collecting anything, and "running init twice does not overwrite settings without consent" holds in both modes. **No file** — the fresh flows of tasks 130/131 unchanged, and the run writes the configuration for the first time: the new `src/cli/init-persist.ts` drives the landed layers (`enforceConfigPermissions` creates the directory at `0700` or corrects/refuses, `writeConfigFile` writes the canonical `config.json` at `0600` atomically — §5.3 step 5's configuration half; the database half lands with the storage-consuming init steps, task 133+). The collected initial account is deliberately not persisted (the stable id and §6.3 secret-source assembly are task 133's) and the summary states so plainly. **Valid file** — §5.3's subsequent-run flow: current settings are displayed with accounts masked to their secret state (a stored key never appears; an environment-sourced key names only its variable — §6.3/§15.4), every settings question names its current value and Enter keeps it (the shared `retainOnEmpty` parser wraps the task-130 validators — the same informed-Enter semantics as a shown default), `init --yes`'s absent optional options keep the current values instead of silently reverting them to the spec defaults (an explicit flag is the consent to change that setting; `--timezone` stays mandatory; `--account-label`/`--api-key-env` against an existing configuration are a clear failure, never silently ignored), and existing accounts plus every setting init does not ask about (retention, logging, providers, auth) survive verbatim through `validateSettings`' §5.3 keep-or-change merge. **Nothing is written unless a setting actually changes** — an unchanged re-run prints `No changes — the existing configuration is kept` and leaves the file byte-identical. Immediately before any write the file is re-read and the run refused (exit 1, nothing written) when its state changed meanwhile — the stale-detection guard: a fresh run requires the file to still be absent (a file created after the initial look is never clobbered), a re-run requires the same configuration that was displayed, and the short re-read-to-rename window that remains is accepted (D-101 — a lock would serialize only cooperating init processes, not hand edits or foreign writers). **Present but invalid** — a hard refusal (exit 1) printing the loader's own secret-free problems and leaving the file untouched: overwriting a configuration whose contents cannot be validated is never consented to (§6.2). One state is deliberately tolerated on both the read and the write side (D-101): a non-loopback bind or `required` mode without a password hash is probed structurally with a placeholder (D-022's technique, never by matching problem wording) — and only a hash that is **absent**: a present-but-malformed hash (empty, non-string) or a non-object `auth` section is a defect the probe never masks, so such files stay invalid and are never rewritten — persisted hash-less with the `quotameter passwd` remedy, and re-read as pending — §15.1's own sentence binds _starting_ ("refuse to start") and §15.2 names `passwd` as the completer, so the intermediate state the task-130 note always promised now actually exists on disk; any further defect still refuses loudly. The handler's detection and persistence are injectable seams (`InitDeps.readExisting`/`persistConfig`) for the unit suites; `test/integration/cli-init-idempotent.spec.ts` pins the done-condition end-to-end — first run creates the file (0700/0600, canonical JSON, no key inside), the second run offers keep-or-change and preserves values on retain (byte-identical file), a change touches only the typed setting while a hand-edited `historyRetentionDays`, logging level, and accounts survive, and `init --yes --timezone X` twice keeps a customized `--port` from the first run (no silent revert) — through both the in-process handler with a real filesystem and the real launcher in a child process; the task-130/131 integration suites now run every invocation in its own temporary `QUOTAMETER_CONFIG_DIR` (a successful init writes), and task 131's reproducibility pin compares two fresh-directory runs with the path normalized. A new type-only `CanonicalConfig` view on `src/config/load.ts` describes what `parseConfig` actually emits (sections materialized, D-018) with no runtime code — the module's pinned coverage keys are untouched.
- Non-interactive `init --yes` with environment-based key input (task 131, spec §5.3; decision D-100): `quotameter init --yes` takes every value from explicit options — `--timezone` (mandatory), `--host`, `--port`, `--polling-interval`, `--account-label`, `--api-key-env` — and never touches stdin (the handler branches before a prompter is constructed, so a prompt fallback is impossible by construction; the option surface is declared in the dispatch table and renders in `init --help`). Validation is the task-130 interactive layer reused, not re-derived: the new `src/cli/init-yes.ts` feeds every flag through the same exported parsers, with an absent optional flag as the parser's empty input, so "omitted" inherits exactly the pressed-Enter semantics (`127.0.0.1`/`8787`/`300`, no account). A missing or blank `--timezone` is one dedicated failure naming the flag (`quotameter init: --timezone is required with --yes: Quotameter never applies the system time zone automatically…`, exit 1 — and a set `QUOTAMETER_APP_TIMEZONE` does not satisfy it, because service-load overrides play no role while init collects explicit values); an invalid value fails identically to the typed answer; every value problem is reported in one pass, one prefixed stderr line each. The API key enters only through the environment, by name: there is deliberately no `--api-key <value>` option (a value flag would put the secret in the process listing and shell history, §15.4; a key-carrying unknown flag fails as a usage error without echoing the value — the parser names the option, not its argument), and `--api-key-env <variable>` is §6.3's `apiKeyEnv` mechanism at the input boundary — the name is shape-checked against the account schema's pattern (single-sited as the new exported `API_KEY_ENV_NAME_PATTERN`), the reserved `QUOTAMETER_` prefix is rejected with its remedy (the service's strict env scan would reject such a variable once set), and the variable must actually be set (an empty value counts as unset; an inherited prototype name such as `toString` — which resolves on `process.env`, verified on the pinned runtime — does not count: `Object.hasOwn`). Only the name is recorded (`InitAnswers.accountApiKeyEnv`); the value is consulted exactly once, for the non-empty check, and is never retained or printed — and no `--api-key-env` problem contains the supplied argument, because the argument may be a mistyped key (an underscore-only key is a syntactically valid variable name; a review-round finding on the first version, which echoed it — §15.4). The summary reports `'label' (API key from environment variable 'NAME', value never stored or printed)`. Task 133 consumes the name for the connection test and persistence. A key source without `--account-label` is a problem (the label creates the account, as interactively); any value option without `--yes` is a clear failure instead of a silently ignored flag or a half-prefilled wizard. Done-condition evidence is pinned end-to-end against the real launcher with stdin closed: the exact missing-`--timezone` failure (distinct from the wizard's EOF cancellation, so no prompt fallback), defaults on absent flags, all-problems-in-one-run reporting, unknown-option exit 2, the unset-variable, reserved-prefix, and pasted-key-as-argument failures all leaving the argument out of every output line, the key value absent from stdout+stderr, and reproducibility — the same invocation run twice prints byte-identical stdout (`buildYesAnswers` is pure over flags plus an injected environment snapshot, and the summary carries no timestamps, hints, or randomness).
- Interactive `init` questions behind the first real command handler (task 130, spec §5.3; decision D-099): `quotameter init` now asks the §5.3 questions in order — explicit IANA time zone, bind host, port, polling interval, optional initial Z.ai account label, optional API key — validates every value, and prints a masked summary. Three new modules under `src/cli/`: `prompts.ts` is the prompt seam whose production `createReadlinePrompter` deliberately avoids `readline`'s `question()` for two verified defects (piped input delivering several answers in one chunk silently drops every line that fires while no question callback is pending, and a close without a final line leaves the promise pending forever) — it keeps the interface for raw-mode line editing and echo but owns the line queue, settles pending questions with `null` on `close`, and routes echo through a mutable gate so `askSecret` shows the prompt while the typed key never reaches the terminal (terminal/raw mode is enabled exactly when stdin is a TTY, the only mode where output muting can hide input). Type-ahead never echoes either (a code-review finding on the first version, which muted only at the secret prompt — pasting the whole answer block at the first prompt would have echoed the key): a `data` listener attached before the interface mutes any chunk carrying more than one answer before readline can echo it, and a line arriving with no question pending latches a silent mode for the rest of the flow; PTY transcript tests prove both shapes — line-by-line typing echoes normal answers but never the key, and a whole-block paste echoes nothing. `init-questions.ts` runs the flow: an invalid answer is a stderr complaint and the same question again, and any closed input (EOF, Ctrl-C, closed pipe) cancels the whole flow with `null` — the handler renders that as the standard failure `quotameter init: input closed before the questions were answered — initialization cancelled` with exit 1, so `init < /dev/null` aborts cleanly instead of hanging or defaulting. Empty-answer semantics are pinned per decision D-099: Enter takes the default shown in the prompt for host (`127.0.0.1`), port (`8787`), and interval (300 s, range 60–3600 of §7.1); Enter skips the label/key questions (add the key later in the UI, §5.3 step 3); and the time zone has **no default** — the empty answer is the loud "Time zone is required: Quotameter never applies the system time zone automatically" error, the system zone appears inside the prompt as a hint only (adopting it still means typing it), and validation reuses `isValidTimeZoneName` from the configuration contract, accepting only what the rest of the system accepts, with accepted names canonicalized through `Intl` (`utc` → `UTC`, aliases kept). The per-value parsers and `buildInitConfig` (answers → the `server`/`app` sections; the `accounts` array awaits task 133's stable-id and secret-source assembly) are exported single sites for `init --yes` (131) and the idempotent flow (132); the summary reports the key only as `configured (hidden)`/`skipped` (§6.3/§15.4), warns that a non-loopback host requires a password before the service starts (§5.3 step 4, `quotameter passwd`), and states plainly that the questions only collect — at the stage task 130 landed in, nothing was written yet, so exit 0 implied no configured service (the configuration write, its trailer, and the exit-0-means-written contract are task 132's, above). Verified by `test/unit/cli-prompts.spec.ts` (real prompter over TTY-dressed PassThrough streams: echo, mute, EOF, double-question-after-close, Ctrl-C byte), `test/unit/cli-init-questions.spec.ts` (per-value validation tables, empty/blank distinctions, hint-not-adopted, canonicalization, cancellation at every question position, secret never in complaints), `test/unit/cli-init.spec.ts` (exit codes, masked summary, password note), and `test/integration/cli-init.spec.ts` (the real launcher in child processes: the one-chunk piped input that motivated the line queue, retries, immediate and mid-flow EOF, and the PTY transcript). `cli-dispatch.spec.ts` drops `init` from the still-stubbed list.
- The runtime version gate derives its minimum from `engines.node` and is boundary-tested in `make check` (task 129, spec §5.1/§17.2; decision D-098): `bin/quotameter.mjs` no longer hardcodes `[22, 13, 0]` beside the manifest — the declared minimum is parsed from `package.json`'s `engines.node` (exactly `>=X.Y.Z`; any other shape, or an unreadable manifest, is a fatal packaging error), so the gate npm's warning cites and the gate the launcher enforces cannot drift apart, and the manifest-finalization task (184) moves the gate by editing one field. Ordering is pinned: manifest read → engines parse → version gate → the `--version`/`-v` fast path → dynamic-import delegation — a below-minimum runtime is rejected with the actionable message ("requires Node.js >= 22.13.0 (found …). Upgrade Node.js (for example via the official binaries or nvm) and try again.") before any module under `src/` loads, `--version` does not bypass the gate (task 054's CI contract), and gate exits set `process.exitCode` instead of calling `process.exit()`, closing the pipe-truncation hazard the file's own discipline documents for every other exit. Comparison semantics are now explicit and shared by all three implementations (`bin/`, `src/util/version.ts`, `scripts/verify-runtime.mjs`): order by the numeric `major.minor.patch` prefix, ignore a prerelease/build suffix (the first `-`/`+` onward), so a nightly of a supported line (`22.13.0-nightly…`) runs where the old `src/util/version.ts` parse threw `Invalid version string` and the old bin comparator passed `'garbage'`/`'v22.13.0'` by NaN accident — the launcher now fails closed on an unparseable `process.versions.node` with a dedicated "cannot determine the Node.js version" message, and the dev gate `scripts/verify-runtime.mjs` reads the same declaration and moved its `node:sqlite` import behind its version check (the module is the unsupported feature the gate exists to withhold). `src/server/main.ts`'s `verifyRuntime` (the `quotameter run` defense-in-depth) gains the same upgrade hint; its message and the launcher's stay partially-regex pinned by the pre-existing suites. The done-condition — supported and unsupported versions tested — is delivered as a shared boundary matrix (`test/helpers/nodeVersionGate.ts`) consumed by both gate suites: the unit block pins `satisfiesMinimum`'s verdict per case, and `test/integration/cli-bin-version-gate.spec.ts` runs the real launcher as a child process with each case faked into `process.versions.node` through a data-URL `--import` preload (the property is non-writable but configurable — verified on the pinned 22.13.0 runtime), covering exactly 22.13.0 vs 22.12.x, older/newer lines (18/20/21/23/24), short forms (`22.13`, `22`), suffixed nightlies on both sides of the boundary, and malformed values; fixture-package runs prove the gate follows a raised (`>=22.14.0`) or relaxed (`>=20.0.0`) declaration and fails closed without one, and a no-fallback pin proves a faked 22.12.9 stops at the gate message with no command-module load attempted. Boundary evidence recorded in the task-129 commit body; the CI `install` and `unsupported-node` jobs' contracts are preserved by construction.

- The coverage-threshold guard pins its single-instance premise executably (U-027, spec §17.3; decisions D-093/D-094): the guard's matcher mirror is faithful only while `test/unit/coverage-thresholds.spec.ts`'s `import 'picomatch'` and the installed runner's own load resolve the same package — true by range construction since U-024 (the declared `^4.0.7` matches vitest@5.0.5's own picomatch range, one root copy), but U-024's resolution evidence was prose in a commit body, and a future range divergence (a vitest major on `picomatch@^5`, say) would nest a divergent copy under `node_modules/vitest/node_modules/` and split the mirror silently — the guard would keep judging threshold keys with semantics the runner no longer applies, green all the while. The guard now carries a resolve-identity assertion: `require.resolve('picomatch')` from the spec's own location (via `createRequire(import.meta.url)` — the suite's established ESM seam) and a second resolve rooted at the installed vitest's package directory (`paths: [dirname(resolve('vitest/package.json'))]`, an explicitly exported subpath) must yield the same path — the `paths` form reproduces the runner's candidate walk, where a nested copy shadows the root one exactly as it would for vitest's own import. The failure message names the invariant, both resolved paths, and the remedy (re-pin the range or dedupe). Demonstrated live on a scratch split (not committed, container `node_modules` volume only): a `cp -r` duplicate of the root picomatch nested at `node_modules/vitest/node_modules/picomatch` — what npm materializes on a range divergence — fails exactly this one assertion (`/app/node_modules/picomatch/index.js` vs `/app/node_modules/vitest/node_modules/picomatch/index.js`) while the suite's other five guard tests stay green, proving the split silent everywhere else; the nested copy was deleted and the tree verified clean. The ambient `test/unit/picomatch.d.ts` header now points at the executable pin; its removal instruction is unchanged.

- The coverage-threshold guard's matcher is a declared dependency, not a transitive accident (U-024, spec §17.3; decision D-093): `test/unit/coverage-thresholds.spec.ts` imports `picomatch` to mirror the installed Vitest 5's threshold-glob matching one-for-one (`resolveThresholds` compiles each non-global key with `picomatch(key)`, default options), but the package reached the suite only through other packages' dependency ranges — the U-015 review's P3-1 recommended the declaration and was declined only because `package.json`/the lockfile sat outside that task's file set. `picomatch@^4.0.7` now sits in `devDependencies` — the exact range the installed vitest@5.0.5 declares, so one instance serves both the guard and the runner by range construction rather than tree luck: the lockfile already resolved a single shared root `node_modules/picomatch@4.0.7` (no nested duplicates before or after; the only lockfile change is the range added to the root block), and `require.resolve('picomatch')` from the guard's directory and from inside `node_modules/vitest` returns the same file on the host and in the container (`npm ls picomatch`: every dependent — vitest, vite, tinyglobby, `@vue/language-core`, fdir's optional peer — deduped onto it). The declared 4.0.7 ships no type declarations (no `types`/`typings` field, no `.d.ts` in the package), so the minimal ambient `test/unit/picomatch.d.ts` stays, its header now pointing at the declared version it mirrors. The failure mode was already safe (an unresolvable import fails the gate red, never silently green); the undeclared relationship itself is what is gone.

- The compound-key instant sorts join the shared ordering family (U-023, spec §10; decision D-096): the two subtraction-based compound-key sorts D-090's census flagged and left outside U-013's file set — `src/services/peak-hours-intervals.ts`'s `(start, end)` peak-occurrence union order (D-073/D-079) and `src/storage/backups.ts`'s `byInstant` `(timestampMs, seq)` retention order (D-065) — migrate to a new builder beside the single-key comparator: `compareByEpochMs(primary, tieBreak)` in `src/util/compare-epoch-ms.ts` (the "extends this one" branch of D-090's own consequences sentence — one file keeps one import site for one concern; a sibling leaf would split a single contract family). Pinned contract: primary epoch order with the D-090 exact-sign ladder, an ascending finite-`number` tie-break (domain deliberately "any finite number" — an epoch at the services site, an integer counter at the storage site; the family claims neither identity), and non-finite `NaN`/`±Infinity` throwing a `TypeError` per key role on every invocation — both keys of both items validated eagerly, primary pair first, so rejection never depends on where a sort happens to stop comparing; equal keys yield `0`, preserving the stable-sort input order the peak-hours suite pins. Both migrations are wiring-only with zero assertion changes in `test/unit/peak-hours-intervals.spec.ts` and `test/integration/startup-migrations.spec.ts`, and `compareEpochMs` itself is byte-identical in behavior and message. The migration audit re-checked D-090's seam discipline at both sites and found both finite by construction, so no new guards were needed: the peak-hours edge values come from the D-079 resolver contract (whose finiteness promise the sort now enforces loudly instead of silently collapsing a `NaN` onto the tie-break), and the backups keys are sealed by the `now` non-negative-integer gate plus the file system itself — a grammar name caps at 255 bytes, bounding a digit run at 241 digits (~1e241, finite), while a run that parses non-finite needs 309 and a 323-byte name fails outright on the pinned runtime (`ENAMETOOLONG`, verified). A parse-seam guard rejecting such runs was implemented and withdrawn: unreachable through any supported file system, it could ship only as the defensively-unreachable branch class `docs/agents/testing.md` forbids. The family's contract is pinned by new `compareByEpochMs` blocks in `test/unit/compare-epoch-ms.spec.ts` mirroring the U-013 structure (everyday and ±8.64e15-anchor ordering, ascending tie-breaks, the non-finite table with eager-validation and message-precedence pins, `Array#sort` use incl. equal-key stability).

- The retention horizon is single-sited (U-014, spec §11.3; decision D-091): §11.3's cutoff arithmetic — `now − retentionDays` days — existed as twin computations, `src/storage/retention-cleanup.ts`'s private `MS_PER_DAY` day-ms constant (D-063, task 091) and `src/storage/history-retention.ts`'s horizon over `util`'s `DAY_MS` (D-082, task 123), kept behaviorally equal only by the cleanup-alignment test — the finding task 123's review recorded as P3 and that wave's file sets deferred. The value is now one exported definition, `historyCutoff(now, retentionDays)` in `retention-cleanup.ts`, consumed by both the cleanup's delete cutoffs and `boundHistoryRange`'s horizon: the documented two-sided alignment (strict-before deletes, closed-interval reads) holds by construction. Placement follows the D-058 sibling taxonomy — `history-retention.ts` already imports D-063's §11.3 bounds constants, so the export rides an existing, documented intra-`storage` edge, and `retention-cleanup.ts` is already the storage-side owner of §11.3 window policy; the neutral-leaf alternative (`src/storage/retention-horizon.ts`) was rejected because it would split that policy across two modules (D-090's `compareEpochMs` leaf precedent served siblings that could not import each other — these do). The private `MS_PER_DAY` is gone: `util`'s `DAY_MS` is the unit end-to-end and no day-ms literal remains in the retention path (`grep MS_PER_DAY|86_400_000 src/storage/*.ts` returns only `migrations.ts`'s two single-sited §15.2 session constants, a different family). The helper is pure arithmetic — both consuming seams keep their own test-pinned input validation, so no second error vocabulary exists. Additive tests pin the helper's own contract directly: a value table at §11.3's 7/30/180/3650 windows against the spec file's independent day-ms literal, the day-boundary semantics (a row exactly at `historyCutoff` survives the strict-before deletes, one 1 ms earlier goes; the report's `historyCutoff` field is the helper's value), and the clamp's reported horizon equal to the helper at both window bounds — beside the unchanged D-063/D-082 alignment suites, green with unmodified assertions.
- The D-085 coverage exclusions are closed and their files enforced (U-016, spec §17.3; decision D-089): `src/config/env.ts`, `src/config/load.ts`, `src/config/reload.ts`, and `src/providers/transport.ts` — the four modules task 125 left under the global 90% thresholds only — join the per-file critical-module map in `vitest.config.ts`, per D-085's lift-to-measured-first widening path. Two measured gaps closed with tests: the transport's body-release catch handler (`ignoreReleaseFailure`, functions 95.45% → 100) is pinned by a unit test whose injected fetch resolves a non-2xx response whose `body.cancel()` rejects — the status observation must be delivered and the release failure never surface (a real stream's `cancel()` always resolves, so the fetch seam is the only deterministic route); and `parseJson`'s non-Error arm in `load.ts` is pinned by a value whose string coercion throws a non-Error class instance — the total-function promise ("the only failure mode becomes one readable problem") holds, `invalid JSON: [object Object]`, never a rethrow (branch 98.27% → 99.13%). Three single-branch arms are unreachable by construction and become the map's only documented exceptions (D-089), each key pinned at its measured maximum so any new uncovered arm — worth 0.86–2.78 pp, always below the pin — still fails the gate: `env.ts` branches 97.67 (the unknown-name sort comparator's equal-key arm: object keys are unique, and the Proxy `ownKeys` invariant enforces it too, while string `<`/`>` is a total order — "neither less nor greater" is impossible), `load.ts` branches 99.13 (the "must be one of the allowed values" fallback: every ConfigSchema union is literal-only today, so Ajv always emits const line-mates for a failed anyOf — reachable only when a non-literal union joins the schema), and `reload.ts` branches 97.22 (the `?? CONFIG_DEFAULTS.logging.level` fallback: the canonical parser always materializes `logging.level`, and no injection seam bypasses it). No production code changed. Enforcement demonstrated on scratch runs (not committed): skipping the transport test fails with `Coverage for functions (95.45%) does not meet "src/providers/transport.ts" threshold (100%)`, skipping the parseJson test with `Coverage for branches (98.27%) does not meet "src/config/load.ts" threshold (99.13%)` — the exception axes bite exactly like the 100% axes.

- The epoch-millisecond comparator is single-sited in `src/util/compare-epoch-ms.ts` (U-013, spec §10; decision D-090): the three byte-identical private `compareEpochMs` copies in `src/services/history-gaps.ts`, `history-downsample.ts`, and `history-timeline.ts` (tasks 119–121) are replaced by one shared `util` leaf — the promotion task 121's review P3 recommended and that wave's file sets deferred. The pinned contract: a **total order over finite epoch-ms** (exact −1/0/1 sign comparison, no subtraction — exact at the ±8.64e15 ECMAScript time-value bounds where a difference-based comparator would rely on rounding) and **fail-fast rejection of non-finite input** (`NaN`/`±Infinity` throw a `TypeError` naming both values, where the private copies silently treated `NaN` as equal to everything — not a total order). Every migrated call site sorts only values its seam validator already proved finite, so no consumer's observable ordering behavior changes and the three suites stay green with unchanged assertions; the new `test/unit/compare-epoch-ms.spec.ts` pins ordering at everyday and extreme magnitudes (the ±8.64e15 anchors included), antisymmetry/reflexivity, `Array#sort` use, and the non-finite throw table. Two similar subtraction-based compound-key instant sorts outside the task's file set are flagged, not migrated: `peak-hours-intervals.ts` (`(start, end)`) and `storage/backups.ts` (`byInstant`, `(timestampMs, seq)`).
- Critical-module coverage targets are enforced configuration, not hand-verified claims (task 125, spec §17.3; decision D-085): `vitest.config.ts` coverage thresholds now carry per-file glob entries holding the §17.3 critical modules at **100% statements/branches/functions/lines** (`perFile: true`, so a violation fails the run naming the exact file and axis) beside the unchanged global 90% aggregate — time-zone logic (`src/util/clock.ts`, `src/util/rolling-ranges.ts`), peak-hours (`src/services/peak-hours*.ts`: `peak-hours-intervals.ts` + `peak-hours-dst.ts`, with later §9.3 family files joining automatically), sanitization (`src/providers/sanitize.ts`), and secret-handling (`src/util/logger.ts` for §15.4's Pino redaction, `src/config/permissions.ts` for the 0700/0600 enforcement, `src/config/write.ts` for atomic writes of the secret-bearing config). No threshold exceptions: every listed module measures 100/100/100/100 on the current suite; the boundary is recorded in D-085 — `src/config/env.ts` (97.67 branches), `src/config/load.ts` (98.27), and `src/config/reload.ts` (97.22) are configuration-loading mechanics (§6), not secret protection, and stay under the global thresholds only. `make check` now runs `test-ci` — the exact `npm run test:ci` command CI's `test` job runs — so local checks fail exactly where CI fails, coverage thresholds included (suite cost 44.6 s → 57.5 s; `make test` remains the fast inner loop). The enforcement was demonstrated failing on a scratch run with an injected unmeetable threshold (`src/storage/maintenance.ts`, measured 83.33/50/50/83.33): four `ERROR: Coverage for … does not meet … threshold` lines and a non-zero exit.
- Cycle reset detection reads the previous valid point directly (U-011, spec §12.1, §11.2; decision D-083): `detectCycleResetEvents` in `src/services/events-cycle.ts` now builds its comparison base from one account-level `QuotaRepository.getLatestValidMetricValues(accountId)` read (D-077, task 122) instead of materializing each metric's full retained series through `getMetricSeries` and walking back past trailing invalid rows — O(series) rows per valid metric per successful cycle (~52 000 rows at §11.3's default retention, the compromise D-078 recorded because `src/storage/**` belonged to task 122 in that wave) becomes one indexed window-function statement per cycle, served by the `metric_values_series_access` index and performed lazily, so a zero-metric or all-invalid snapshot still reads nothing. The swap is a pure cost change with no observable behavior difference, pinned rather than assumed: the facade's series ordering was already `(collected_at, run_id)` — never arrival order, despite D-078's rationale prose — so the walk-back selected exactly the valid row maximizing `(collected_at, run_id)`, which is D-077's `ORDER BY collected_at DESC, run_id DESC` window; trailing invalid rows, out-of-order arrivals (a later-arriving run with an older stamp), and equal stamps all resolve identically before and after, no time bound is introduced (D-078's rejection of a `to: collectedAt` lookup stands, and an out-of-order new snapshot stays suppressed by the detector's chronology guard — missed, never false), and D-078's flagged latest-point facade follow-up is closed by recording that D-077's method already is that read (`src/storage/repository.ts` is untouched). The task-118 suites keep their observable behavior: `test/integration/events-cycle.spec.ts` proves both §12.1 event-time branches end-to-end against a real database through the new read unchanged, `test/unit/polling-persist.spec.ts`'s read-before-transaction pin now records one account-level read, and `test/unit/events-cycle.spec.ts` gains the ordering-reconciliation pin at the seam.
- Mutation-survivor triage round 2 (U-008, spec §17.3): every survivor of the 2026-10-30 report (run 41105458027) in the seven secret-handling and §17.3-critical modules — `src/config/load.ts`, `src/util/logger.ts`, `src/config/reload.ts`, `src/util/clock.ts`, `src/config/permissions.ts`, `src/config/env.ts`, `src/providers/sanitize.ts` — is either killed by a new test (79) or excluded in code with a reasoned Stryker ignore comment (43, per `docs/mutation-testing.md`/D-055; equivalences verified empirically where feasible: V8's sort-comparator call convention, Node's empty-encoding normalization, Ajv's own range-keyword message text, pino's undefined-merging-object normalization, and a 31-shape corpus of real V8 JSON-parse errors for the snippet-trim regex). The killing tests pin exact vocabulary the previous suites matched only loosely: the logger's full sensitive-key list through spellings only the deep sanitizer reaches, blank marker secrets leaving whitespace verbatim, fixed-clock finiteness messages naming each seam, ISO-designator end-anchoring, environment coercion staying type-honest (`12ab`/`8080` digit strings), pointer-escape (`~1`/`~0`) rendering with null-guard totality in error formatting, per-union allowed-value lists, `jsonEquals` type discipline against malformed configurations, permission refusals carrying their errno `cause` and the exact mkdir level sequence, and a complete `[REDACTED]` landing exactly at the truncation cut kept intact; two self-referential logger assertions now pin literals instead of the mutated export. The two no-coverage mutants deferred from U-009 close with this round: `env.ts`'s `config.accounts ?? []` fallback is removed in favor of a non-null assertion stating the `Validated`-brand invariant (the fallback was unreachable by construction — defaults completion materializes `accounts`), and `load.ts`'s anyOf fallback string is excluded with a comment documenting the graceful-degradation role it keeps under schema growth. Scoped Stryker re-verification on the final tree: every previously surviving or uncovered site in the seven files reads 0 survived / 0 no-coverage — the in-code exclusions flip their mutants to Ignored (env's comparator ×8, load's allowed-value ternary ×4 and both message arms), the killing tests stay lethal (including the pointer-ternary and anyOf-message survivors of the interim reports), and the refactored accounts block passes 8/8 killed; clock, logger, permissions, reload, and sanitize were verified at 0/0 by full-file scoped runs. Provider-adapter and infrastructure survivors (mapping/provider/snapshot, metrics/config schemas, storage connection, and every file with ≤ 9) are deferred to the next triage round.
- Nine of the eleven no-coverage mutants of the 2026-10-30 mutation report (issue #6, run 41105458027) are resolved (U-009); the remaining two are deferred to the parallel U-008 round with the analysis below. `src/cli/output.ts` — the production CLI sink `systemOutput`, `runCli`'s default whenever no `deps.output` is injected — read 0% because every in-process dispatch test injects a recorder, and Stryker's per-test analysis can neither attribute coverage from, nor activate mutants in, the jiti-loaded child process that `test/integration/cli-bin.spec.ts` spawns; the new `test/unit/cli-output.spec.ts` executes the sink in-process (spying on `process.stdout`/`process.stderr` writes, the server-main pattern), pinning exactly one write per call with exactly one appended newline and multi-line text intact — all six mutants die (scoped Stryker run: 100%, 0 no-coverage). `src/cli/parser.ts` and `src/providers/transport.ts` each carried one no-coverage mutant on a `?? ''` fallback that exists only to satisfy `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` and is unreachable by construction (the `positionals.length > maxPositionals` guard; `String.prototype.split` never returning an empty array); the dead fallbacks are replaced by non-null assertions stating the invariant, so the mutant anchor disappears with the code (scoped runs: parser 0 no-coverage, transport 100%). `src/storage/connection.ts`'s rethrow of a close failure that is not the tolerated `ERR_INVALID_STATE` double-close — previously no test drove any other close error — is now pinned with a proxied real database whose `close()` throws `SQLITE_IOERR`: the original error must reach the shutdown path unwrapped, `isOpen` is already `false` (the documented flip-before-close ordering), and a retry stays a no-op (scoped run: 0 no-coverage). The remaining two no-coverage mutants (`src/config/env.ts:318`, `src/config/load.ts:101`) sit in files in flight by the parallel U-008 round and are deferred to it with the analysis recorded in the U-009 commit: env's `config.accounts ?? []` never observes an absent `accounts` (defaults completion materializes it), and load's `'must be one of the allowed values'` fallback needs an `anyOf` failure without same-path `const` errors while every `Type.Union` in `ConfigSchema` is literal-only — both type-driven, unreachable through the public pipeline today.
- Mutation-report score now follows Stryker's own formula (decision D-055): `detected / scoreable` per file and overall, with ignored and error-status mutants excluded from the denominator but still visible as counts — the issue's score can no longer disagree with the verdict line once deliberate in-code Stryker ignore comments exist (U-006).

### Fixed

- The Vue component tests are type-checked by exactly one project (U-030, spec §17.3, §4.4; decision D-092's type leg completed, no successor decision): `ui/tsconfig.json` — whose `include` names `../test/component/**/*.ts` and which `typecheck:ui`/`typecheck:all` (make check, make check-all, CI's static job) run — **extends the root `tsconfig.json` and so inherits its `exclude: ["test/component"]`** (an extended config resolves the base's path fields against the base config's directory — exactly the directory the UI project's include names), so the 23 component spec files were root files of no project at all: `tsc -p tsconfig.json` skipped them by its own exclude and `vue-tsc -p ui/tsconfig.json` by the inherited one, leaving D-092's "a UI type error fails `make check`" promise blind to the entire component suite. The UI project now overrides the inherited exclude with `"exclude": ["**/node_modules"]` (declaring any `exclude` also replaces TypeScript's default excludes — the pattern restores the `node_modules` default location-independently, and the other defaults have no reachable path: the includes are scoped to `ui/` and `test/component/`, and no `ui/node_modules` exists), making it the single owner by the structure the tree already documented (`test/component/vite-config.spec.ts`'s "ui/tsconfig.json is the single type-checking project for `ui/**` and its specs", D-129's recorded structure). Ownership evidence (`--listFiles`, in the commit body): the server project's program contains 0 files under `test/component/` (348 non-`node_modules` program files total), the UI project's contains all 23. Surfaced and repaired in the same commit — the specs had never been type-checked, and strict mode (with `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `noUnusedLocals`/`noUnusedParameters`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`) found 49 real errors across 9 files: fixture typing that widened every annotated settings field to `string | number` (the `annotated` helper is now generic so each field's literal type survives), `wrapper.find(...).element.value` reads on the broad `VueNode` type (now cast through `HTMLInputElement`/`HTMLSelectElement` at the element the testid names), `setChecked()` — deliberately private in `@vue/test-utils` 2.5's `DOMWrapper` types — replaced by the public `setValue()`, which the library itself routes to `setChecked` for checkbox/radio inputs (verified in the installed source; runtime suites unchanged, 23 files / 580 tests green), discriminated-union access on the chart option/series/datum unions narrowed by their `type` discriminants instead of shape guards, one missing `type VueWrapper` import, unused fake-runtime parameters `_`-prefixed, and three `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` non-null pins in the i18n completeness scan where the regex guarantees the capture group. Scratch-error evidence (not committed): a deliberate `TS2322` inside `test/component/chart-sync.spec.ts` fails `npm run typecheck:ui` naming the file and position while the server `npm run typecheck` stays green over the same tree (exactly one owner, no double-checking ripple), and after the revert `make check` is green end to end. No workflow change: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` already runs `typecheck:all` — its static job now actually covers the specs. The ownership rule and the `extends`-inherits-`exclude` pitfall are recorded in `docs/agents/testing.md` and `docs/agents/gotchas.md`; no new DECISIONS entry (the documented structure did not change — the fix makes it true).

- The `cli-service-logs` backpressure cell is deterministic (U-047, spec §17.1; decision D-167): `test/unit/cli-service-logs.spec.ts`'s "gates forwarding on the sink until it drains (backpressure)" — real children through the production `runJournalctlPassthrough` — tripped three full runs (U-039's rig run 3 at ~5.4 s, U-044's final-value rig run, task 181's landing `make check-all`) while green in every isolation, all three the same mechanism: the child flooded 200 lines in a tight loop and the re-pause proof silently required the OS pipe to deliver at least two reads, but under load the ~1.6 KB flood coalesced into one read — after the first drain there was nothing left to re-pause, and the internal 5 s wait threw ("the re-pause never registered a second drain listener"). A timing assumption on chunk coalescing, which deadline sizing cannot fix — D-163 recorded and deferred exactly this. The cell now drives the child's chunk boundaries by an explicit parent↔child handshake (the U-001/U-002 readiness-marker pattern applied to chunking): the child installs its `SIGUSR2`/`SIGTERM` handlers before writing the `PID:<pid>` announcement (nothing is signalled before it), writes `CHUNK<n>` only when signalled — creating the marker `wrote-<n>` from the write callback, which fires only after the bytes reached the OS pipe, so the marker proves the chunk pending on the reader's side — and on `SIGTERM` writes `FINAL` and exits 0. The gate pins keep their exact strength and gain non-vacuity: exactly the announcement is forwarded while gated; forwarding is held against data KNOWN to sit in the pipe — causally (`resume()` is impossible: the test holds the only drain listener and the child exits only on the test's signal) and over a settle window that causality makes flake-proof (a holding pause ⇒ no event can exist; a pause-less runner must forward inside it, the bytes already pending when the window opens); the first drain forwards exactly the pending chunk and the still-full sink re-pauses, with the drain-listener REPLACEMENT (one at a time) pinned through a tracked set the way the real emitter holds listeners (`once('drain')` adds, `removeListener('drain')` removes — a runner stacking listeners without removing fails the pin); releasing the sink plus the second drain resumes delivery, and the joined forwarded calls reconstruct the whole stream byte-exactly (`PID…CHUNK1…CHUNK2…FINAL` — content, not write counts, since the ungated phase may still coalesce reads; the pipe preserves order, not boundaries). The child's first action is writing a pid file — the leak guard, read by the cleanup to SIGKILL even when the announcement wait expired; after a settled outcome the kill is skipped (the pid may have been recycled), ESRCH is the already-exited answer, and the `once`/`removeListener` spies delegate non-drain events to the real methods. The fixed 5 s child timer is gone: the cell measures 0.12–0.19 s plus the deliberate 200 ms settle window under the six-hog starvation rig versus ~5.3 s before, so the 15 s per-test pin and the 5 s internal failure bounds hold ≈40×/≈25× the measured rig worst. No production code, seam, or assertion elsewhere changed. The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, 2 P2 / 3 P3, all five accepted and folded in) hardened exactly these: the phase-1-timeout child leak (pid-file guard), the single-instant gate observation (settle window), the unpinned replacement claim (tracked set), the unconditional post-success kill and broad catch (settled flag, ESRCH-only), and the swallowed non-drain `once` registrations (delegation). Verified per the U-039/U-044 rig method: 10/10 targeted suite runs under the six-hog rig (cell 0.120–0.172 s pre-review-fix, 0.34 s with the settle window), 5/5 at ambient, and full green `make check` runs under the rig and at ambient at the final tree (4371 tests, 0 failures, coverage thresholds met).

- The 2026-11-04/05 landing-gate load flakes are pinned, closing the D-094 census family's last default-timeout member and its process-orchestration siblings (U-044, spec §17.1; decision D-163, D-094's rule applied with D-087's verdict criterion): `test/integration/cli-doctor.spec.ts`'s "pins the --raw option surface in --help" — the family's only remaining default-timeout spawn test, expired repeatedly at the 5000 ms default under parallel-worktree waves, always green in isolation — now declares `{ timeout: 30_000 }` (the house value every other single-boot launcher suite pins for the same `jiti/register` boot shape; the suite's five doctor-running siblings keep their 60 s pins). `test/integration/single-instance.spec.ts`'s U-017 regression arms timed out twice on marker waits whose 10 s default one child boot could exceed under load — the waits are causal U-001/U-002 file handshakes (verified in the module: `guard-waiting` is the third starter's first and only reachable liveness probe while the lock path is vacant; `saw-stale` is the breaker's pre-guard inspection), so the verdict was sized deadlines, not new synchronization: the suite's `waitForFile` default went 10 s → 20 s and the runner-side `blockUntil` park bound 15 s → 25 s (strictly above the spec-side ceiling — the child parks from its marker write, the parent waits only after observing it), the real-process describe's `CHILD_TIMEOUT` 20 s → 45 s, and the U-017 arms kept their 60 s pins re-margined against measurement. `test/integration/server-main.spec.ts`'s "starts, shuts down cleanly on SIGTERM" (the third site, tripped identically in the same waves — scope-extended per the task's retire-the-class spirit) gets the same treatment: `signals-ready` wait 10 s → 20 s, real-signals `CHILD_TIMEOUT` 20 s → 30 s. Sizing per D-094's rig method (six hogs, full coverage `make check`, pool load ~14–17): marker latency = one runner boot at 1.8–2.2 s isolated and ≈5.5 s worst in-pool (20 s ≈ 3.6×); whole-test rig worsts 3.6–5.8 s for the single-boot cells (30 s ≈ 5×), 8.8–11.1 s for the two-boot real-process tests (45 s ≈ 4×), and 14.2–17.4 s for the U-017 arms (60 s ≈ 3.4×) — every value ≥ D-094's ≈3× bar. The pre-existing `guardTimeoutMs: 20_000` orchestration bounds were verified to cover only post-boot phases (~1–2 s) and stay. No assertion, spawn, environment, or `src/**` file changed — timeouts and docblocks only, measurements recorded beside each constant. Verified: two full `make check` runs under the rig at the final values (4338 tests, the target suites 100% green in both; one run fully green, the other's single failure an unrelated `cli-service-logs.spec.ts` backpressure-chunking flake under the rig, recorded for the main session) plus two full ambient runs fully green. Out-of-scope flakes the same runs exposed — recorded, not fixed here: that `cli-service-logs` cell.

- The runtime-verification script's gate is now proven and wired (U-036, spec §5.1, §17.2; decision D-161): the U-036 review wave found `scripts/verify-runtime.mjs` performing its `node:sqlite` import before any version comparison (so exactly the runtimes the script exists to reject crashed on the import instead of getting the actionable message), carrying no npm script entry unlike its sibling tools, and keeping a local comparator divergent from `src/util/version.ts` on missing segments. Task 129's restructure (commit `2eb8b3c`) had already moved the import behind the gate and rewritten the comparator to the canonical prefix ordering — but nothing pinned either property, and no test exercised the script at all. The gap closes executably: `test/integration/runtime-verify-script.spec.ts` runs the real script as a child process over every case of the shared boundary matrix (`test/helpers/nodeVersionGate.ts`, now consumed by all three gate suites — launcher, canonical comparator, and this script; a plain-JS copy the script keeps because it runs under plain `node`, pinned equivalent instead of shared), faking `process.versions.node` through the data-URL `--import` preload seam. **The gate-before-import ordering is pinned behaviorally, not textually** (the U-032 lesson): a `module.register()` resolve hook that throws for `node:sqlite` simulates the module-less runtime — under it, a faked 22.12.9 still prints `quotameter requires Node.js >= 22.13.0 (found 22.12.9)` with the `make shell` remedy and exits 1 with the hook never firing, while the supported runtime fails on the blocked import (the probe's non-vacuity proof) and a static import would die before any module body runs — verified live before the suite was written. Text pins remain only where execution cannot reach (the resolve hook intercepts neither `createRequire` requires nor `process.getBuiltinModule` lookups — both verified live to bypass it): the source is pinned to carry exactly one `await import('node:sqlite')` occurrence and no static-import spelling and no sidestep API. The broken-manifest fail-closed branch (no package.json at all, unparseable JSON, JSON `null`, no `engines.node`, or a non-`>=X.Y.Z` range → `fix the manifest`, never a TypeError) is covered by fixture packages — as is the derivation the shared matrix cannot test against the real manifest: a raised declaration orders at the patch level (`>=22.13.5` rejects a faked `22.13.4` naming `22.13.5`), a raised minor and a relaxed declaration are followed, and the matrix itself gained a four-segment arm (`22.13.0.1` → invalid, all three gate suites). The script is wired as `npm run verify:runtime` beside `verify:build`, and `make verify-runtime` invokes the npm script (the sibling invocation shape); both wirings are pinned exactly by test. The script's exits now follow the launcher's `process.exitCode` discipline (an `async main()` whose if/else chain keeps the SQLite probe after the last gate arm) — the review round's correction of the first draft's `process.exit(1)` style, whose size-bound justification for immediate exit did not survive scrutiny.
- The CI test jobs are pinned to a systemd-less runner image and the CLI treats an inert systemd as absent (t-ci-systemd orchestrator task, spec §5.1, §5.2, §5.3, §5.5; decision D-160): GitHub's `ubuntu-latest` moved to the 26.04 image between the green 2026-10-29 runs and the red 2026-11-01 runs, and its job sessions run a **live** systemd — there `systemctl --user enable --now` accepted real jobs, `timedatectl` answered, and the real-launcher tests' `quotameter service install` (whose owner home comes from `/etc/passwd`, not the redirectable `HOME`) really wrote `~/.config/systemd/user/quotameter.service` and enabled it. The in-process default-seam `init` tests did exactly that, contaminating every later owner-resolving test (doctor reporting a current user unit, status exiting 1, control exiting 0 — ~15 failures across the three Node matrix legs with no repository change). Two halves fix it. **The environment half**: CI's `test` job and the daily `mutation` workflow run `runs-on: ubuntu-24.04` — the systemd-less image class the suite was designed around (D-103's premise, quoted in ~ ten suite docblocks) — with workflow comments naming the re-work a newer image requires. **The predicate half**: `probeServiceCapabilities` no longer trusts the `/run/systemd/system` marker alone — when it exists, one read-only query per scope verifies the claim (`systemctl is-system-running`, then `systemctl --user is-active default.target` only when the runtime-dir facts hold), under a 10 s race that classifies a wedged bus as not-answering; an answer carrying systemd's own inertness diagnostics (the bus-connect failure in both of its wording generations — ≤256's `Failed to connect to bus` and ≥257's `Failed to connect to {system,user} scope bus via local transport`) or a spawn failure of the binary itself classifies the machine absent, routing chroots, WSL-without-systemd, marker-inheriting containers, and dead-bus machines to the same graceful §5.3/§5.2 fallbacks instead of raw systemctl errors. Exit codes never classify (a degraded system or an inactive target is a live manager answering); `userManager` now implies `systemd`; the probe is async with every `probeCapabilities` seam widened to accept a synchronous fixed answer (existing injections unchanged); a marker-less machine still probes spawn-free. The two init unit suites that never meant to exercise service setup inject the systemd-less answer with forbidden command/write seams (the `forbiddenPrompter` precedent), making them hermetic on any host. Verified: the rewritten probe-classification block in `test/unit/cli-service-setup.spec.ts` (absent/inert/live matrix, both probe commands pinned), the affected unit and real-launcher integration suites green in the systemd-less container, and green `make check`; the final CI proof lands on the pinned jobs.

- The dependency-license gate no longer fails on the project's own package at the first release version bump (U-032, spec §4.3, §4.4; decision D-155): `scripts/check-licenses.mjs` excluded the private (UNLICENSED) repository package from the unapproved-license check by the exact license-checker key `quotameter@0.0.0` — a version-qualified pin that goes stale the moment `package.json`'s version changes, verified live before the fix (a scratch bump to `999.9.9` made the real gate fail with `UNAPPROVED quotameter@999.9.9: UNLICENSED`, the development scope's checked count rising 479→480 as the own package entered the set). The exclusion now matches by NAME, read from `package.json` at runtime, with each checker key reduced to its name part (everything before the last `@`, so scoped keys keep their scope) — version-independence holds by construction (no version input exists) and the exclusion additionally survives a package rename. The approval flow is otherwise unchanged. The policy is pinned by `test/unit/check-licenses.spec.ts`, which extracts and executes the script's own code — the structural pattern, hardened after the task's review round demonstrated that text-only pins are evadable: the real `packageNameOf` is compiled from the source and run over the key table, and the real `SELF` initializer is evaluated with the manifest read stubbed against both the current manifest and a scratch-bumped one, asserting the entry set is exactly the package name in both cases (the done-condition: the self entry stays excluded at `999.9.9-scratch-bump.0` while foreign packages at either version never match); text pins remain only where execution cannot reach (the `SELF` construction's shape, the skip wiring, the whole-script ban on quoted `name@x.y` literals). Every guard is proven lethal live: the pre-fix script fails 6/6, a runtime-interpolated version entry fails three tests, a one-character helper drift fails two, an extra hardcoded entry fails one. Known accepted residual (verified: no package in `package-lock.json` shares the root name today): should a same-named package ever enter the dependency graph, the name-only exclusion would also skip it — distinguishing it from the root requires the version, the exact coupling this change removes.

- The release workflow can now deliver a working publish (U-029, spec §4.4; decisions D-008, D-152): `.github/workflows/release.yml`'s publish job ran on Node 22's bundled npm 10 — incapable of the tokenless OIDC trusted publishing `npm publish --provenance` needs, so D-008's release channel failed at the first tag by construction —, built no web UI before packing (a successful publish would have shipped a tarball without `ui/dist`), and ran no tests before publishing. The job now bumps npm to the shared `11.9.0` pin (the same pin as the Dockerfile and ci.yml, held identical by test), refuses to publish unless the tagged commit is on `master` (a compare against `master` must report the tag at or behind it — a green PR-head run does not qualify) and its `CI` check run completed successfully (the mechanical form of D-008's green-CI precondition: tags pushed mid-CI, onto red commits, onto commits that never ran CI, or off `master` are refused with an explanation), runs the coverage-gated suite (`test:ci`), builds and verifies the web UI _before_ the tarball allowlist check, and only then removes the `private` marker and publishes with provenance; the workflow still carries no npm token of any kind. Publish blockers deliberately remain with task 184 (Stage 14 manifest finalization): `ui/dist` is not yet in the `files` allowlist (and `scripts/check-package.mjs`'s exact-match ALLOWED will need matching for the packed `ui/dist/**` entries — npm lists each built file), and npm refuses `--provenance` without a `repository` field — the build-before-check ordering means the pipeline needs no further change the moment 184 lands (documented in `docs/release.md`, which now describes the pipeline step by step and drops its stale mutation-in-CI mention, D-045). The structural invariants — npm bump presence and placement, the three-file pin identity, the ≥ 11.5.0 floor, build-and-verify-before-check ordering, the test gate, check-before-publish, the master-ancestry + green-CI gate, `--provenance`, `id-token: write`, the `vX.Y.Z` tag trigger, and the absence of any npm token/secret — are pinned by the new `test/unit/release-workflow.spec.ts` (the tz-verify line-scanner pattern, proven lethal on five scratch mutations), so workflow drift fails `make check`. The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, APPROVE with 3 P2 / 5 P3 — the reviewer probed `npm pack` file listing, the live check-runs API, and the jq program) was folded in before landing: the master-ancestry half of the gate, the `verify:build` < `check:package` order pin, the ≥ 11.5.0 (not ≥ 11) pin floor, `checks: read`, the scanner's job bound and blank-line tolerance, and three docs-truthfulness fixes.

- The no-network guard inspects every `fetch` input shape instead of passing objects through uninspected (U-031, spec §17; decision D-154): `test/helpers/network-guard.ts`'s guarded `fetch` computed a hostname only for `string` and `URL` inputs, so `fetch(new Request(...))` — or any other object input — reached `assertAllowedHost(undefined)` and passed straight through to the real network, defeating the §17 no-network guarantee (any test constructing a Request could hit a live provider endpoint; no suite code did yet, so nothing relied on the hole — verified by grep). The guard now extracts the host for `Request` inputs from the spec-guaranteed-absolute `Request#url` (parsed without a base, so a spec-violating relative value is a loud parse failure, never a pass-through) and rejects every other input shape with a distinct "unrecognized input" error — never inspecting a coerced object, because fetch stringifies non-Request objects via `ToString` at the call boundary and any property-based guess would inspect a decoy (`{ url: 'http://localhost/', toString: () => 'https://evil.example/' }` is exactly the bypass duck-typing would open). The `node:http`/`node:https` interception moves from the two global agents' own `createConnection` properties to `Agent.prototype.createConnection` (marker-idempotent, `http.Agent` patched before `https.Agent` so an inherited rather than own https `createConnection` is skipped instead of double-wrapped), covering custom `new Agent()` instances and `agent: false` default agents too. The guard's docblock and a new `docs/agents/gotchas.md` section state the honest scope: a `createConnection` passed as a request/agent option, direct `node:net`/`node:tls` sockets, direct `undici` module imports, `node:dns`, and fetch redirect following (undici follows a `3xx Location` after the initial input is inspected — test fetches keep `redirect: 'error'`, as the provider transport does) stay outside the guard — documented as out of scope rather than closed, since §17 needs a loud tripwire for accidental network use by test code, not a process-wide socket sandbox. The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, APPROVE with 1 P2 / 3 P3, all reproduced and folded in) additionally aligned the instance reads with what fetch itself reads — `new URL(String(input))` for `URL` (ToString is fetch's own boundary conversion) and the `Request.prototype` `url` getter (internal-slot-backed) for `Request`, so shadowed accessors cannot decoy the host — and moved the suite's blocked-host pins from a live public IP to TEST-NET `192.0.2.1`, so a guard regression fails an assertion instead of causing real egress. Pinned by the extended `test/unit/network-guard.spec.ts` (15 tests): a Request-object fetch to an external host is refused, a Request to loopback still reaches the network layer (`ECONNREFUSED`, the guard not the failure), coercible objects and numbers are refused as unrecognized shapes, tampered `hostname`/`url` accessors are refused by reading what fetch reads, external `node:http` and `node:https` requests through fresh custom agents and `agent: false` are blocked, loopback through custom http/https agents still connects, and a second `installNetworkGuard()` changes no hook — beside the four pre-existing pins, unchanged. Full suite green under `make check`.
- The `Retry-After` gate now applies to suspended-account verification probes (U-028, spec §7.2; decisions D-062, D-064, D-066): `src/services/polling-scheduler.ts`'s dispatch loop computed the D-062 `notBeforeEpochMs` gate before every normal and manual dispatch, but the suspended branch admitted and dispatched its hourly verification probe straight after `admitVerification` without consulting the gate — while the module's own field doc promised "dispatch never starts before it" and D-066 (3) pins "the deferral gate applies to every dispatch" ("do not poll before that time" is unconditional). A rate-limited verification probe makes the interleaving reachable: its `Retry-After` (up to one hour) is computed from the probe's **settle** wall reading while the admission re-arm uses the earlier **admission** reading, so a probe at the one-hour cap with any nonzero duration leaves the gate shut past the next cadence instant — and the account was probed before its deadline. The suspended branch now applies the same wall-delta gate the manual-test branch applies, checked after the D-064 cadence predicate but **before** `admitVerification` (admission re-arms the armed instant, so a shut gate waits without consuming the window); a gate that is already open at admission — the common case, since a 401/403 outcome resets the gate — leaves behavior unchanged. Verified: `test/unit/polling-scheduler.spec.ts` gains the fake-clock regression interleaving (429 deferral from a held probe settling past its admission → cadence due inside the deferral window → no dispatch until the gate opens, then the probe, release, and the resumed plain interval), the over-block guard (a short deferral that opens before the cadence does not delay the next probe), the exact-instant boundary pin (a zero-duration probe with the maximum `Retry-After` lands the gate exactly on the cadence instant and admits then, not a wake tick later), and the no-spurious-wake pin (a counting sleep seam proves the settle pass waits straight to the cadence instant — no wake fires at a deferral opening that precedes it, the property the gate's after-the-cadence placement exists for); both the regression pin and the wake pin are proven lethal by mutation — removing the scheduler fix fails exactly the regression test, hoisting the gate above the cadence predicate fails exactly the wake pin — and the scheduler suite stays green (41 tests before this change, 45 with the four new pins) alongside the full unit suite. The review round (deepseek-v4.1-flash, one round, APPROVE with 1 P3) was folded in before landing: the P3 — the placement comment's no-spurious-wake claim was asserted but not pinned — is what the fourth test above makes executable. No decision entry: D-066's composition text already pins this; the fix makes the code truthful to it.
- The real-launcher `start/stop/restart` and `service install/uninstall` suites no longer race Vitest's default 5 s per-test timeout under parallel-worktree load (U-039, spec §17.1; decision D-094's rule applied to the last real-launcher suites without per-test pins): every test in `test/integration/cli-service-control.spec.ts` and `test/integration/cli-service-install.spec.ts` spawns the `quotameter` binary through the real launcher (`jiti/register` transpiles the CLI module graph in the child), and the 2026-11-02 waves (tasks 154/158/166) pushed that boot past the default three times — always green in isolation. All eight tests now declare `{ timeout: 30_000 }` (the installed Vitest 5 options-object form), the house value the 141/142/143 real-launcher suites (`cli-service-reload`/`-status`/`-logs`) already pin for the same boot shape, sized per D-094 from full `make check` runs under the six-hog starvation rig (the U-010/U-012 method): 1.2–2.4 s at ambient sibling load (4.6 s for the cold jiti boot right after a fresh install), full-rig worst 5.1–5.9 s across load 4–21, and 13.2–13.4 s — still green — in the one sizing run where a sibling worktree's burst stacked on the hogs (load 21–38); that stacked-burst sample is tail evidence, not the sizing basis — the hogs are the instrument calibrating to the observed ambient waves (~18), so a burst on top models a world harsher than any observed wave — and 30 s holds ≈5× the regime worst (D-094's bar is ≈3×) while staying green through the burst. No assertion, spawn, or environment changed — signatures and docblocks only, the measurements recorded beside each constant. Verified: five full `make check` runs under the rig (the last two at the final value; the two suites 8/8 green in all five, worst 13.4 s) plus three ambient full runs (all fully green, 191 files / 3811 tests). Out-of-scope flakes the same runs exposed — recorded, not fixed here: `cli-doctor.spec.ts`'s `--help` spawn test expired at the 5 s default twice (both under the rig; the family's only remaining default-timeout test — the suite's other five tests pin 60 s), the same burst exceeded `cli-init-yes.spec.ts`'s 20 s per-test pin, and `cli-init-service.spec.ts`'s 15 s PTY readiness-marker deadlines tripped inside its 40 s per-test pins.
- The DST property suite's pinned timeouts follow D-094's sizing rule (U-025, spec §17.1; decisions D-079, D-094): task 115 pinned `test/unit/peak-hours-dst.property.spec.ts`'s compute-bound tests at 15/30 s before the rule existed, and U-020's rig had measured the every-minute scan-oracle property at 17.2 s worst against its 30 s — adequate for every observed wave but a 1.75× margin, below D-094's ≈3×-full-rig-worst rule. Fresh measurements on the pinned container (full `make check` runs under the six-hog starvation rig, the U-010/U-020 method, host load 15–16 with the ambient sibling wave): the scan-oracle property completed at 16.8/19.3 s worst (7.6 s clean isolated), and the same runs showed the exhaustive minute block — the suite's other pinned family, re-measured per the task's method — at 7.1 s worst per test (1.4 s clean; the first-executed test also pays the coverage warm-up), so the pins held margins of only 1.55× and 2.11×. Both families are re-sized per the rule: `60_000` for the scan-oracle property (3.1× its measured worst) and `25_000` for the exhaustive sweeps (3.5×), recorded beside each test in its docblock. The gate-budget call: a timeout is a ceiling, not a duration — the green-path `make check` wall time is unchanged (~2m50 s under the rig), so only a genuinely stuck run's detection latency grows, bounded and rare; no cap was needed and the 3× rule stands. The suite's third property (fixed-offset zones, 0.03–0.11 s under the rig) stays on the 5 s default, far below the D-094 census line. No assertion, `numRuns`, generator, or seed changed — timeout values and docblocks only; `docs/agents/gotchas.md`'s recorded 15/30 s mention is updated to the re-margined values. Verified: two sizing runs plus two further full `make check` runs under the rig at the final values and five ambient runs (sibling-wave load ~12 without hogs, where the property still took up to ~20 s — above one rig sample, as did one exhaustive test at ~8 s — confirming the sizing) — all green, 106 files / 2090 tests each.
- The transport suite's expiry cases meet the same 10× delay margin as every other real-socket suite (U-026, spec §7.2, §17.1; D-087's stated rule applied to the shapes U-021's review P3 flagged and that task declined only on file-set grounds): `test/integration/transport.spec.ts` — real-socket coverage since task 104 — kept three legacy expiry shapes whose server delays sat at ~6.7× their short deadlines (60 ms against a 400 ms response delay in the headers case, 120 ms against a body completing at ~800 ms in the slow-body trickle, and the retry cycle's cut attempt repeating 60 vs 400 ms), below the ≥10× margin D-087 asks of new or reworked expiry cases. The deadlines stay short — that is the D-087 point — and the delays now ride named constants in the U-021 `EXPIRY_TIMEOUT_MS` style (`EXPIRY_TIMEOUT_MS = 60` for the headers case and the retry cut, `BODY_EXPIRY_TIMEOUT_MS = 120` for the slow-body read, each delay written `10 ×` its deadline) under a D-087 docblock: every delay is arrival-anchored — the mock arms `delayMs` inside `req.on('end')` and the trickle server's handler starts at request arrival, strictly after the deadline starts inside `providerFetch` (D-042) — so parallel load can only move the response further past the deadline, never before it. Every assertion is preserved verbatim (wiring-only), including the headers case's `< 350 ms` elapsed bound, which measures deadline-fire lateness, not the server delay, and only widens its margin; the remaining 28 tests are untouched — delivered-response assertions already at generous deadlines (the 5 s house value; the custom-cap boundary case passes none and runs at the transport's 15 s default), the caller-abort case at 10 s, and phase-choreography timers that race no deadline. `docs/agents/gotchas.md`'s legacy-shape carve-out is closed: every real-socket expiry case in the tree now meets the convention. Verified with the U-012 matrix method: 10/10 suite runs (31/31 tests each) under the six-hog starvation rig (one pinned busy-loop per core 0–5 of the 8-core container), and full `make check` runs green both under the rig (106 files / 2090 tests, 94 s) and at ambient parallel-wave load (61.9 s); suite wall time unchanged (3.88–3.92 s per isolated run vs 4.16 s pre-change), and all hogs were killed and verified dead afterwards.
- The snapshot suite's delivered-response tests no longer share a runner-speed-coupled deadline (U-021, spec §7.2, §17.1; decision D-087's rule applied to the last instance U-012 flagged): `test/integration/zai-coding-plan-snapshot.spec.ts` wired one `TIMEOUT_MS = 2_000` into every provider it constructed — §7.2's deadline is one total bound that starts inside `providerFetch` before dispatch (D-042), so every assertion awaiting an arrived outcome silently required the runner to finish the whole exchange within 2 s of runner time under v8 coverage plus parallel load. The margin had stayed empirically stable (U-010's starvation matrix included), but it was the same latent class as the validate suite's observed 120 ms flake. All 40 delivered-response/evidence-arrival tests — the healthy cycle, the 18 fixture-matrix rows (resolves and parse-rejection alike: both classify arrived 200 bodies), the skip-report and §7.4 hand-off cases, the 500/401/wrong-content-type/over-cap/429 transport cases, the refused-connection and destroyed-socket cases, the fail-soft describe, the silent-logger case, and the two caller-abort tests (a fired deadline would win D-072's precedence over their ~50 ms abort) — now run at `DELIVERED_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000`, the transport suite's value for the same loopback round-trip since task 104 and §7.2's minimum configured timeout. The suite's one deadline-expiry case keeps its short 120 ms deadline, and its mock delay — verified arrival-anchored (the mock arms `delayMs` inside `req.on('end')`, strictly after the deadline starts) — rises from 5× (600 ms) to D-087's ≥10× margin (1_200 ms), making the expiry load-uninvertible. Every assertion is preserved verbatim — a wiring-only change; U-010's arrival gating on the soft-endpoint abort test is untouched. Verified with the U-012 matrix method: 10/10 suite runs (41/41 tests each) under the six-hog starvation rig at host load 10–16, and 3/3 full `make check` runs (two under the rig, one ambient) — all green. The standing rule for future suites is recorded in `docs/agents/gotchas.md`.
- Compute-bound property tests no longer flake against Vitest's default 5 s per-test timeout under parallel-wave load (U-020, spec §17.1; decision D-094): task 124's history properties legitimately do more work than 5 s allows when the runner is starved — ~100 fast-check runs over `size: 'max'` series (220–400 points) with brute-force oracle walks each — and one such test failed at ambient parallel-worktree load ~18 in U-012's matrix. Measured on the pinned container (clean isolated → v8-coverage → six-hog starvation rig, the U-010 method): the downsample cluster property 0.95 s → 1.4 s → 6.8 s worst in a full coverage run at host load 11–17 (the cross detector property 0.46 → 0.77 → 5.9 s; downsample preservation 0.65 → 0.98 → 5.5 s; downsample budget-scale 0.43 → 1.11 → 2.4 s) — the rig reproduces and exceeds the observed ambient flake. Those four tests now declare `{ timeout: 20_000 }` (the installed Vitest 5.0.5 options-object form), ≈3× the measured worst case — the peak-hours-dst house margin (task 115 already pins its own compute-bound properties at 15/30 s) — so a doubling of the worst observed wave still stays green while a genuinely stuck run fails in 20 s. The scan of the other property suites under the same rig found nothing else at risk (events-detection's heaviest 0.8 s, history-gaps 0.37 s, history-timeline 0.62 s, rolling-ranges 18 ms — all left on the default), and no assertion, `numRuns`, or generator was touched. Verified: 10/10 runs of the two suites under the rig (worst 2.4 s), 4/4 full `make check` runs under the rig (load 11–17), 2/2 full runs at ambient load — all green; the standing rule is recorded in `docs/agents/gotchas.md`.
- A failed startup now attempts the release of what it leaked before returning its failure code (U-022, spec §5.6, §17.1; decision D-095): `runServer`'s startup-failure branch printed the startup error and returned `1` without calling `app.stop()` — so a component whose rollback stop had rejected (exactly the leak U-018/D-088 made retryable from the app's `'failed'` state) never received its retry from the only caller that knows startup failed, and a still-leaked handle could keep the process alive after the failure code had been returned (unobservable today because `buildComponents()` registers no handle-owning component; it becomes real with the first storage/listener component, §5.6). The branch now performs a best-effort, **awaited** `app.stop()` before returning: after a clean rollback it is D-088's documented no-op (nothing extra logged), and a rejecting retry is reported through the existing fatal-diagnostics seam (`one or more components failed to stop: …` — the shutdown vocabulary, message-only, no stack-spam) and swallowed — the original startup error, reported first, stays the process's diagnosis and the exit code stays `1`. Two additive `server-main` tests pin the branch with a release-marker component whose `stop` rejects on the first `failStops` calls: with one failing stop, the entry point's awaited retry is what releases the marker (exactly two stop calls, the release observed, no stop-failure output — D-088's route proven reachable from `main`); with two, the retry's `AggregateError` is logged and swallowed while `runServer` still resolves to `1`, the startup error printed first, and the component stays held and addressable. The existing 21 server-main tests are unmodified and green.

- The local gates now catch UI type errors before CI does (U-019, spec §4.4, §17.3; decision D-092): `make check` and `make check-all` ran only the server type check (`tsc -p tsconfig.json`), while CI's required `static` job runs `npm run typecheck:all` (server `tsc` plus `vue-tsc` over `ui/tsconfig.json`) — so a UI type error passed both local gates and then failed the required CI job, the one parity hole task 125's test-leg wiring (`make check` → `test-ci`, D-085) left open despite the documented CI parity (D-045, AGENTS.md, gotchas, the README gate table). Both gate recipes now run `typecheck-all` — the same `typecheck:all` command CI runs — at a measured cost of ≈2.2 s wall for the added `vue-tsc` leg on the current `ui/` scaffold (`make typecheck` 10.96 s → `make typecheck-all` 13.16 s, warm deps); the standalone `make typecheck` target remains the fast server-only loop, and the CI workflow is unchanged (it already ran the full check). Demonstrated on a scratch run (not committed): a formatting- and lint-clean `ui/src/__scratch-type-error.ts` carrying only `TS2322` failed `make check` at the `typecheck-all` leg naming the file and position — a state that passed both gates before the change; the file was deleted afterwards and the tree verified clean.

- The single-instance lock can no longer strand a live holder through the stale-break recovery window (U-017, spec §5.6, §17.1; decision D-086, successor to D-017): `src/server/single-instance.ts`'s rename-aside protocol vacated the lock path between the capture and the restore, so a third process could hardlink its own lock into the vacancy — the restoring `fs.link` then failed `EEXIST`, the error was swallowed, and the displaced live holder's lock stayed stranded in the aside file while both processes kept running (the interleaving the 2026-10-31 kimi review reported as P1; the same hole existed on the release path).
